# Catholic Social Teaching · Reference Card

> Open book. This card is meant to be open while you work — including during
> quizzes. Nothing here is something you're expected to recall cold.

Every problem in this course puts two questions to a sentence of the Church's social teaching: **is it a permanent principle, a contingent application, or an empirical premise**, and **what is its level**. The answers fix what the sentence asks of a Catholic. A principle is owed religious submission of mind and will, graded within rung 3; an application that a magisterial document makes is owed serious, respectful consideration, after which a Catholic may judge it otherwise without dissenting; an empirical premise answers to the evidence ([the three assents](#the-three-assents)). Most mistakes move a claim from one box into another, in one of [two symmetrical directions](#the-two-symmetrical-errors): inflating an application into binding teaching, or demoting a principle to a preference. Both are graded strictly, and so are their cousins on the ladder, "an encyclical is infallible" and "an encyclical is the Pope's opinion". The card is the lookup surface for all of it: the ladder as this course uses it, every document the lessons cite from 1864 to 2026 with the level the lessons give it, how to read the citations, the terms, every position, distinction and document the lessons link (each with its level and its principle-or-application sort), the argument skeletons, the thinkers, what the course takes from its siblings, and the traps. Levels come from [`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md), which owns the ladder; where that course and this card differ, that course wins. Where the build's verification corrected the syllabus, this card follows the correction.

## Authority and citations

### Levels of authority

**The ladder grades acts, not topics: find the act that proposed a claim and what it proposed it as; the rung follows, and the rung fixes the assent.** The ladder is [`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md)'s, the grading of rung 3 is [4.3](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-03-religious-submission.md)'s, and reading a genre is [4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)'s. This course uses them on every claim and re-teaches none of them. What it adds is the second axis: whether a sentence is a principle, an application or an empirical premise ([principle and application](#principle-and-application)).

| Rung | What the act proposed it as | Assent owed | In this course |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | divinely revealed: defined, or taught infallibly by the ordinary universal magisterium | faith | that God created all things (Lateran IV; Vatican I, *Dei Filius*); that directly killing the innocent is always gravely wrong (*Evangelium Vitae* 57, a confirmation, not a definition: [`moral-theology` 5.1](../moral-theology/lessons/05-01-the-inviolability-of-innocent-life.md)); that the Decalogue binds Christians (Trent VI can. 19); love of neighbour (Mt 22:39); that the human person is made in God's image ([`creation-grace-last-things` 2.1](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md)); that faith's assent is not necessitated by rational argument (*Dei Filius* ch. 3, can. 5) |
| **2** | definitively to be held | firm acceptance, as firm as rung 1 (the motive differs) | none. No claim in this course is taught at rung 2. Where theologians read a text as definitive (DH 2's core), infallible (GS 80) or *ex cathedra* (*Quanta Cura*), that is not manifest, so the lessons default down |
| **3** | authentic ordinary magisterium, not definitive | religious submission of mind and will, graded by the document's character, the frequency of the teaching and the manner of speaking (*Lumen Gentium* 25) | almost every principle of the corpus: subsidiarity (4.4's own example of the rung), the common good, the universal destination of goods, the just wage, the right of association, solidarity, the option for the poor, the right to migrate |
| **4** | no act: common teaching of the schools | the respect owed a strong theological consensus | Aquinas's articles as his; double effect as a framework; reconciling RN 6's "by nature" with Aquinas's division of goods "devised by human reason" (q.66 a.2 ad 1) through q.57 a.3 |
| **5** | no act: freely disputed among Catholics | none beyond the arguments | what *Justice in the World*'s "constitutive" means; whether FT 120's "secondary" changes RN; development or reversal in DH, and what DH 1's duty requires of a state now ([5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)); whether the possession judgment is principle or a reading of conditions; each Catholic reading's distinctive premise ([6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md)) |

**The seam after rung 3.** Rungs 1 to 3 record what the Magisterium did; rungs 4 and 5 are theologians' notes on how securely a claim follows when no act has spoken. "Only rung 4, so less than an encyclical" is a malformed sentence: no magisterial assent is owed at all, only the respect due to a consensus.

**Grades inside rung 3, in the lessons' words.** Repetition, genre and manner raise or lower the grade, never the rung (LG 25).

- **at least rung 3**: a rung-3 act has spoken and nothing higher is manifest. Indelible dignity (*Dignitas Infinita*, a declaration of the Dicastery for the Doctrine of the Faith approved in ordinary form, so the Dicastery's act; *Magnifica Humanitas* 52–53 raises its grade; [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)); the duty of societies toward the true religion and no coercion into faith (DH 1, 10; [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)); legitimate defence (GS 79 §4; [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)); GS 80's condemnation of indiscriminate destruction of cities, at the heaviest conciliar grade ([5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md)); that wages owed may not be withheld (RN 20; Deut 24:14–15; Jas 5:4; [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)); the Church's competence over the natural law (*Humanae Vitae* 4; [`moral-theology` 1.3](../moral-theology/lessons/01-03-natural-law-in-light-of-revelation.md)); *Quanta Cura* ([5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)).
- **rung 3 at a heavy grade**: QA 117–120 on socialism (QA 121's "solemn authority" is LG 25's manner of speaking, with no defining formula; [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)); Leo XIII's teaching on the state and religion, at its date (*Immortale Dei* 6; *Libertas* 21; [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md)); the common good, "at its heaviest grade" by sixty-five years of repetition ([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md)); the option for the poor ([3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md)); the just wage ([4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)).
- **rung 3**: the default for the principles of an encyclical, of a council's documents, and of an apostolic letter or exhortation in what it teaches. Genre sets a default and the operative sentence decides: *Octogesima Adveniens*, an apostolic letter to a cardinal, "sits in the rung-3 range; the verb decides" ([2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)).
- **rung 3 at a light grade, plus the weight of what it restates**: a dicastery's document approved by the pope in ordinary form stays the dicastery's act. *Donum Veritatis* (1990), *Libertatis Nuntius* (1984), *Libertatis Conscientia* (1986), the 2002 Doctrinal Note, *Antiqua et Nova* (2025). Likewise the doctrine restated in *Erga Migrantes Caritas Christi* (2004, an instruction the pope approved) and *Exsul Familia*'s doctrine (1952; [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)).
- **rung 3 at its lightest, at most**: papal addresses, radio messages, World Day messages and a letter to one episcopate, for what they teach or restate.

**Particular and regional teaching.** A bishops' conference teaches authentically in its territory, never universally; under *Apostolos Suos* 22 (1998) a doctrinal declaration needs unanimity, or two-thirds with Rome's *recognitio*, and even then binds only there. It restates universal principles at their own weight and cannot move a universal level ([bishops' conference teaching](#bishops-conference-teaching); [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)). Regional assemblies of bishops (Medellín 1968, Puebla 1979) are authentic pastoral teaching in their region and no act of the universal magisterium; a pope's letter commending such a document is reception, not promotion ([3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md)).

**Off the ladder.** Three verdicts are not rungs, and the lessons keep them apart:

- **No magisterial level** (or **none of its own**): the [*Compendium*](#compendium-of-the-social-doctrine) (a pontifical council's compilation with no papal approval formula: each sentence keeps its source's weight); [*Justice in the World*](#justice-in-the-world) (a synod's text published with a generic, conditional approval: at most rung 3 at its lightest for what it restates); advisory bodies (the International Theological Commission); the Fathers as witnesses, however weighty; theologians and philosophers; lay movements and proposals (the social Catholics, Belloc, Chesterton); conferences' appeals (Rome, 2016); invented texts. A papal newspaper interview is "not a teaching act of the kind the ladder grades" ([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md)).
- **Law or discipline, no rung**: canons (can. 749 §3); norms (*Apostolos Suos*'s complementary norms; *Exsul Familia*'s, since replaced); penalties (the Holy Office decree of 1949); liturgical law (the [Mass for the Care of Creation](#mass-for-the-care-of-creation), 2025); and practical directives, which bind conduct, not assent to their truth: MM 239's decisions of the hierarchy ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)), *Divini Redemptoris* 58's no-collaboration rule, a 1937 directive later returned to prudence (PT 160; [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)).
- **No doctrinal rung**: a method ([see, judge, act](#see-judge-act)); an [empirical premise](#empirical-premise), even inside a rung-3 document; a figure; a word ("social justice" as vocabulary); a historical aside (MH 29's dating of the name "social doctrine"); an analytic tool (*Dignitas Infinita*'s four senses); a pastoral programme ([the four verbs](#the-four-verbs), no rung beyond the principles beneath them); a juridical act in international law (the Holy See's ratification of the nuclear-weapons ban treaty, 2017).

**The rules the lessons use every time.**

1. **Find the act, not the book you read the sentence in.** The Catechism locates: each paragraph keeps its source's weight and adds none. Where a paragraph footnotes nothing, look for the acts that teach the same thing: PT 25 and 106, EMCC 29 and MH 81 behind CCC 2241 ([5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)), *Libertatis Conscientia* 79 nearest CCC 2243 ([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md)). Where none can be found, as for CCC 2241's natural-law duty of hospitality, the act is the Catechism's own promulgation (*Fidei Depositum*, 1992): **rung 3**, nothing higher manifest ([5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)). A compendium, a Father's sermon, a famous tag and a Summa article are not acts.
2. **The rung belongs to the document; religious submission attaches to its principles.** An encyclical's applications and empirical premises do not inherit its rung ([the three assents](#the-three-assents)).
3. **Default down.** Nothing is understood as defined unless that is manifest (can. 749 §3). "Some theologians read it as definitive" or "some hold it infallible" leaves the claim at the floor the act manifestly reaches.
4. **Name the higher truth a principle restates, and do not pass its rung on.** That innocent life may never be directly taken is rung 1 (EV 57); that theft is wrong guards the Decalogue. Solidarity rests on the command to love one's neighbour (rung 1), but SRS 38's social definition of it is rung 3 ([3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md)).
5. **Repetition raises the grade, not the rung.** *Magnifica Humanitas* restating *Dignitas Infinita* ([3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)), *Centesimus Annus* 30 restating RN 6 ([2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)), QA calling subsidiarity unchangeable ([2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)): heavier, still rung 3.

**Principle or application:** a method of grading, not a teaching. The rules it applies are magisterial (LG 25's signs) and canonical (can. 749 §3).

- **Trap:** "an encyclical is infallible" / "an encyclical is just the Pope's opinion" (an encyclical is rung 3 by genre, and no social encyclical defines anything).
- **Trap:** "never defined, so not binding" (rung 3 binds as religious submission) / "taught for a century, so definitive" (frequency raises the grade only).
- **Trap:** "the Catechism (or the *Compendium*) says so, so it binds at that weight" (it locates; find the act).
- **Trap:** "the Pope teaches it" of a dicastery note approved in ordinary form ([4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)), or "only a dicastery's opinion" of an approved declaration ([3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)): rung 3 at its grade, the dicastery's act.
- **Trap:** promoting a conference letter to universal teaching, or dismissing it as binding no one ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).

### The three assents

**Three kinds of claim, three different things owed: religious submission of mind and will to a permanent principle; serious, respectful consideration to a contingent application that a magisterial document makes; to an empirical premise, whatever the evidence supports.**

The lessons write it in this order ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)). A document's rung belongs to the document. Religious submission attaches to its **permanent principles**, graded by genre, frequency and manner (LG 25). Its **contingent applications** (figures, institutions, policies, readings of present conditions) ask **serious, respectful consideration**. After that consideration a Catholic may judge an application differently without dissenting. He may not deny the principle, treat the application as weightless, or claim the Church's authority for his own application. **Empirical premises** answer to the evidence.

| Kind of claim | What is owed | The texts (paraphrased) |
|---|---|---|
| Permanent principle | religious submission of mind and will (LG 25; *Donum Veritatis* 23) | *Compendium* 80: social doctrine, as part of moral teaching, shares that teaching's dignity and authority and is authentic magisterium; the weight of each teaching and the assent it asks depend on its nature, its independence from contingent and variable elements, and how often it is invoked. (Its grading sentence is footnoted to DV 16–17 and 23, its authority sentence to CCC 2037.) Some principles restate a truth taught higher, and the lessons name that act: innocent life never directly taken, rung 1 (EV 57) |
| Contingent application in a magisterial document | serious, respectful consideration | *Donum Veritatis* 24 (CDF, 1990): some interventions mix solid principles with contingent and conjectural elements, and often only time sorts the necessary from the contingent; in matters not of themselves irreformable, a loyal willingness to submit remains the rule; prudential interventions may have deficiencies, yet it would be false to conclude that the Magisterium is habitually mistaken in its prudential judgments. *Gaudium et Spes* 43: the faithful may, with equal sincerity, disagree about a concrete solution, and no one may claim the Church's authority for his own opinion. *Evangelii Gaudium* 182 (2013): teachings on contingent situations are open to discussion, yet the Church must be concrete, or the great principles stay generalities |
| Empirical premise | the evidence's verdict; **no doctrinal rung** | *Laudato Si'* 188: the Church does not presume to settle scientific questions or to replace politics. LS 61: on many concrete questions she has no reason to offer a definitive opinion. *Centesimus Annus* 3: an encyclical's analysis of events passes no definitive judgment, since that does not fall per se within the Magisterium's domain |

- **Where they sit in the method** ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)): empirical premises at SEE, principles at JUDGE, applications at ACT ([see, judge, act](#see-judge-act)).
- **Disagreeing is not dissenting.** Conscience judges acts, not doctrines ([`moral-theology` 2.5](../moral-theology/lessons/02-05-the-primacy-of-conscience-rightly-understood.md); DV 38), so voting otherwise after weighing an application is the practical judgment GS 43 gives the lay Catholic ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **Conferences mark their own levels, each in its own words.** *The Challenge of Peace* (US bishops, 1983) §10: its applications (such as "no first use") are "not binding in conscience" but owed "serious attention and consideration". That wording is CoP's alone. *Economic Justice for All* (1986) §135: its specific judgments do not carry the same moral authority as its statements of universal moral principle and formal Church teaching, and deserve serious consideration. *The Harvest of Justice Is Sown in Peace* (1993) separates universal principles that bind from judgments about which Catholics may disagree ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md)).
- **Whose applications are owed the second assent.** Those a magisterial document makes. A civil body's prudential judgment (a town's by-law, a group's own request, where public order falls in a given case, which CCC 2109 leaves to political prudence and the civil authority) has **no magisterial level**: weigh it on the evidence, and disagree freely ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)).
- **The edge.** Not every decision is an application offered for weighing. MM 239: where the hierarchy has decided a point, Catholics must follow its directives. 1.3 reads such a directive as binding conduct as discipline, not assent to its truth; and GS 43 forbids anyone to pass off his own application as such a decision ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md); [prudential judgment](#prudential-judgment)).
- **Level.** LG 25: a dogmatic constitution's rule for grading. *Donum Veritatis* (CDF doctrinal instruction, 24 May 1990, approved in ordinary form): **rung 3 at a light grade**, plus the weight of LG 25 it applies. GS 43 (in the doctrinal first part) and EG 182: **rung 3**. *Compendium* 80: **no level of its own**; its rule applies LG 25's signs (**rung 3**). LS 61, 188 and CA 3: **rung-3** principles about applications.

**Principle or application:** principles about the corpus itself, owed religious submission.

- **Trap:** "prudential means optional": demotion by a side door. CoP §10 and EJA §135 ask consideration and DV 24 makes loyalty the rule; you may end by disagreeing, not skip the weighing ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **Trap:** reading GS 43 as a licence for any disagreement. Its freedom covers concrete solutions, not principles, and the same paragraph binds the person quoting it ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **Trap:** giving "not binding in conscience" to both US letters. It is CoP §10's wording; EJA §135 says its judgments lack the moral authority of universal principles.
- **Trap:** quoting DV 24 for its deficiency half alone. The same section makes loyal willingness the rule and denies that the Magisterium is habitually mistaken in its prudential judgments.
- **Trap:** owing a by-law or a parish group's proposal the consideration due to a magisterial application (it has no magisterial level), or waving a magisterial application away unweighed (which is not what the documents ask).

### Principle and application

**The course's central distinction: a permanent principle holds whatever the facts; a contingent application joins a principle to empirical premises in a prudential judgment about here and now.** 1.2's picture: "heal, never harm" binds every physician; the dose depends on the evidence and on a judgment at the bedside; a doctor who prescribes another dose has not rejected medicine, and one who calls healing "one view of medicine" has.

- **The documents draw the line themselves.** *Centesimus Annus* 3 (1991): Leo XIII's principles belong to the Church's doctrinal patrimony and engage her teaching authority, while the analysis of events passes no definitive judgment. The first note of *Gaudium et Spes* (1965): Part Two mixes elements of permanent and of transitory value, to be read with changeable circumstances in mind; the note sorts elements, not parts, and Part Two also holds GS 68's principle and GS 80's condemnation ([2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)). *Rerum Novarum* marks its own line (1903 English): wages and hours referred to boards "as circumstances, times, and localities differ so widely", and that recourse only "advisable" (§45); "a general and lasting law" for associations (§57) against their organization by "circumstances of fact and of time" (§56); rest "as a general principle" (§42) ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)). *Mater et Magistra* 132–133 set a principle and its application side by side ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)). QA 65 ties partnership to present conditions and to what is possible; LE 14 commends its ownership proposals whether or not each can be applied ([4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)). PP 24's "sometimes" and GS 71's "according to the different cases" keep expropriation and land reform applications ([3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)).
- **The test** ([the standing question](#the-standing-question)), sharpened by *Compendium* 80: **would the sentence still hold if the facts it mentions changed?** If yes, a principle; if not, an application or a premise. Few sentences come marked (CA 3; CoP §§9–10); for the rest, ask ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **What counts as an application:** every figure; every proposed institution (QA's [vocational orders](#vocational-orders); the design of an [international authority](#international-authority) in PT and CiV; a world fund from military budgets); every policy; every reading of present conditions (MH 4's [*res novae*](#res-novae); SRS's 1987 verdict on the blocs; MH 71's platforms as subsidiarity's highest level); every verdict that a bill fits a principle ([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)).
- **Direction, not weight.** An application inherits the principle's direction, never its weight ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)'s ladder of claims).
- **Time does some of the sorting** (DV 24): *Divini Redemptoris* 58's 1937 no-collaboration directive was later returned to prudence (PT 160; [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)).
- **The disclaimers are teaching too** ([the doctrine's disclaimers](#the-doctrines-disclaimers)): no technical solutions, no model (CA 43), no political system (GS 76), no monopoly on reading social reality (EG 184). Refusing to apply at all is its own failure (EG 182).
- **The hard edges, stated flatly** (syllabus Notes): the *principle* of subsidiarity is rung 3; the *proposals* of vocational orders and of an international authority, and every figure in every recommendation, are applications; and how far a given judgment of Francis or Leo XIV on nuclear weapons, war or migration develops principle or reads present conditions is argued among Catholic theologians. The lessons present that argument and do not settle it ([5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)).
- **Level.** The distinction as the documents state it: **rung 3** (CA 3, an encyclical; GS's first note) ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)). How MH 27 (social doctrine not a handbook but shared discernment) and MH 46 (a "core set of unchanging truths") weigh against each other in a hard case: **freely disputed (rung 5)** ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).

**Principle or application:** a principle about the corpus. Every lesson's **Principle or application.** paragraph runs it on its own claims.

- **Trap:** reading OA 4, MH 27 or EG 184, which disclaim a blueprint, as disclaiming principles (demotion; [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **Trap:** taking a fact stated in an encyclical for doctrine. CA 3 denies definitive force to its own reading of events, and CA 12's later confirmation of RN's prognosis leaves it empirical ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)).
- **Trap:** reading a "however" as weakening the principle. RN 45's principle carries no number; the particulars go elsewhere because they vary ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **Trap:** a fused claim. "Dignity grounds a right to a minimum income" joins a principle (the right to the means of a worthy life, PT 11; GS 26) to an instrument (an application); split it ([3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)).
- **Trap:** moving a claim across the line in either direction: see [the two symmetrical errors](#the-two-symmetrical-errors).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md)

### Empirical premise

**A claim about how the world is (what a wage floor does to jobs, who bears a tax, what warms the climate), taken from the sciences or from those who live a situation. It stands or falls with the evidence and has no doctrinal rung, even inside a rung-3 document.**

- **The documents' own account.** CA 3; LS 188 and 61 (MH 23 cites LS 61); CA 59 (the teaching dialogues with the human sciences and takes in what they contribute); GS 36 (created things have their own laws, which each science uncovers by its own methods: the [rightful autonomy of earthly affairs](#rightful-autonomy-of-earthly-affairs)); EJA 134 (a prudential judgment is only as sound as its principles and its information); LN VII.3–5 (scientific knowledge of a situation is presupposed, but a borrowed method of analysis needs a prior critique) ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).
- **The rule.** A principle applied to a false empirical premise yields a mistaken application, not a false principle. New evidence can revise an application; it cannot revise that work is for the person ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).
- **Keep the document's hedge**: it is the mark that a sentence is a premise ("it would appear", "experience shows", "a number of studies indicate", "can", "often").

| Text | The premise (paraphrased) | Lessons |
|---|---|---|
| RN 3, 15, 39, 47 | the guilds gone and nothing in their place; community of goods would dry up the sources of wealth (CA 12 later judged RN's picture and prognosis accurate: still empirical); the causes of strikes; that no one would leave his country if it gave him the means of a decent life (a prediction, not a teaching about migration) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) |
| QA 74; MM 59, 124; PT 134–135 | wages and unemployment; the description of the age; the causes of rural flight; states unequal to world problems | [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md) |
| OA 8–9, 44 | urbanization; multinational enterprises beyond national control | [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md) |
| PP 57, 76 | the terms of trade (raw materials swing in price and miss the rising value of manufactures); that disparity endangers peace | [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) |
| LE 1, 17 | the automation forecast; the account of prices | [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md) |
| SRS 15 | "experience shows" that denying the right of economic initiative destroys initiative | [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md) |
| CA 24, 33, 34, 48 | 1989's economic failure as a consequence of violated rights; the record of economies closed to world trade; "it would appear" that the free market is the most efficient instrument, for solvent needs and marketable resources; "it would appear" that needs are best met by those closest to them | [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) |
| DH 15 | most constitutions already declare the right | [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md) |
| CiV 46, 58 | a plurality of forms makes the market more civilized and more competitive; badly given aid breeds dependence | [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md) |
| EG 54 | trickle-down theories never confirmed by the facts | [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md) |
| LS 23; *Laudate Deum* 5–19 | a very solid scientific consensus that the climate system is warming; in a separate, hedged sentence, that a number of studies indicate most recent warming is due mainly to human activity (LD 11 calls the human origin beyond doubt) | [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md) |
| FT 168, 262 | that trickle-down theories go unconfirmed; doubts that deterrence answers today's threats | [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| MH 5, 95, 98, 101, 108, 150, 151, 158, 161 | innovation driven by private, often transnational actors richer than many governments; concentrated digital power; statements about AI may soon date ([the MH 98 caveat](#the-mh-98-caveat)); AI's energy and water use; AI "tends" to strengthen whoever has money, know-how and data; current approaches "can" deskill and surveil workers and frequently force them to the machine's pace (quoting *Antiqua et Nova* 67); job contraction; theories promising automatic prosperity often prove illusory; concentration of wealth | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) |

- **Level.** **No doctrinal rung.** The principle that applications rest on such premises and are revisable with them is **rung 3** (CA 3; GS 36; LS 61, 188) ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).

**Principle or application:** neither: the third kind of claim, entering at SEE ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).

- **Trap:** "a fact stated in an encyclical becomes doctrine" (inflation) / "the evidence is contested, so the principle is open" (demotion: evidence revises applications, not principles) ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).
- **Trap:** "*Laudato Si'* defined the climate science, so doubting a projection is dissent" / "creation, stewardship and the poor's water are the Pope's opinion on science" ([6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)).
- **Trap:** merging LS 23's two sentences into "a very solid consensus that warming is human-caused" ([6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)).
- **Trap:** dropping a hedge (MH 150's "can", CA 48's "it would appear"), or attaching "trickle-down" to MH 158, which does not use the word ([4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)).

### Prudential judgment

**The judgment, by those competent, that applies a principle to a concrete case through empirical premises. It is revisable and open to sincere disagreement among Catholics, and when a magisterial document makes one, it is owed serious, respectful consideration.**

- **The texts.** DV 24, GS 43 and EG 182 ([the three assents](#the-three-assents)). Leo XIV, address of 17 May 2025: "doctrine" in social doctrine means a disciplined body of knowledge, not an opinion, and it helps form prudential judgments (paraphrased) ([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).
- **Who judges.** Past the principle, the verdict belongs to lay Catholics, legislators, local communities and all responsible for the common good (GS 43; OA 4; DCE 29; MM 238, which allows sincere Catholics to differ on application). Bishops teach the principles; a conference's verdict on a bill is an application ([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)). The Catechism assigns the judgment whether a given war meets the conditions to those responsible for the common good (CCC 2309; [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)), and where public order falls in a given case to political prudence and the civil authority (CCC 2109; [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)).
- **A directive is not an application to weigh and set aside.** MM 239: where the hierarchy has decided a point, Catholics must follow its directives. PT 160 leaves the timing and manner of cooperation with movements to prudence, chiefly of those who lead in the field, acting by natural law, the social teaching and the directives of Church authority, and says the Church may judge the practical application of her teaching. Such a directive binds conduct as discipline, not assent to its truth, and can lapse, as *Divini Redemptoris* 58's ban did ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)).
- **Civil prudence has no magisterial level.** A legislature's bill, a town's by-law or a group's own request is weighed on the evidence and argued freely ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).
- **Level.** DV 24: **rung 3 at a light grade** ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)). GS 43 and EG 182: **rung 3**. MM 238–239 and PT 160: **rung 3** as claims about application; a directive issued under them: **discipline, no rung** ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)). The 2025 address: **rung 3 at its lightest**, at most. The judgment itself has no rung of its own.

**Principle or application:** the judgment is an application; the rules that govern it are principles.

- **Trap:** "prudential means optional" (demotion; [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **Trap:** "the Church teaches that this bill must pass" or "violates subsidiarity": the verdict on a bill has no rung, and no one may claim the Church's authority for his own solution (inflation; GS 43; [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)).
- **Trap:** "MM teaches that every application is free": read §239 ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)).
- **Trap:** reading CCC 2309's last sentence as putting rulers beyond criticism, or a bishop's verdict on a war as binding: it assigns competence only ([5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)).

### The two symmetrical errors

**Inflation presents an application as a binding principle ("the Church teaches this figure"); demotion reduces a principle to a preference ("the living wage is one opinion"). Both are graded strictly, and each has a cousin on the ladder: "an encyclical is infallible" and "an encyclical is the Pope's opinion".** Every lesson's Watch out names at least one. The forms the lessons meet, paraphrased:

| Topic | Inflation | Demotion | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|
| the corpus | "the Church's model is X"; a "third way" with its own blueprint | "no model, so nothing to say about economics"; "the Vatican's politics" | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md) |
| the *Compendium* | "the *Compendium* says so, so it binds" | "only a summary, so it weighs nothing" (it carries its sources' weight) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md) |
| applications | "no first use is Catholic teaching"; "the Church teaches this figure" | "prudential means optional"; reading OA 4 or MH 27 as disclaiming principles | [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md) |
| the method | "the method makes sociology dogma"; "the Church requires this by-law" | "the teaching follows the data" | [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md) |
| the sources | "Acts 4 binds Christians to common ownership" | "Ambrose was preaching, so it binds no one" (the sermon has no rung; the principle PP 23 takes from it is rung 3) | [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md) |
| *Rerum Novarum* | "Leo told Catholics to form Catholic unions" as doctrine (§54's conditional advice) | "so the right to unionize is application too" (§51's natural right) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md) |
| *Quadragesimo Anno* | "QA requires a corporative constitution"; "QA defined that no socialist can be Catholic" | "the proposal's fate sinks the encyclical"; "1931 politics" | [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md) |
| *Pacem in Terris* | a world superstate read into PT 137–141 | the principle dismissed as a period piece | [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md) |
| GS and JW | "the 'constitutive' sentence is papal teaching or a definition" | "pastoral means not binding" | [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md) |
| dignity | "dignity requires housing first" | "dignity is an empty word" | [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md) |
| the common good | "the Church teaches that the common good requires this budget" | "a pious slogan" | [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md) |
| subsidiarity | "the Church teaches this bill violates subsidiarity" | "one opinion about federalism" | [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md) |
| solidarity | "solidarity requires Measure 7" | "a feeling", or "the Pope's word for one side's politics" | [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md) |
| property | one's preferred instrument as doctrine (no vacancy tax or land reform is taught) | the social function as papal opinion (MH 66 calls it doctrine) | [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md) |
| the option | the option choosing a party, a tax or an economic model | "a Latin American slogan Rome merely tolerated" | [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md) |
| work | the priority of labour as a ruling on ownership; the strike unlimited once a union votes | the priority of labour as a slogan; the strike merely tolerated | [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md) |
| the wage | "a living-wage figure is Catholic teaching" | "one opinion about labour markets" | [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md) |
| the two critiques | "EG 54's trickle-down verdict binds as doctrine" | "QA 120 is a 1931 opinion" | [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md) |
| enterprise | CiV's praise of the economy of communion binding owners to adopt it | the gift chapter as pious decoration | [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md) |
| *Magnifica Humanitas* | "MH obliges every firm to match each automation with job guarantees" (§156) | "'someone must answer' is a pious hope for engineers" (§105) | [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md) |
| the state | "democracy is the only legitimate regime" (CA 46's condition read as a mandate) | "CA 46 is one pope's view" | [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md) |
| religious liberty | "DH defined religious liberty" | religious liberty as a truce, the "hypothesis" to drop in a Catholic majority | [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md) |
| war | "the criteria are defined" | "the just-war conditions are Aquinas's opinion" | [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md) |
| deterrence | "since 2017 possession is defined as intrinsically evil" | "John Paul II approved deterrence, so Francis gave his view" | [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| migration | every limit on admission a sin | any limit at all enough; the qualifier (as far as they are able) a matter of taste | [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) |
| ecology | "LS defined the climate science"; a Catholic must back whatever LS 165–173 or LD 59 proposes | creation, stewardship and the poor's water as "the Pope's opinion on science" | [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md) |
| development | a superstate read into CiV 67 | dismissing the need for a world-competent authority | [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) |
| distributism | "RN 46 makes distributism doctrine" | demoting the direction for law that QA 59–63, MM 113 and GS 71 restate | [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md) |
| the readings | a reading's distinctive premise presented as the Church's teaching | "DH binds no one's mind" (refusing DH 2 any assent) | [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) |

- **The cousins on the ladder.** Overstated: "an encyclical is infallible" ([2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)); "MH made indelible dignity definitive" ([3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)); "*Antiqua et Nova* is papal teaching" ([4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)); "LN is infallible" ([3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md)); "Medellín made the option Church teaching" ([3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md)). Understated: "an encyclical is the Pope's opinion"; "a dicastery's declaration is only an opinion" ([3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)); "LN is a dead letter" ([3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md)); "*Quanta Cura* was plainly not *ex cathedra*" ("not manifest" is not "plainly not"; [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)).
- **Do not overcorrect.** The fix for one error is not the other: "QA endorsed Fascism" is wrong, and so is "QA condemned the system" ([2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)); "PT 160 made cooperation with communists a duty" drops its question whether the time has come ([2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)).
- **Level.** Not a teaching but the course's grading rule: overstating a level is an error and so is understating one. Its ground is in the documents: GS 43 (no one may claim the Church's authority for his own opinion), CA 3 and DV 24 (rung 3).

**Principle or application:** a grading rule built on rung-3 principles about the corpus.

- **Trap:** spotting only one direction. The lessons' Watch outs grade both, and so do the problems.

### Levels of authority used in this course

Every document the lessons cite, in date order, with the level the lessons give it. **P** is a principle (religious submission of mind and will), **A** an application (serious, respectful consideration), **E** an empirical premise (no rung, the evidence decides). A document's rung is its own; its sentences then sort into P, A and E, and this table shows the main sorts. Each document's own entry under Positions and distinctions has the detail. Section numbers follow [the vatican.va English](#citations-and-how-to-read-them). Where a level is what a Boss part asks you to produce, the row says so and stops.

**Before 1864** the lessons cite: *Mirari Vos* (Gregory XVI, encyclical, 15 August 1832), rung 3 at its date ([5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)); Lateran IV, *Firmiter* (1215), with Vatican I, for creation, defined, **rung 1** ([3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)); Trent VI can. 19 (1547), that the Decalogue binds Christians, defined ([3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)); Trent VII on baptism, only as a text Pink's reading leans on, and *Exsurge Domine* (1520), named and left to `apologetics-catholic-claims` 7.1 ([5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)). That scripture is God's word is **rung 1**, and a given exegesis has **no level** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)). The Fathers are witnesses with **no magisterial level** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)). Aquinas's articles have **no magisterial level** as his: common teaching (**rung 4**), and **rung 3** where an act restates them ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)).

Principles **rung 3**, conciliar grade: the person and the common good (§§25–26, 74–75), rightful autonomy (§36), the disclaimers and lay freedom (§§42–43, 76), work and the wage (§§67–68), the destination of goods (§69), ownership (§71), migrants (§§65–66). ### Citations and how to read them

**Every citation here is a lookup you can follow: a document by name and section, the Catechism by paragraph, Aquinas by part, question and article, scripture by book, chapter and verse. A document named without a number is better than a document with a wrong one.**

**Abbreviations.** RN *Rerum Novarum* · ID *Immortale Dei* · QC *Quanta Cura* · QA *Quadragesimo Anno* · DR *Divini Redemptoris* · MM *Mater et Magistra* · PT *Pacem in Terris* · LG *Lumen Gentium* · AA *Apostolicam Actuositatem* · GS *Gaudium et Spes* · DH *Dignitatis Humanae* · PP *Populorum Progressio* · OA *Octogesima Adveniens* · JW *Justice in the World* · EN *Evangelii Nuntiandi* · LE *Laborem Exercens* · CoP *The Challenge of Peace* · LN *Libertatis Nuntius* · LC *Libertatis Conscientia* · EJA *Economic Justice for All* · SRS *Sollicitudo Rei Socialis* · DV *Donum Veritatis* · CA *Centesimus Annus* · CCC the Catechism · EV *Evangelium Vitae* · EMCC *Erga Migrantes Caritas Christi* · DCE *Deus Caritas Est* · CiV *Caritas in Veritate* · EG *Evangelii Gaudium* · LS *Laudato Si'* · FT *Fratelli Tutti* · LD *Laudate Deum* · DI *Dignitas Infinita* · AeN *Antiqua et Nova* · DT *Dilexi Te* · MH *Magnifica Humanitas* · ST Aquinas, *Summa Theologiae*.

**Documents: name and section, in the vatican.va English numbering.** Other editions number differently, so convert before you cite.

- MM's English numbers differ from the Italian (English 59 is Italian 45; English 236 is Italian 217).
- RN: vatican.va prints an unnumbered paragraph after §42; cite it as §42.
- LN is cited by chapter and paragraph (VII.3); the vatican.va English has no VI.6.
- Pius XII's Pentecost message of 1941 is unnumbered in its Italian original, so the lessons cite it without numbers.
- A later document's footnote may follow another numbering. MH's note to §69 cites "RN 26": that passage is RN 35 here. MH's "PT 5" is PT 9, and its "PP 17" matches PP 44. CCC 1930's "PT 65" is PT 60–61 here. The "QA I, 184–186" of CCC 1883's note are AAS pages, not sections: the subsidiarity sentence is QA 79.

**The *Rerum Novarum* text.** The lessons quote it only from *The Great Encyclical Letters of Pope Leo XIII* (Benziger, New York, 1903; translator not named; public domain), cited by the vatican.va section numbers. The vatican.va English is a later revision under copyright, never quoted; it differs at §§9, 13, 37, 45 and 46. So §45 reads "remuneration ought to be sufficient to support a frugal and well-behaved wage-earner", and §46 "induce as many as possible of the humbler class to become owners". Two slips in the 1903 transcription: §45's "underlines" is "underlies", and §8's "enjoyroent" is "enjoyment". ([*Rerum Novarum*](#rerum-novarum); [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md).)

**Other public-domain texts.** Leo XIII's *Immortale Dei*, *Libertas*, *Sapientiae Christianae* and *Quod Apostolici Muneris* come from the same 1903 volume. Quote *Immortale Dei* 4 only from it: the vatican.va English drops a clause there ([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md)). *Diuturnum* is paraphrased, since no public-domain English print was available ([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md)). Aquinas is the English Dominican Province translation. The Fathers are Schaff's *Nicene and Post-Nicene Fathers*. Belloc (1912) and Chesterton (1926) are quoted from their own editions.

**Why everything from 1931 on is paraphrased.** Copyright. Every document from *Quadragesimo Anno* onward, the Council, the Catechism, the *Compendium*, the conference letters and every modern theologian are paraphrased and cited by name and section. A term of art of five words or fewer may stand in quotation marks, attributed, as a name ("structures of sin", "social mortgage"), and so may a document's own hedge shown as evidence ("it would appear"). When a lesson could not read a modern book, it says only what is well documented ("on his account") and gives no chapter or page.

**"(lesson's translation)."** Where only a pre-1931 original is public domain, a lesson may print its own translation, at most 150 words, marked as such, with the original where a word decides the reading. It never translates from a copyrighted English version. The course uses this for Ambrose, *De Nabuthe* 12.53 ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)). From later Latin texts the lessons give only a decisive word or phrase with a gloss: QA 87's *doctrina socialis christiana*, MM 236's *aspicere, iudicare, agere*, MM 65's common-good formula, PT 106's *non fucatum*, PT 127's *ad violata iura sarcienda*, JW's *ratio constitutiva*.

**The Catechism: by paragraph (CCC 2309).** It locates; it does not fix a level ([levels of authority](#levels-of-authority)). Its footnote names the act, so read it, and check that there is one: CCC 2241, 2243, 2435 and 2439 carry none. Three traps: CCC 2432's note is CA 37, not CA 35; CCC 2434's notes are Lev 19:13, Deut 24:14–15, Jas 5:4 and GS 67 §2, not LE 19; and the SRS 16 note belongs to CCC 2440, not 2439.

**Aquinas: by part, question, article** (ST II-II q.66 a.7; "ad 1" is his reply to the first objection). His attributions are his own. The right-intention sentence of q.40 a.1 that he assigns to Augustine is, by the English Dominican translators' note, found in Gratian ([5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)).

**Scripture and the Fathers.** Scripture is the Douay–Rheims (Challoner), with modern verse numbers and the Douay number in brackets where it differs. Ambrose is cited by chapter and section (*De Nabuthe* 12.53), because Migne's columns differ between printings. Gregory's *Pastoral Rule* III.21 is Barmby's Chapter XXI, headed "Admonition 22" ([3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)). Augustine's *Contra Faustum* XXII.74 is Stothert's translation in NPNF first series, vol. 4 ([5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)).

**Why there are no Denzinger numbers.** In this course **DH means *Dignitatis Humanae***. Denzinger–Hünermann numbers appear only if checked at a source that prints that numbering, and old Denzinger numbers are not DH numbers. None is used: councils and papal acts are cited by name, session or chapter, and canon (Trent VI can. 19; *Dei Filius* ch. 3, can. 5).

***Justice in the World* has no numbers of its own.** The Latin (AAS 63 (1971) 923–942) is unnumbered, in an introduction and four parts; English editions add numbers 1–77. So "§6" means the English numbering, and the lessons say so. The famous sentence's Latin is *ratio constitutiva*, not the *dimensio constitutiva* one secondary account reports. The rescript is AAS 63, 897 ([*Justice in the World*](#justice-in-the-world); [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)).

**Genres to name exactly.** [*Laudate Deum*](#laudate-deum) is an **apostolic exhortation** (so MH's note 220), not "the second ecological encyclical". *Octogesima Adveniens* is an **apostolic letter**, often miscalled an encyclical. *Evangelii Nuntiandi*, *Evangelii Gaudium* and *Dilexi Te* are **apostolic exhortations** (LN V.5's English calls EN an "Apostolic Constitution": a slip). *Justice in the World* is a **synod's text**, not a papal document. *The Harvest of Justice* is a conference's **reflection**, not a pastoral letter. *Libertatis Nuntius*, *Libertatis Conscientia* and *Donum Veritatis* are the CDF's **doctrinal instructions**; *Dignitas Infinita* a **declaration**; *Antiqua et Nova* a **note**. ["The social encyclicals"](#the-social-encyclicals) is a scholars' convention, and its members are not all encyclicals ([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md)).

**Dates to state exactly.** Signed is not published: MH (15 and 25 May 2026), *Dilexi Te* (4 and 9 October 2025). Approved is not dated: *Dignitas Infinita* (25 March and 2 April 2024), *Antiqua et Nova* (14 and 28 January 2025), EMCC (1 and 3 May 2004). Dated is not delivered: John Paul II's UN message (7 and 11 June 1982). The *Compendium*'s Presentation is dated 2 April 2004.

**Slips in the vatican.va English, to read around.** SRS 41's "technical revolutions" is *technical solutions* in the Latin. *Compendium* 193's first sentence on solidarity garbles the Italian, which calls it a social principle ordering institutions, and then a moral virtue. MH 31 misspells QA as "Quadragesima Anno". Three key terms are the course's, not the English text's: QA's English says "Industries and Professions", not [vocational orders](#vocational-orders); MM's says "increase in social relationships", not [socialization](#socialization); and MM 236's says "look, judge, act".

## Positions and distinctions

One entry per position, distinction, document or figure the lessons link, grouped by the module where it is first cited. Each gives the plain sense first, then the canonical statement (paraphrased unless the text is public domain) with its document and section, the level as the lessons mark it, whether it is principle, application or empirical premise, the traps, and the lessons that use it. Levels come from [`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md) ([levels of authority](#levels-of-authority)); the assent each kind of claim asks is [the three assents](#the-three-assents).

**Module 1: What the Church's social doctrine is**

### Social doctrine of the Church

**The Church's moral teaching about life in society: work, property, the economy, the state, war, migration, creation.** It is the body of papal, conciliar and curial teaching on social questions from [*Rerum Novarum*](#rerum-novarum) (1891) to [*Magnifica Humanitas*](#magnifica-humanitas) (2026). Its development began when the Gospel met industrial society (CCC 2421); its older roots in scripture, the Fathers and Aquinas are [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)'s. The corpus is defined by subject and issuer, not by a canon ([the social encyclicals](#the-social-encyclicals)).

- **What kind of knowledge.** Not an ideology and not a "third way" between liberal capitalism and Marxist collectivism, but a category of its own, belonging to theology and particularly to moral theology, since it aims to guide conduct (*Sollicitudo Rei Socialis* 41, 1987; *Compendium* 72–73). *Centesimus Annus* 5 calls it a genuine doctrine. *Magnifica Humanitas* 27: not a handbook of norms awaiting application but a shared discernment where the Gospel meets history's questions. Leo XIV (address of 17 May 2025): "doctrine" here means a disciplined body of knowledge, not an opinion.
- **The name.** MH 29 credits Pius XII (1950; its note cites *Menti Nostrae*) with the title "Social Doctrine of the Church". The words are older: *Quadragesimo Anno* 87's Latin has *doctrina socialis christiana*. So MH dates the title, not the words. "Social doctrine" and "social teaching" are both in use.
- **Level.** That it is moral theology about social life: **rung 3** (SRS 41, an encyclical). What the corpus contains: description and convention, **no magisterial level**. MH 29's dating of the name is history, not faith or morals: **no rung**. The 2025 address: **rung 3 at its lightest grade**, at most.

**Principle or application:** the self-description is a permanent principle about the corpus itself, owed religious submission of mind and will.

- **Trap:** "social doctrine is just the Vatican's politics" (demotion; SRS 41, GS 76, Leo XIV's "not an opinion").
- **Trap:** "social doctrine is an economic system, a third way with its own model" (inflation; SRS 41, CA 43).
- **Trap:** "'social doctrine' is a 1950 coinage" (MH 29 dates the title; QA 87's Latin has the words).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md)

### Competence in the moral order

**The Church claims to judge what social, economic and political arrangements do to persons, not how to run them.** *Gaudium et Spes* 76 (1965): the Church claims the freedom to teach her social doctrine and to pass moral judgment on public matters when persons' fundamental rights or the salvation of souls require it, using only means that accord with the Gospel (paraphrased); CCC 2420 restates it for economic and social life. *Quadragesimo Anno* 41 (1931) had drawn the same line: no competence in matters of technique, a right and duty wherever the moral law is touched.

- **The ground.** Her charge to interpret the whole moral law, natural law included (*Humanae Vitae* 4; CCC 2036), argued and levelled in [`moral-theology` 1.3](../moral-theology/lessons/01-03-natural-law-in-light-of-revelation.md); cited here, not re-argued.
- **The founding self-claim.** *Rerum Novarum* 16 (Benziger 1903): Leo approaches the question "in the exercise of the rights which manifestly appertain to us", for "no practical solution of this question will be found apart from the intervention of Religion and of the Church". "Apart from" makes the Church necessary to a solution, never sufficient; "others besides ourselves" (rulers, employers, the wealthy, the workers) have real work to do. Competence, a limit and a division of labour, in seed.
- **Level: rung 3** (GS 76; the encyclicals). The competence over the natural law: **at least rung 3**, as `moral-theology` 1.3 fixes it.

**Principle or application:** a permanent principle about the corpus.

- **Trap:** competence is not omnicompetence. GS 76 claims moral judgment *when rights or souls require it*; *Caritas in Veritate* 9 and *Laudato Si'* 188 disclaim technical and scientific settlement ([the disclaimers](#the-doctrines-disclaimers)).
- **Trap:** confusing technical expertise, which the corpus disclaims, with moral competence, which it claims.
- **Trap:** "doctrine concerns faith and morals, so wages and markets are outside it" (SRS 41: social doctrine belongs to moral theology).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)

### The doctrines disclaimers

**What the corpus says it does not do: no technical solutions, no model, no system, no "third way", no party; it does not replace the State or settle science.** The disclaimers are part of the teaching, not modesty about it: the Church disclaims the blueprint, not the judgment. Paraphrased:

| Disclaimer | Texts |
|---|---|
| No technical solutions; no claim to interfere in the politics of states | SRS 41 (1987; the vatican.va English misprints "technical revolutions": read "solutions"); *Caritas in Veritate* 9 (2009), quoting PP 13 for the second half. PP 13 itself does not say "no technical solutions": it says the Church does not wish to be involved in any nation's politics and offers a global perspective on man. |
| No models to present: workable models arise in history from those who face the problems | *Centesimus Annus* 43 (1991) |
| Bound to no particular form of culture, nor to any political, economic or social system | *Gaudium et Spes* 42, 76 (1965) |
| Not a "third way" between liberal capitalism and Marxist collectivism, but a category of its own | SRS 41 |
| No universally valid message is the pope's ambition or mission; communities discern their options | *Octogesima Adveniens* 4 (1971); restated MH 26 |
| Must not take on the political battle for the most just society or replace the State, yet may not stay on the sidelines; claims none of the State's functions | *Deus Caritas Est* 28 (2005); MH 21 (2026) |
| Does not presume to settle scientific questions or replace politics; on many concrete questions gives no definitive opinion | *Laudato Si'* 188, 61 (2015; MH 23 cites LS 61) |
| Not a repertoire of technical solutions or a model, but principles for interpreting events | MH 24 |
| Not a social document; no monopoly on interpreting social reality | *Evangelii Gaudium* 184 (2013), quoting OA 4 |

- **Early forms.** *Rerum Novarum* 32 (the State "as rightly apprehended", no particular form of government); RN 42, 45 and 56 (hours, wages and union forms left to circumstances); QA 41 (no competence in technique) and QA 86 (the vocational bodies' form free).
- **Later instances.** *Laborem Exercens* 1 and 19 (not a treatise on economics; the deontological and moral aspect); LE 14 (whether its ownership proposals apply is left open); CiV 47 (no universally valid solution); MH 97 and 153 (no comprehensive treatment of AI; no single model of change); *Dignitatis Humanae* (a right and a limit, no constitutional model, [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)); no war plan ([5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)); no immigration model and no admission number ([5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)); LS 60 (no one path to a solution, [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)); no aid share or tariff ([6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)); no preference for small firms or Belloc's "no third course" ([6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)).
- **Root.** Augustine's two cities lie beneath the restraint: no earthly order is the Kingdom ([`history-of-political-thought` 2.2](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-02-augustine-the-two-cities.md); 1.4).
- **Level: rung 3.** They recur from 1965 to 2015 across a council and three popes, so frequency raises the grade within rung 3 (*Lumen Gentium* 25). None is a definition.

**Principle or application:** permanent principles about the corpus, owed religious submission; they sit beside a claimed [competence](#competence-in-the-moral-order).

- **Trap:** "no model means the Church has nothing to say about economics" (demotion; the disclaimers are themselves rung-3 teaching beside GS 76's claim).
- **Trap:** "the Church's model is X" (inflation; contradicts CA 43 and SRS 41).
- **Trap:** reading OA 4, MH 27 or EG 184, which disclaim a blueprint, as disclaiming principles (demotion; MH 46 and CA 3 keep the principles fixed).
- **Trap:** quoting GS 42's "no proper political mission" without its next sentence: that religious mission yields a task, light and strength for building the human community.
- **Trap:** reading LE 1 as disclaiming moral competence over economic life.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md)

### Rerum Novarum

**Leo XIII's encyclical of 15 May 1891 on the condition of the workers: the charter of the modern corpus.** Addressed to the world's bishops. Two cures were on offer for [the social question](#the-social-question): the liberal (a freely agreed wage is just and the State stays out, as §43 states it) and the socialist (community of goods administered by the State or the municipality, §4). Leo refuses both and sets three agents to work: the Church, a limited State, and the workers' own associations.

| Sections | Content (1903 English quoted; vatican.va numbering) |
|---|---|
| 1–3 | the condition: the guilds gone and nothing in their place; workers under "a yoke little better than that of slavery itself" (§3). §3's "rapacious usury" is [`theology-of-debt` 6.1](../theology-of-debt/lessons/06-01-usury-and-finance-in-modern-teaching.md)'s. |
| 4–15 | the socialist remedy refused: it hurts the worker first, who works in order to own (§§4–5); it is unjust, for "every man has by nature the right to possess property as his own" (§6), a right grounded in reason and foresight (§§6–7), in labour (§§9–10) and in the father's duty to his family (§§12–13); community of goods "must be utterly rejected" (§15) |
| 16–30 | the Church and the two classes: no solution "apart from" Religion and the Church (§16; see [competence in the moral order](#competence-in-the-moral-order)); class is not "naturally hostile to class" (§19); employers must not treat men "like chattels to make money by" (§20, quoting Jas 5:4 on wages); the rich may possess but not use as they please, and alms from surplus are owed (§22, quoting Aquinas) |
| 31–47 | the State: "the State as rightly apprehended", no particular form (§32); the [common good](#common-good) with distributive justice to every class (§33); it must not absorb individual or family (§35); "the law must not undertake more, nor proceed further, than is required for the remedy of the evil" (§36); the poor's special claim (§37); Sunday rest, hours, children and women (§§41–42); the liberal view stated (§43); labour personal and necessary (§44); the [wage floor](#just-wage) (§45); [widening ownership](#widening-ownership) (§46); no taxing property away (§47) |
| 48–61 | associations: unions the chief means (§49); a [natural right](#unions-and-the-strike) the State may not abolish, though it may stop evidently evil societies (§§51–52); watch over, not intrude (§55); Catholic societies where existing ones are hostile to religion (§§54, 57) |
| 62–63 | a joint remedy, with charity "the mistress and the queen of virtues" (§63) |

- **What was new in 1891** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)): not the principles but a pope applying them systematically to wage labour: free agreement does not settle a wage's justice, workers may associate, the State must protect them, while property and alms stay.
- **Leo marks his own line:** "a general and lasting law" for associations (§57) against their organization by "circumstances of fact and of time" (§56); rest "as a general principle" proportioned to strength (§42); wages and hours referred to boards because "circumstances, times, and localities differ so widely" (§45, the Source of [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)).
- **Reception.** Later documents keep its anniversaries (QA 15, MM 7, OA 1, LE, CA 2, MH 3); QA 39 calls it the "Magna Charta" of Christian social activity. *Magnifica Humanitas* 30 (2026): many conditions have changed; the primacy of labour and the bond between the Gospel and a just social order endure. RN appeared the year Engels published Marx's Gotha critique ([`history-of-political-thought` 6.6](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/06-06-marx-the-state-revolution-and-after.md)).
- **The text.** Quote only *The Great Encyclical Letters of Pope Leo XIII* (Benziger, New York, 1903; translator not named; public domain), cited by the vatican.va section numbers. The vatican.va English is a later revision (it differs at §§9, 13, 37, 45 and 46) and is never quoted. The 1903 transcription's "underlines" in §45's first sentence is an OCR slip for "underlies". vatican.va prints an unnumbered paragraph after §42: cite it as §42.
- **Level.** An encyclical: **rung 3** by genre; nothing in it is defined. At **rung 3**: the natural right of [private property](#private-property) and the rejection of community of goods (§§4–6, 15, 22), restated in CA 30, repetition adding weight within the rung (LG 25); the just-wage floor (§45); the State's duty to the weak and its limit (§§32–37); widening ownership as a direction for law (§46); the right of association (§51). That workers may not be treated as chattels or defrauded of wages (§20): **at least rung 3** (CCC 2414 and 2434 locate it).

**Principle or application:** principles: the rights to own, to associate and to a wage owed in justice; the State's protective duty and its limit; the worker's dignity; classes ordered to cooperation. Applications: Catholic societies as the preferred form (§54, "Under these circumstances"); boards for wages and hours (§45); land as the model of a worker's property (§47); the occupational line §42 draws for women (the principle beneath it, work fitted to strength, age and sex and protective of family life, is restated in LE 19, which does not repeat the line and forbids excluding women from work they are capable of). [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): the vanished guilds (§3), the causes of strikes (§39), the forecast that community of goods would dry up "the sources of wealth" (§15), which CA 12 judged accurate without making it doctrine.

- **Trap:** "an encyclical this foundational is infallible" / "being from 1891, merely Leo's opinion" (rung 3, restated by QA, LE and CA; nothing defined).
- **Trap:** "RN canonized the free market" (drops the wage floor, the State's protective duty and the common use of goods) / "RN endorsed socialism by attacking employers" (drops the property right and the refusal of class war).
- **Trap:** "RN's property argument is Locke's, or Aquinas's." Its labour argument (§§9–10) resembles Locke's labour-mixing ([`history-of-political-thought` 4.1](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/04-01-locke-against-filmer-and-property.md)) but is "further proof" after reason and foresight, beside the family argument, with use kept common (§22). For Aquinas the division of possessions is a human addition to natural law ([`history-of-political-thought` 2.4](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-04-aquinas-on-kingship-tyranny-and-property.md)). Never write "RN repeats Aquinas".
- **Trap:** "§19 condemns every strike" (CA 14: class struggle as a method, not every social conflict; §39 treats strikes as symptoms whose causes law should remove).
- **Trap:** RN is not evidence of theological liberalism (the event is [`church-history` 8.3](../church-history/lessons/08-03-leo-xiii-and-the-modernist-crisis.md)'s).
- *Not done here:* the premise-by-premise reading of §13 and §45, the reconstruction of §§4–15, and what intervention §37 contemplates are close-reading tasks the card leaves to the reader.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)

### Magnifica Humanitas

**Leo XIV's first encyclical, on safeguarding the human person in the age of artificial intelligence: the corpus's newest document.** Signed 15 May 2026 (RN's 135th anniversary, MH 3), published and presented 25 May 2026; addressed to the Catholic faithful, all Christians and all people of good will (§16). 245 sections: Introduction (1–16); chapter 1, the Gospel's dynamic approach and the corpus read (17–45); chapter 2, foundations and principles (46–89); chapter 3, technology and dominance (90–130); chapter 4, truth, work and freedom (131–181); chapter 5, the culture of power and the civilization of love, on war (182–228); Conclusion (229–245).

| MH | Paraphrased | Read in |
|---|---|---|
| 3–5 | RN gave impetus to the social doctrine; digitalization, AI and robotics are today's [*res novae*](#res-novae) (4, a reading of conditions); innovation driven by private, often transnational actors richer than many governments (5, an empirical premise) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md) |
| 22–29 | listening to the signs of the times is spiritual discernment (22); no definitive opinion on many concrete questions (23); not a model (24); OA 4 restated (26); not a handbook but shared discernment (27); principles presented in the *Compendium* (28); the title "Social Doctrine of the Church" credited to Pius XII (29) | 1.1, [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md) |
| 30–36 | readings of RN, QA (31: Pius XI formulated subsidiarity systematically; silent on the vocational order), John XXIII (33), GS, PP and OA (34–36) | [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)–[2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md) |
| 46 | a "core set of unchanging truths"; [the five principles](#the-five-principles) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md) |
| 50–58 | dignity and rights as foundations; the four senses restated; DI 1 quoted | [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md) |
| 59–81 | common good (59–64), universal destination (65–67), subsidiarity (68–72), solidarity (73–76), social justice (77–81) | Module 3 |
| 90–181 | AI, truth and work; §98 dates its own statements about AI | [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md) |
| 157, 159, 162 | economic freedom not absolute; measures beside GDP (an application); tax systems lightening the burden on the weakest | [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), 1.1, 1.2 |
| 34; 81; 194 | religious freedom restated; migrants a "litmus test" of social justice; the belief that deterrence is indispensable called erroneous | [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |

- **Level.** By genre **rung 3**. The rung belongs to the document; religious submission attaches to its principles; its readings of present conditions and its policy proposals are applications, owed serious, respectful consideration; its empirical premises have no rung. Restating *Dignitas Infinita* raises the grade, not the rung (3.1). MH 29's dating of the name is history: **no rung**. What chapter 5's sentences on war teach is [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)–5.4's question, not settled here.
