- **Citation slips worth knowing.** The vatican.va English of MH 31 misspells "Quadragesima Anno". MH 69's note cites "RN 26" for what is RN 35 in the vatican.va numbering. MH's note citing PT "87" gives the AAS page of what is PT 163 in the vatican.va English.

**Principle or application:** principles, owed religious submission: those it restates and develops (dignity, the five principles, data under the universal destination, profit never systematically sacrificing the person's work). Applications: platforms as subsidiarity's "highest level" (§71), oversight and data regimes, metrics beside GDP. Empirical premises: concentration of power (§§5, 95), deskilling (§150), the speed of job losses (§151).

- **Trap:** "MH banned AI" / "MH is the Pope's opinion about gadgets" (4.5).
- **Trap:** reading §98 as "none of it binds": it dates statements about AI, not statements about persons.
- **Trap:** reading MH 27 ("not a handbook") against MH 46 (unchanging truths) as if either cancelled the other; MH holds both and does not weigh them for a hard case (1.2).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md).

### The social encyclicals

**The customary list of the corpus's major documents: a scholars' convention, not a promulgated canon, and not all of them encyclicals.** The documents themselves keep *Rerum Novarum*'s anniversaries:

| Anniversary | Document | Its own words |
|---|---|---|
| 40 | *Quadragesimo Anno*, encyclical, 15 May 1931 | QA 15 |
| 70 | *Mater et Magistra*, encyclical, 15 May 1961 | MM 7 |
| 80 | *Octogesima Adveniens*, **apostolic letter** to Cardinal Maurice Roy, 14 May 1971 | OA 1 |
| 90 | *Laborem Exercens*, encyclical, signed 14 September 1981 (prepared for 15 May) | its introduction |
| 100 | *Centesimus Annus*, encyclical, 1 May 1991 | CA 2 |
| 135 | *Magnifica Humanitas*, encyclical, signed 15 May 2026 | MH 3 |

- **Genre traps.** *Laudate Deum* (4 October 2023) and *Dilexi Te* (signed 4 October 2025) are apostolic exhortations; *Justice in the World* (1971) is a synod's document, not papal; the *Compendium* is a compilation; *Economic Justice for All* (1986) is a bishops' conference letter. Genre narrows the search; the issuer fixes whose act it is ([`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)).
- **Level.** The list itself: **no magisterial level** (convention). Each document carries its own genre's weight.

**Principle or application:** neither: a convention.

- **Trap:** "it's on the list, so it's an encyclical", or so it binds like one.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md)

### Social doctrine and evangelization

**Teaching the social doctrine is part of preaching the Gospel, not a sideline in economics.** *Centesimus Annus* 5 (1991): teaching and spreading the social doctrine belongs to the Church's evangelizing mission and is an essential part of the Christian message, since it draws out the Gospel's consequences for life in society; the new evangelization must include it among its essential elements. CA 54: the social teaching is itself an instrument of evangelization. *Caritas in Veritate* 15 (2009): an essential element of evangelization. SRS 41 says the same. *Evangelii Nuntiandi* 29 (1975): evangelization would not be complete without an explicit message about rights and duties, social and international life, justice, development and liberation.

- **Level: rung 3.** This is the floor beneath the dispute over the 1971 Synod's [constitutive dimension](#constitutive-dimension): that evangelization includes the social message is rung-3 papal teaching (EN 29, 31; CA 5).

**Principle or application:** a permanent principle about the corpus.

- **Trap:** "the social teaching is an optional extra to the Gospel" (demotion).
- **Trap:** "action for justice is the Gospel's centre" (inflation; EN 27 makes the proclamation of salvation in Jesus Christ the foundation, centre and summit; EN 34–35 link liberation and salvation without identifying them).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md)

### The lay mission in temporal affairs

**Ordering society justly is directly the task of lay Catholics, acting with their own competence; pastors teach the principles.** *Gaudium et Spes* 43 (1965, Part I): secular duties belong properly, though not exclusively, to the laity; they should not imagine their pastors have a concrete solution to every problem, or that this is their mission. The faithful may disagree "with equal sincerity" about concrete solutions, and no one may claim the Church's authority for his own opinion. *Deus Caritas Est* 29 (2005): the direct duty to work for a just ordering of society is proper to the lay faithful; forming just structures is not directly the Church's task. CCC 2442 locates it: it is not for pastors to intervene directly in the political structuring of social life.

- **5.1 adds** ([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md) T6): citizens have the right and duty to vote for the common good (GS 75), and [participation](#participation) is a duty rooted in dignity (CCC 1913–1915). The CDF's Doctrinal Note (24 November 2002) §3: many parties are legitimate because many choices are contingent; §5: no appeal to pluralism or lay autonomy covers policies that undermine fundamental ethical requirements.
- **Ceded.** The laity's state within the Church is [`ecclesiology-and-mariology` 3.3](../ecclesiology-and-mariology/lessons/03-03-the-laity-charisms-and-the-sensus-fidei.md)'s; the *Compendium* gathers the lay commitment from §541.
- **Level: rung 3** (GS 43 in the doctrinal first part; DCE). The Doctrinal Note, a CDF act approved in ordinary form: **rung 3, light grade**.

**Principle or application:** a principle about who applies the principles; the choices the laity then make are applications.

- **Trap:** "Catholics must vote for a Catholic party" (inflation).
- **Trap:** "lay autonomy means any policy will do" (Doctrinal Note §5).
- **Trap:** reading GS 43 as a licence for any disagreement: its freedom covers concrete solutions, not principles, and the same paragraph forbids claiming the Church's authority for one's own opinion.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md)

### Compendium of the social doctrine

**A pontifical council's systematic map of the corpus (2004): a locator with no magisterial level of its own.** *Compendium of the Social Doctrine of the Church*, prepared by the Pontifical Council for Justice and Peace; Presentation dated 2 April 2004 (Cardinal Renato Raffaele Martino); a covering letter of the Secretariat of State (29 June 2004) conveys the pope's good wishes; there is no papal approval formula. MH 28 (2026) still cites it; it remains the current edition.

| Part | Sections | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| Introduction | 1–19 | |
| Part One | 20–208 | God's plan of love; the Church's mission and social doctrine; the person and human rights; the principles |
| Part Two | 209–520 | seven fields: family, work, economic life, the political community, the international community, the environment, peace |
| Part Three | 521–574 | social doctrine and ecclesial action; the lay faithful from 541 |
| Conclusion | 575–583 | for a civilization of love |

- **Its self-description.** §8: it cites documents of differing authority and leaves local applications to bishops' conferences. §80: the social doctrine, as moral teaching, shares that teaching's authority; each teaching's weight and the assent it asks depend on its nature, its independence from contingent and variable elements, and how often it is invoked (paraphrased; footnoted to CCC 2037 and *Donum Veritatis* 16–17, 23). §80 is the basis of [the standing question](#the-standing-question).
- **Sections the lessons use.** 72–73 (moral theology); 76–78 (the sciences); 84 n. 132 and 95 (PT the first document addressed to all men of good will); 94 ("socialization" a key word of MM); 164, 166, 169, 170 (common good, [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md)); 185–188 (subsidiarity, [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)); 66 (the social doctrine integral to the evangelizing ministry); 182 (files the option for the poor under the universal destination).
- **Level: no magisterial level of its own.** Each sentence keeps its source's weight, as [`theology-of-debt`'s card](../theology-of-debt/reference.md) already has it. A formulation an act later teaches gains that act's level: MH 60 cites §164 and MH 63 quotes §169. §80's rule applies LG 25's three signs: **rung 3** (1.2).

**Principle or application:** a locator, not on that axis; follow its footnote to the act.

- **Trap:** "the *Compendium* says so" as a level (overstates; its own §8 warns). Never put it in an "act" column.
- **Trap:** "it is only a summary, so it weighs nothing" (understates; it carries its sources' weight).

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)

### Res novae

**"New things": Leo XIII's word for the upheaval of his day, which Leo XIV turns to digitalization, AI and robotics.** *Rerum Novarum*'s Latin opens *Rerum novarum semel excitata cupidine*: the craving for "new things", that is, for upheaval. *Magnifica Humanitas* 4 (2026): where Leo XIII spoke of new things, today's are digitalization, AI and robotics, which are transforming the world; technology is not in itself hostile to humanity. On 10 May 2025 Leo XIV told the cardinals he took his name mainly because RN met the social question of the first industrial revolution, and the Church now offers her social teaching in answer to another, and to AI.

- **The shift.** In RN the "new things" are the revolutionary craving; in MH they are the technologies themselves.
- **Level.** MH 4's judgment is a reading of present conditions, resting on [empirical premises](#empirical-premise) and carried by [prudential judgment](#prudential-judgment), not a principle (1.1). The 2025 address: **rung 3 at its lightest grade**, at most; it explains a name and teaches nothing new.

**Principle or application:** application.

- **Trap:** treating "AI is today's *res novae*" as doctrine.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)

### Bishops conference teaching

**A bishops' conference teaches authentically in its own territory, never as universal magisterium: it can restate a principle at that principle's own weight, but cannot make its policy everyone's.** *Apostolos Suos* (John Paul II, apostolic letter *motu proprio*, 21 May 1998) §22 and its complementary norms, art. 1: a conference's doctrinal declaration is authentic teaching in its territory only if passed unanimously, or by two-thirds of the members with a deliberative vote and the Holy See's *recognitio*; it is never universal.

- **Conferences mark their own levels.** *The Challenge of Peace* (US bishops, 3 May 1983) §§9–10 sorts its statements into universally binding principles (non-combatant immunity, proportionality), restated papal and conciliar teaching, and applications that people of good will may read differently (its "no first use"), which are "not binding in conscience" but owed "serious attention and consideration". *Economic Justice for All* (1986) §134: a prudential judgment is only as sound as its principles **and** its information; §135: its specific recommendations do not carry the moral authority of universal principles and formal teaching, and ask "serious consideration". Keep the two wordings apart: "not binding in conscience" is CoP's alone. [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) runs CoP's self-marking on deterrence, with the conference's 1993 letter marking its own applications too.
- **Level.** The 1983 and 1986 letters are **particular** teaching from before 1998: restated universal principles keep their own weight; the applications, by the letters' own words, bind no one's assent. *Apostolos Suos*: its account **rung 3**, its norms **law**.

**Principle or application:** a conference's verdict on a bill, a figure or a threshold is an application. Even passed unanimously, a policy recommendation is not a doctrinal declaration: *Apostolos Suos* 22 concerns doctrinal declarations.

- **Trap:** "a conference letter binds every Catholic" / "it binds no one". It teaches authentically in its territory, never universally, and cannot move a universal level (the Winnipeg Statement, [`moral-theology` 6.3](../moral-theology/lessons/06-03-humanae-vitae-its-authority.md)).
- **Trap:** citing a papal newspaper interview against a conference letter as if it were a ruling. An interview is not a teaching act of the kind the ladder grades (1.1, Example 2).
- **Trap:** "prudential means optional": the letters themselves ask that their applications be weighed.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md).

### The five principles

**Magnifica Humanitas 46 (2026) names five principles of the social doctrine, to be held together: the common good, the universal destination of goods, subsidiarity, solidarity and social justice.** MH 46 says the social doctrine is living yet holds a "core set of unchanging truths". MH 27, in the same encyclical, calls it "not a handbook" of principles and norms to be applied, but shared discernment. The tension is the corpus's own: the principles stand, and applying them is discernment, not deduction (1.2). MH sets dignity and rights out first, as foundations (§§50–58), then the five:

| Principle | MH | Entry |
|---|---|---|
| Common good | 59–64 | [common good](#common-good) |
| Universal destination of goods | 65–67 | [universal destination of goods](#universal-destination-of-goods) |
| Subsidiarity | 68–72 | [subsidiarity](#subsidiarity) |
| Solidarity | 73–76 | [solidarity](#solidarity) |
| Social justice | 77–81 | [social justice](#social-justice) |

- **One teaching, two filings.** The *Compendium* (182) files the [preferential option for the poor](#preferential-option-for-the-poor) under the universal destination; MH 78 files it under social justice.
- MH reuses the five, with dignity, as the criteria for judging AI and digital power ([4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)). None of the five is new: social justice is QA's term of art ([2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)), subsidiarity QA's formulation, solidarity SRS's definition.
- **Level: rung 3** (an encyclical, signed 15 May 2026). How §27 and §46 weigh against each other in a hard case: **freely disputed (rung 5)**.

**Principle or application:** principles, owed religious submission.

- **Trap:** reading MH 27 as disclaiming the principles (demotion).
- **Trap:** reading MH 46 as making application a deduction from the five (MH 27 refuses that).

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)

### The standing question

**The question the course puts to every sentence: is this the principle or its application, and at what level?** 1.2's working form, sharpened by *Compendium* 80: **would the sentence still hold if the facts it mentions changed?** If yes, it is a principle; if it rests on them, an application or an [empirical premise](#empirical-premise). Then find the act that fixes the level ([`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md)).

- **Some sentences come marked:** CA 3 (principles in the doctrinal patrimony; the analysis of events not definitive); CoP §§9–10 ([bishops conference teaching](#bishops-conference-teaching)); RN 57 against RN 56; RN 45's "advisable"; *Gaudium et Spes*'s title note ([Gaudium et Spes](#gaudium-et-spes)); QA 65's tie to present conditions.
- **The test does not run itself.** Often only time sorts the necessary from the contingent (*Donum Veritatis* 24): a 1937 directive (*Divini Redemptoris* 58) was later returned to prudence ([practical directive](#practical-directive); [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)). Unmarked sentences may be disputed among theologians.
- **In use before 1891** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)): the Jubilee's land return and the same-day wage were Israel's law; the principle they carry is not.
- **Level.** The test is a method: **no magisterial level**. Its basis, *Compendium* 80 applying LG 25's three signs: **rung 3** (1.2).

**Principle or application:** the tool that sorts them.

- **Trap:** letting a strong verb decide. QA 82's "must" carries principles through a proposal whose form QA 86 leaves free.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)

### See, judge, act

**The method by which principles meet a situation: establish the facts, judge them by principles, decide what to do here.** *Mater et Magistra* 236 (John XXIII, 15 May 1961): three stages that should normally be followed in putting social principles into practice: take stock of the actual situation, assess it by those principles, and settle what the circumstances allow and require; it sums them up as "look, judge, act" (vatican.va English; Latin *aspicere, iudicare, agere*; the Italian text numbers it §217). MM 229: theory passes into practice with difficulty, partly because what justice requires in a given case is hard to pin down. MM 237: the young should practise it. MM 238: sincere Catholics may differ over applications. MM 239: where the Hierarchy has decided a point, Catholics must follow its directives ([practical directive](#practical-directive)).

| Stage | Kind of claim | Assent |
|---|---|---|
| SEE | [empirical premises](#empirical-premise), from the sciences and from those who live the situation | no rung; revisable on evidence |
| JUDGE | principles | mostly **rung 3**; religious submission |
| ACT | [prudential applications](#prudential-judgment), by the laity, local communities and those who govern | serious, respectful consideration; Catholics may differ (GS 43) |

- **Origin.** [Cardijn's JOC](#cardijn-and-the-joc). MM names no one; tracing the formula to Cardijn is the historians' work. Later restatements of the same movement: OA 4 (1971); MH 26–27 (2026).
- **Evidence and principle.** New evidence enters at SEE and can change the ACT; it reaches JUDGE only as data to be judged, never as a vote on the principle. SEE is already a theological act ([signs of the times](#signs-of-the-times)). [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md) runs it on climate: the seeing is climate science and hydrology, the judging the principles, the acting prudence.
- **Level:** a method commended, with **no doctrinal rung**. A procedure contains nothing to assent to; its outputs take their level from their content.

**Principle or application:** neither; it sorts claims by stage.

- **Trap:** "the teaching follows the data" (demotion: data revise applications, never principles).
- **Trap:** "a fact stated in an encyclical is doctrine", or "the method smuggles sociology into doctrine" (inflation; CA 3 denies definitive force to its own analysis of events).
- **Trap:** "MM made the method binding" (it names stages "normally" followed).
- **Trap:** "the evidence is contested, so the principle lapses" (JUDGE does not move when SEE is disputed) / "the Church requires this by-law" (ACT belongs to those responsible).
- **Trap:** "Vatican II invented see-judge-act": Cardijn's movement (1920s), MM 236 (1961), then GS 4 (1965).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)

### Cardijn and the JOC

**Joseph Cardijn's Young Christian Workers: the movement in which see-judge-act began.** Joseph Cardijn (1882–1967), a Belgian priest, built the Jeunesse Ouvrière Chrétienne (JOC, the Young Christian Workers). It took that name in 1924, was blessed by Pius XI in 1925, and was a specialized movement of Catholic Action. Its groups of young workers looked at their own workplaces, judged what they found by the Gospel, and chose one action they could take. Paul VI made Cardijn a cardinal in 1965.

- Catholic Action's interwar fate is [`church-history` 9.1](../church-history/lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md)'s. *Mater et Magistra* 236 adopts the method without naming him.
- **Level:** history, **no level**.

**Principle or application:** neither: history.

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)

### Mater et Magistra

**John XXIII's encyclical of 15 May 1961 on Christianity and social progress, for RN's seventieth anniversary (MM 7): the teaching carried to organized modern society, the farm and the poor nation.** Addressed to the bishops, clergy and faithful of the Catholic world (it is PT, not MM, that names all men of good will). Cite the vatican.va English numbers; the Italian numbers differently (English 59 = Italian 45; English 236 = Italian 217).

| MM | Paraphrased |
|---|---|
| 37 | QA's remedy recalled as economic and vocational bodies autonomous and independent of the State ([vocational orders](#vocational-orders)) |
| 53 | [subsidiarity](#subsidiarity), quoted from QA 79, the rule of State action in the economy |
| 59–67 | [socialization](#socialization): ties and associations multiply; to be steered by authorities with a sound view of the common good and by autonomous intermediate bodies |
| 65 | its Latin has the formula of the [common good](#common-good) that GS 26 later uses (the vatican.va English renders it loosely) |
| 71 | pay left neither to the market nor to the stronger ([just wage](#just-wage)) |
| 123–137 | farming a depressed sector; a farm programme (tax relief, bearable credit, insurance, price protection) |
| 132–133 | taxes proportioned to capacity (principle) beside account taken of farmers' difficulties (application), in adjacent paragraphs ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)) |
| 155–173 | rich and developing nations probably the hardest problem; [solidarity](#solidarity) forbids indifference (157); destroying needed food an outrage (161); aid must build capacity (163), respect each nation's character (169–170) and never be a disguised colonialism (171–173) |
| 219 | the person the foundation, cause and end of every social institution |
| 229, 236–239 | [see, judge, act](#see-judge-act); differences over application (238); directives and cooperation (239) |

- **Level.** An encyclical: its principles **rung 3**; the method **no doctrinal rung**.

**Principle or application:** principles: socialization steered by the common good and intermediate bodies; subsidiarity; solidarity's demand on rich nations; aid without domination. Applications: the farm programme, by MM's own words: directives to be applied as circumstances allow or demand (126), for a farm structure no one can fix in advance (142). Empirical premises: associations multiplying (59); why people leave the land (124).

- **Trap:** "MM's farm proposals are binding Catholic farm policy" (MM 126, 142).
- **Trap:** citing MM by the Italian numbering.
- **Trap:** "MM shows every application is free" (drops §239).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)

### Signs of the times

**The Church's duty to examine the events and movements of each age and read them by the Gospel.** *Gaudium et Spes* 4 (1965): the Church has always been bound to examine the signs of the times and interpret them in the Gospel's light. GS 44: the whole People of God, pastors and theologians above all, listens to the age's many voices, sorts them, and weighs them against God's word. *Magnifica Humanitas* 22 (2026): this listening is spiritual discernment, not mere sociology, and it unfolds revealed truth without changing it; Leo XIV's address of 17 May 2025 quotes GS 4.

- **Citations.** Cite GS 4 and 44. GS 11 speaks of discerning signs of God's presence in events but does not use the phrase in the vatican.va English. John XXIII's own earlier use of the phrase is not asserted in this course.
- **Level: rung 3** (a conciliar pastoral constitution, defining nothing; both passages doctrinal in its own note's sense).

**Principle or application:** a principle about the Church's mission. Which events count as signs, and what they show, is SEE work: it rests on evidence and is judged by the Gospel.

- **Trap:** "reading the signs of the times means following the age" (MH 22: facts are read, not obeyed).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)

### Rightful autonomy of earthly affairs

**Created things and societies have their own laws, which the sciences uncover by their own methods; autonomy meaning independence from God is false.** *Gaudium et Spes* 36 (1965) distinguishes two senses. Rightful: created things and societies have their own stability, truth, goodness, laws and order, which each science deciphers by its own methods; inquiry done scientifically and within moral norms never truly conflicts with faith. False: that creatures do not depend on God and may be used without reference to Him.

- **Three uses.** It warrants SEE taking its facts from the sciences ([1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)). It pairs with GS 42–43: no proper political mission, yet a task, light and strength, and the laity's proper field ([2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)). And its two senses are the course's answer on secularism ([5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md)): the first taught, the second refused. The CDF's Doctrinal Note (2002) §6 puts it as the political sphere's autonomy from the Church, not from morality. MH 20–21 (2026) quotes GS 36 and says the Church does not claim the State's functions.
- **Level: rung 3.**

**Principle or application:** a principle.

- **Trap:** reading "autonomy" as freedom from moral judgment: "a secular state, yes; a state beyond moral judgment, no" (5.1).
- **Trap:** the reverse, letting faith settle what a science must find out.

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md).

### Pacem in Terris

**John XXIII's encyclical of 11 April 1963 on universal peace in truth, justice, charity and liberty: rights paired with duties, at four scales, addressed also to all men of good will.** Published less than two months before John XXIII's death (3 June 1963). [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md) calls it the first encyclical whose address names all men of good will; the texts it cites for that (*Compendium* 84 n. 132 and 95; MH 33) say the first Church document so addressed, the wording [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md) uses.

| PT | Content (paraphrased) |
|---|---|
| 1–7 | an order the Creator wrote into human nature, revealed by conscience ([natural law as common language](#natural-law-as-common-language)) |
| Part I, 8–45 | among persons: [rights paired with duties](#rights-and-duties) (9, 11–34) |
| Part II, 46–79 | persons and [public authority](#political-authority): from God, a moral force, bound by the moral order (46–51); fits any genuinely democratic form (52); the common good its sole reason (54); a government violating rights lacks binding force (60–61) |
| Part III, 80–129 | among states, subjects of rights and duties under the same natural law |
| Part IV, 130–145 | the world community: the [universal common good](#universal-common-good) (132) and an [authority](#international-authority) of world-wide reach by consent (136–141); the UDHR a step forward, with just reservations (143–144) |
| Part V, 146–172 | pastoral exhortations, including [the hinge](#error-and-the-person-who-errs) (157–160) |

- The architecture: [the four orders of Pacem in Terris](#the-four-orders-of-pacem-in-terris). War in the atomic age and the arms race (109–113, 127) are [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)'s; emigration and immigration (25, 106) [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)'s. The vatican.va English leaves ¶1 unnumbered, and its heading "Signs of the Times" before §126 may be the translators'.
- **Level.** An encyclical: its principles **rung 3**.

**Principle or application:** principles as above. Applications: any design for a world authority, PT 145's hope for UN reform, each decision to work with a movement. Empirical premises: states acting by treaty now unequal to the universal common good (134–135).

- **Trap:** "PT asks for a world superstate" (138: consent, not force; 140–141: subsidiarity).
- **Trap:** "PT's rights are borrowed secular liberalism" (grounded in a nature God made, 9, 28, 38; paired with duties; reservations about the UDHR, 144; repeated citation of Pius XII's 1942 Christmas message, six years before it). "The Pope's opinion" understates rung 3.
- **Trap:** "PT 159–160 softened the judgment on communism" (the philosophy does not change; only the 1937 directive lapsed) / "PT made cooperation a duty".

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)

### Natural law as common language

**The social principles rest on a moral law written in human nature and knowable by reason, so they can be argued with anyone.** *Pacem in Terris* (1963), addressed to all men of good will as well as to the Church, argues from an order the Creator wrote into human nature, from which rights and duties flow (§§5–6, 9). *Caritas in Veritate* 59 (2009): the natural law, found as an ethical convergence across cultures, is a firm footing for dialogue across cultures, religions and politics. Leo XIV told parliamentarians in June 2025 that it is the compass for lawmaking.

- **Maritain's neighbouring view** ([Maritain](#maritain)): opposed philosophies can agree on a practical list of rights without agreeing why. PT addresses the same mixed audience but names its grounds, God and the natural law.
- **Ceded.** The theory is [`ethics` 4.4](../ethics/lessons/04-04-natural-law-aquinas.md)–[4.5](../ethics/lessons/04-05-the-new-natural-law-theory.md)'s; here it is only the language JUDGE claims to share with everyone.
- **Level.** That the social principles rest on a natural law knowable by all (PT 5–9; CiV 59): **rung 3**. The natural law's existence and knowability by reason: **at least rung 3**, fixed in [`moral-theology` 1.3](../moral-theology/lessons/01-03-natural-law-in-light-of-revelation.md), not re-levelled. The 2025 address: **rung 3 at the lightest grade**, at most.

**Principle or application:** a principle.

- **Trap:** "the social principles need faith to be seen" (PT argues to all) / "agreement in practice means the grounds do not matter" (PT names them).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)

### Octogesima Adveniens

**Paul VI's apostolic letter of 14 May 1971 for RN's eightieth anniversary (OA 1): the principles universal, the options local.** An **apostolic letter**, often miscalled an encyclical, addressed to Cardinal Maurice Roy as president of the Council of the Laity and of the Pontifical Commission Justice and Peace (OA 6).

| OA | Paraphrased |
|---|---|
| 4 | one message of universal validity is neither the pope's ambition nor his mission; Christian communities, with their bishops and in dialogue with other Christians and all people of good will, analyse their own country in the Gospel's light, draw from the social teaching principles of reflection, norms of judgment and directives for action, and discern the options (restated MH 26) |
| 26, 35 | a Christian cannot coherently adhere to the Marxist ideology, nor to a liberal ideology that frees individual liberty from every limit |
| 30 | quotes PT 159's distinction ([error and the person who errs](#error-and-the-person-who-errs)) |
| 31–34 | socialist and Marxist currents: distinguish aspiration, historical movement and ideology without forgetting the link between them |
| 44 | multinational enterprises beyond national control |
| 50 | one faith can lead to different commitments |

- Chenu took OA as the high point of the inductive turn ([the instability objection](#the-instability-objection)).
- **Level.** An apostolic letter to a cardinal sits in the **rung-3 range**; the verb decides (2.4). OA 4: **rung 3** (1.3). Its refusal of both ideologies: **rung 3**, restating Pius XI. Its 1971 verdicts on particular currents: applications.

**Principle or application:** principles: local discernment within universal principles; the two refusals. Applications: verdicts on currents; any option a community chooses. Empirical premise, with an application on it: OA 44's picture of multinationals.

- **Trap:** "OA 4 lets local churches make their own principles": communities draw principles *from* the social teaching; only the analysis and the options are local.
- **Trap:** "only a letter to one cardinal" (understates) / calling it an encyclical (overstates the genre).
- **Trap:** "OA approved socialism" / "OA condemned every socialist": both miss OA 31's distinctions.

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)

### The instability objection

**The worry that a method starting from shifting facts makes the Church's social teaching unstable; the documents reply that the principles are stable and the judgments revisable.** 1.3 states the objection without a name. Marie-Dominique Chenu OP pressed the opposite worry in *La "doctrine sociale" de l'Église comme idéologie* (Cerf, 1979): on his account, "social doctrine" as a timeless system deduced and applied from above had worked as an ideology, and he welcomed the inductive turn of *Octogesima Adveniens*. (His thesis is known here from secondary summaries; the lesson makes no page claim.)

- **The reply texts.** *Sollicitudo Rei Socialis* 3 (1987): constant in its principles of reflection, criteria of judgment and basic directives for action, "ever new" in adapting them to changed conditions. *Caritas in Veritate* 12 (2009): not two social doctrines, one before the Council and one after. Leo XIV (17 May 2025): the social doctrine claims no "monopoly on truth" in analysis or solutions; doctrine is a disciplined body of knowledge that helps form prudential judgments. *Magnifica Humanitas* 27–28: a shared discernment around an unchanging core. *Compendium* 85–86 locates the theme.
- **Level.** Chenu: **no magisterial level**. SRS 3, CiV 12 and MH 27–28: **rung 3**. The address: **rung 3 at its lightest grade**, at most. Whether the reply settles the objection: **rung 5**.

**Principle or application:** the reply is a principle about the corpus; whether it succeeds is open.

- **Trap:** making Chenu the author of the instability objection; he argued the reverse.
- *Not done here:* whether the inductive method makes social teaching more revisable than the rest of moral theology, or only more obviously so, is left open; the card states both sides' texts and gives no verdict.

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)

### The social question

**The nineteenth-century question of how the tradition's principles about property, the poor and the wage apply to industrial wage labour, where the worker owns nothing but his strength.** The old map was drawn for landholders, tenants, households and alms; a spinner sold her strength weekly to the man who owned the machines and had no plot to fall back on ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)). *Rerum Novarum* 1–3 describes it: the guilds gone and nothing in their place, workers isolated before employers and unchecked competition, under "a yoke little better than that of slavery itself" (§3, Benziger 1903).

- **Two answers were on offer before 1891:** [liberal political economy](#liberal-political-economy) (free contract; the State stands aside) and socialism (abolish private ownership). RN refused both ([2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)).
- **The Church's lateness.** Benedict XVI conceded that the Church's leadership was slow to see that a just ordering of society needed a new approach, and named Bishop Ketteler of Mainz as a pioneer (*Deus Caritas Est* 27, 2005; [social Catholicism](#social-catholicism)).
- **Secular diagnosis:** labour as a "fictitious commodity" ([`social-theory` 5.3](../social-theory/lessons/05-03-polanyis-great-transformation.md)). §3's usury sentence is [`theology-of-debt` 6.1](../theology-of-debt/lessons/06-01-usury-and-finance-in-modern-teaching.md)'s.
- **Level.** The term names a problem, not a teaching: **no level**. RN's picture of 1891 is an [empirical premise](#empirical-premise); DCE 27's judgment on the past is a historical judgment, not a principle.

**Principle or application:** neither: the problem the principles were brought to.

- **Trap:** "Catholic social teaching was invented in 1891 to answer Marx" (the principles are scriptural, patristic and Thomist, and the movement half a century old).

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)

### Liberal political economy

**The nineteenth-century view that a wage is a price, just when freely agreed, and that the State should stand aside from the labour contract.** As *Rerum Novarum* 43 states it: wages are regulated by free consent; once the agreed wage is paid, the employer has done his part, and the State intervenes only against non-payment. RN 44–45 refuses it: labour is personal and also necessary to life, and beneath free agreement lies a floor set by natural justice ([just wage](#just-wage)).

- **Its two premises** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)): a moral one, that free agreement makes a price just, which later teaching rejects (agreement between the parties is not sufficient to justify a wage morally: CCC 2434 locates it); and an empirical one, that free wages tend to rise, which answers to evidence.
- **Not to be confused** with the Church's later judgment on capitalism ([critique of liberal capitalism](#critique-of-liberal-capitalism), 4.3), with CA 42's conditional acceptance of a market economy ([Centesimus Annus 42](#centesimus-annus-42)), or with the Catholic liberal reading of the tradition ([catholic liberal reading](#catholic-liberal-reading), 6.4).
- **Ceded.** The economics is [`philosophy-of-economics` 5.4](../philosophy-of-economics/lessons/05-04-markets-and-desert.md)'s; Polanyi's critique [`social-theory` 5.3](../social-theory/lessons/05-03-polanyis-great-transformation.md)'s.
- **Level.** A rival doctrine: **no magisterial level**. Its rejection, that agreement does not settle a wage's justice: **rung 3** (RN 45; CCC 2434 locates).

**Principle or application:** a foil; its moral premise is refused as a principle, its empirical premise left to evidence.

- **Trap:** reading the refusal as a refusal of markets. RN refuses the claim that the bargain settles justice; CA 42 accepts a market economy inside a strong juridical framework, on conditions ([4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)).

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)

### Procuring, dispensing and use

**Aquinas's two relations to external goods: the power to get and manage them, where private possession is lawful, and their use, which the owner must hold as common.** *Summa Theologiae* II-II q.66 a.2, body of the article (English Dominican Province translation, Benziger 1947 printing; 1.4's Source):

- **First relation.** "The power to procure and dispense them": here "it is lawful for man to possess property", even "necessary to human life", for three reasons: each is more careful of what is his own than of what is common to many or to all; affairs run in more orderly fashion when each has his own charge; and there is more peace when each is content with his own.
- **Second relation.** "Their use": here man "ought to possess external things, not as his own, but as common", so that he is "ready to communicate them to others in their need" (warrant: 1 Tim 6:17–18).
- **The replies.** Ad 1: ownership is "not contrary to the natural law, but an addition thereto devised by human reason". Ad 2 (Basil's theatre): the first possessor sins only "if he excludes others indiscriminately from using it". Ad 3: Ambrose's words speak of use. The *sed contra* (Augustine on the Apostolici): the view that owning property is unlawful is "erroneous". It does not praise monks who own nothing; do not cite it for that.
- **Two senses of "common"** in one article: common in ownership, rejected as the general rule; common in use, required, meaning readiness to share, not co-ownership and not a licence for others to take.
- **Reception.** RN 22 quotes the article; QA 45 names the result the [twofold character of ownership](#twofold-character-of-ownership); GS 69 cites it. [`history-of-political-thought` 2.4](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-04-aquinas-on-kingship-tyranny-and-property.md) paraphrases it as political thought; [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md) puts it to work ([ownership and use](#ownership-and-use)).
- **Level.** As Aquinas's article: **no magisterial level**, common teaching (**rung 4**); **rung 3** where RN 22 and GS 69 restate it.

**Principle or application:** principles: possession lawful as to procuring and dispensing; use held common. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): the three reasons, comparative claims about how people tend to behave ("more careful", "more orderly", "more peaceful"). Prudential: how goods are divided, which ad 1 hands to human reason and positive law, the law that fills in what justice leaves open ([`history-of-political-thought` 2.3](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-03-aquinas-on-law.md)).

- **Trap:** "Aquinas taught a natural right to private property" (never write it; see [private property](#private-property)).
- **Trap:** reading "as common" as "the poor may take": a.2 grounds the owner's duty; only a.7's extreme case grounds the needy person's taking ([extreme necessity](#extreme-necessity)).

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)

### The Fathers on wealth

**The patristic teaching that what the rich hold beyond need is in a real sense the poor's, so that giving to them repays a debt.** Ambrose, *De Nabuthe* 12.53, on Ahab's seizure of Naboth's vineyard (lesson's translation from Migne, PL 14): "You are not giving the poor man what is yours; you are returning what is his." "The earth belongs to all, not to the rich." "So you pay a debt; you do not bestow what is not owed." The Latin decides it: *Debitum igitur reddis, non largiris indebitum*, a debt, not a gift.

- **Reception.** *Populorum Progressio* 23 (1967) quotes the passage to show that the right of property is not absolute and unconditional (paraphrased); *Centesimus Annus* 30 restates that it is not absolute. Aquinas cites Basil (the theatre, q.66 a.2 obj. 2) and Ambrose (obj. 3) as objections and reads both as speaking of use (ad 2–3).
- **Citation.** Give chapter and section, not the PL column: PP 23's note gives col. 747, but printings of PL 14 differ.
- **Ceded.** Chrysostom is [`patristics` 3.6](../patristics/lessons/03-06-john-chrysostom.md)'s; Gregory the Great's *Pastoral Rule* is read in [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md); the Fathers on usury are [`theology-of-debt` 4.1](../theology-of-debt/lessons/04-01-the-fathers-against-usury.md)'s.
- **Level.** A Father's sermon: **no magisterial level**, though weighty witness. The principle PP 23 draws from it, that property is not an absolute right: **rung 3** by the encyclical's act, restated in CA 30.

**Principle or application:** the principle is permanent; the sermon is its witness.

- **Trap:** "the earth belongs to all" read as a denial of ownership (Aquinas reads it of use; q.66 a.2's *sed contra* calls the denial erroneous).
- **Trap:** "Ambrose was only preaching, so it binds no one" (demotes the principle PP 23 takes from him).

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)

### Extreme necessity

**When need is urgent and there is no other remedy, taking what one needs from another's goods is not theft.** Aquinas, *Summa Theologiae* II-II q.66 a.7. Its conditions are read in [`history-of-political-thought` 2.4](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-04-aquinas-on-kingship-tyranny-and-property.md) and assumed here, never re-run on cases. *Gaudium et Spes* 69 (1965) restates the teaching (one in extreme necessity may take what he needs from the riches of others), with a note citing the article; CCC 2408 locates it. Beside it stands q.32 a.5: alms are commanded from one's surplus and toward one whose need is extreme.

- **Level.** As Aquinas's article: **no magisterial level**, common teaching (**rung 4**); **rung 3** as the Council restates it (1.4; 3.5).
- *Not done here:* what the Council takes over from a.7, what it adds, what its note requires for applying the principle, and which of the article's words its restatement leaves out are a close-reading task the card leaves to the reader.

**Principle or application:** a principle.

- **Trap:** "the poor may take from the rich": a.2 grounds the owner's duty; only a.7's extreme case grounds the needy person's taking (HPT 2.4).
- **Trap:** reading it as a licence for private redistribution, or for seizing idle property ([3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)).

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)

### Social Catholicism

**The nineteenth-century movement of bishops, laymen and scholars who took up the social question half a century before Rerum Novarum.** 1.4's five lines:

| Who | What |
|---|---|
| Frédéric Ozanam (1813–1853) and fellow students | found the Society of St Vincent de Paul, Paris, 1833 |
| Wilhelm Emmanuel von Ketteler (1811–1877), bishop of Mainz | *Die Arbeiterfrage und das Christentum* (1864): takes up Lassalle's critique of the wage system, calls the liberal self-help remedies futile, proposes workers' cooperatives funded by Christian capital |
| Albert de Mun and René de La Tour du Pin | after the Commune of 1871, Catholic workers' circles; La Tour du Pin wants the guilds restored |
| the Fribourg Union, gathered by Bishop Gaspard Mermillod | from 1884, work that became one source among several for RN |
| Cardinals Gibbons and Manning | Gibbons in Rome (1887) against condemning the Knights of Labor; Manning mediating the London dock strike (1889) |

- Benedict XVI names Ketteler a pioneer (*Deus Caritas Est* 27). The circles' founding year (1871 or 1872) and the Fribourg Union's end are left unstated.
- **Level:** history, **no magisterial level**. Their proposals (cooperatives, circles, guilds) are private applications: they carry only the weight of their arguments, and a Catholic may judge them unworkable without dissent. The social Catholics legitimately disagreed among themselves about guilds and about the State.

**Principle or application:** applications.

- **Trap:** "the social Catholics' guilds or cooperatives were Church teaching" (no magisterial level; even QA's later [vocational orders](#vocational-orders) are an application).

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)

### Just wage

**A wage is just not because it was agreed but because it meets a floor set by natural justice, and it is weighed by the worker's and family's needs, the firm's condition and the common good.** [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md) owns it.

- **Roots** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)). The hireling paid the same day "because he is poor, and with it maintaineth his life" (Deut 24:15, Douay–Rheims); wages "kept back by fraud" cry out (Jas 5:4); Lev 19:13. CCC 1867 lists injustice to the wage earner among the sins that cry to heaven.
- **The floor** (*Rerum Novarum* 44–45, Benziger 1903). Labour is personal and also necessary. Free agreement is the rule, yet beneath the bargain lies "a dictate of natural justice more imperious and ancient than any bargain between man and man": "remuneration ought to be sufficient to support a frugal and well-behaved wage-earner". Wage and hour questions go to "societies or boards", the State appealed to "should circumstances require". Never quote the vatican.va revision ("ought not to be insufficient") as the 1903 text, nor the 1903 file's OCR slip "underlines" (read "underlies").
- **The measures** (*Quadragesimo Anno* 64–75; [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)). The wage contract is not unjust in itself (64); partnership elements are advisable in present conditions (65); no single rule, but [three measures](#three-measures-of-the-wage) (66): the worker's and his family's support (71); the firm's condition, so ruinous wages cannot be demanded but poor management is no excuse (72); the economic common good, above all employment (74).
- **Restated.** *Divini Redemptoris* 51–53 (1937); *Mater et Magistra* 71 (pay left neither to the market nor to the stronger); *Pacem in Terris* 20; *Gaudium et Spes* 67; *Laborem Exercens* 19 (the wage is how most people reach goods meant for common use, so a just wage is the key test of a whole system's justice; a [family wage](#family-wage) or [family allowances](#family-allowance)); *Centesimus Annus* 8, 15; MH 37 (2026). CCC 2434 locates it: agreement between the parties is not sufficient to justify a wage morally.
- **Two justices** (4.2). The employer owes the floor and his share in [commutative justice](#commutative-justice); where a lone employer under competition cannot pay it, the shortfall falls on the system ([social justice](#social-justice); the [indirect employer](#indirect-employer)), and he must support the institutions that make fair pay possible (DR 53).
- **Level.** The floor, the three measures, the family measure, the two justices' duties and the contract's legitimacy: **rung 3**, at a heavy grade by frequency (4.2). That withholding wages owed is gravely wrong: **at least rung 3** (4.2; 1.4, citing LE 19 and CCC 2434, gives the principle as **rung 3**). The same-day rule was a judicial precept of Israel, no longer binding as such ([`old-testament` 2.6](../old-testament/lessons/02-06-covenant-and-law.md)). Partnership forms (QA 65; LE 14): applications. Every figure: **no rung**.

**Principle or application:** principles as listed. Applications: the route that closes a family's gap, the partnership forms, boards versus statute, every figure. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): whether a floor costs jobs (QA 74), whether allowances depress wages.

- **Trap:** a living-wage figure inflated into doctrine / the floor demoted to papal advice on generosity.
- **Trap:** "RN requires a statutory minimum wage" / "RN forbids minimum-wage laws" (§45 prefers boards and keeps the State as backstop).
- **Trap:** thinking the employer's duty ends with the agreed price (RN 43's rejected view).
- **Trap:** binding the household picture. QA 71 and LE 19 picture a father's wage and a mother at home, yet LE 19 makes allowances an equal route and GS 67 states the measure with no household model: the wage teaching binds the floor and the family measure (4.2).
- *Not done here:* setting RN 45's wording against what RN 22 says almsgiving is a duty of, and any payroll arithmetic.

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)

### Universal destination of goods

**God destined the earth and all it contains for the use of every person and people; every title to property answers to that purpose.** [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md) owns it. *Gaudium et Spes* 69 (1965): created goods should reach all fairly, under justice and with charity. *Centesimus Annus* 31: God gave the earth to the whole human race, excluding and favouring no one. *Laborem Exercens* 19 ranks the common use of goods the first principle of the whole ethical and social order, and LE 14 subordinates the right to private property to the right of common use. CCC 2402–2403: the destination "remains primordial". *Fratelli Tutti* 120 calls private property a [secondary natural right](#secondary-natural-right); *Magnifica Humanitas* 65–67 extends the destination to immaterial and cultural goods (patents, algorithms, platforms, data: [data as a shared good](#data-as-a-shared-good)) and MH 66 calls property's social function Church doctrine, not theological opinion.

- **Roots** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)). The land is the Lord's (Lev 25:23; [`theology-of-debt` 1.3](../theology-of-debt/lessons/01-03-the-jubilee-the-land-is-mine.md)); Ambrose ([the Fathers on wealth](#the-fathers-on-wealth)); use held common ([procuring, dispensing and use](#procuring-dispensing-and-use)); [extreme necessity](#extreme-necessity).
- **Development.** RN 8 and 22 (the gift to mankind; right to possess against right to use); QA 45 (ownership exists so that goods meant for all serve all); Pius XII's Pentecost radio message *La Solennità* (1 June 1941): every person's natural right to use the earth's goods comes first, and private property stays subordinate ([2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)); the [social mortgage](#social-mortgage) (John Paul II, 1979; SRS 42).
- **Applied.** The wage as most people's share in goods meant for all (LE 19; [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)); migration (*Exsul Familia*, 1952, quoting the 1941 message; [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)); water, land and the climate (*Laudato Si'* 93–95; [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)); the option for the poor (filed under it by *Compendium* 182; [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md)).
- **Level: rung 3**, heavily repeated (GS 69 and five encyclicals), already levelled in `theology-of-debt` 1.3 (CCC 2402–2403): cited, never re-levelled. Its premise, that God created all things, is defined faith (Lateran IV; Vatican I). Pius XII's message: **rung 3 at its lightest**, at most.

**Principle or application:** a principle, owed religious submission. Every land tax, estate decision, compensation formula or data regime is an application.

- **Trap:** reading it as "every object is at everyone's disposal": God gave the earth to mankind "not in the sense that all without distinction can deal with it as they like" (RN 8, Benziger 1903).
- **Trap:** a licence for private seizure ([extreme necessity](#extreme-necessity) is the only case of taking).
- **Trap:** "property's social function is the Pope's opinion" (MH 66 rules it out in so many words) / "the Church teaches this land tax" (inflation).

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)

### Private property

**The right to own goods as one's own: a natural right in Rerum Novarum's words, the ordinary means by which goods meant for all reach all, and always subordinate to that destination.** [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md) owns it; [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md) uses it against socialism.

- **Before 1891** ([1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md)). Aquinas: possession lawful and necessary as to procuring and dispensing, use common; ownership "an addition thereto devised by human reason" (q.66 a.2 ad 1). Leo XIII, *Quod Apostolici Muneris* (28 December 1878; Benziger 1903): "the Socialists wrongly assume the right of property to be of mere human invention"; the right is "derived from nature"; the rich are "under strict command to give of their superfluity to the poor". The hinge word is "mere".
- ***Rerum Novarum*** (1891; [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)). "Every man has by nature the right to possess property as his own" (§6), grounded in reason and foresight (§§6–7), labour as "further proof" (§§9–10) and the father's duty to his family (§§12–13); community of goods "must be utterly rejected" (§15). God gave the earth to mankind "not in the sense that all without distinction can deal with it as they like", leaving the limits of possession "to be fixed by man's own industry, and by the laws of individual races" (§8). The right to possess is distinct from the right to use (§22). The right "is derived from nature, not from man", and taxation must not drain it (§47; [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)).
- ***Quadragesimo Anno*** (1931; [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)). Ownership has [two sides](#twofold-character-of-ownership), individual and social (45); deny the social side and you drift into individualism, the individual side toward collectivism (46); right and use differ, and abuse or non-use does not forfeit the right (47); public authority may define owners' duties for the common good but cannot abolish the natural right to own and bequeath (49); property carrying too great a power may rightly be reserved to the State (114).
- **Later.** *Gaudium et Spes* 71 (a sphere of autonomy for person and family; an extension of freedom); *Laborem Exercens* 14 (upheld for productive goods too, never absolute, subordinate to common use; socializing some means of production not excluded in suitable conditions); *Centesimus Annus* 13 (opposition to private property follows from socialism's error about the person) and 30 (RN strongly affirmed the right's natural character; always defended; not absolute); *Fratelli Tutti* 120 (a [secondary natural right](#secondary-natural-right)); MH 66 (always subordinate).
- **The natural-right seam.** For Aquinas the division of possessions is a human addition to natural law ([`history-of-political-thought` 2.4](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-04-aquinas-on-kingship-tyranny-and-property.md)); RN says "by nature". Aquinas's own cross-reference, q.57 a.3, calls such ownership natural "in respect of natural reason which dictates it" (the *ius gentium*). Reconciling them that way is the theologians' work, **rung 4**, not a magisterial ruling (1.4; 3.5). Whether FT 120's "secondary" changes RN or restates the old order is **rung 5**. The floor for every reading: a natural right **and** subordinate.
- **Level: rung 3** (QAM; RN 6, 15, 22; CA 30), repetition adding weight within the rung. It guards the Decalogue's ban on theft, whose binding force on Christians Trent defined (Session VI, canon 19).

**Principle or application:** principles: a lawful, natural right; subordinate to the universal destination; regulable, never abolishable; productive property legitimate as it serves work. Applications: which duties a State fixes (QA 49), which property it reserves (QA 114), every estate, tax or compensation decision. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): Aquinas's three reasons; RN 15's forecast.

- **Trap:** "*Rerum Novarum* repeats Aquinas" / "Aquinas taught a natural right to private property" (never write either) / "Leo invented a right Aquinas denied".
- **Trap:** "RN's property argument is Locke's" (the labour argument is "further proof", beside reason and the family; use stays common, §22; [`history-of-political-thought` 4.1](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/04-01-locke-against-filmer-and-property.md)).
- **Trap:** reading QA 114 as endorsing nationalization in general, or QA 49 as forbidding any public ownership.
- **Trap:** reading FT 120's "secondary" as a revocable concession (3.5).
- *Not done here:* the premise-by-premise reading of RN 13 on the family's right of property.

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)

**Module 2: The tradition takes shape, 1891–1971**

### Common good

**The sum of the conditions of social life that let groups and their members reach their fulfilment more fully and readily: the conditions for flourishing, not the flourishing itself.** [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md) owns it. *Gaudium et Spes* 26 (repeated in GS 74); both footnote *Mater et Magistra* 65, whose Latin has the formula (the vatican.va English of MM 65 renders it loosely, and PT 58 quotes that loose form). CCC 1906 and *Magnifica Humanitas* 60 restate it. Its early use is RN 32–33: the State's task is public well-being, with distributive justice to every class ([2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md)).

- **Three elements** (CCC 1907–1909, locating GS 26): respect for the person and his fundamental rights, conscience and religious freedom included; the group's well-being and development (food, health, work, education, family); and peace, the security of a just order kept by morally acceptable means.
- **Three things it is not.** (1) *A sum:* not the simple sum of particular goods, but indivisible and reached only together (*Compendium* 164); MH 60–61 calls it a "plus" no adding of individual goods explains. (2) *The majority's good:* every citizen has a right to share in it, and authority favours no individual or category (PT 56, quoting Leo XIII in the Benziger 1903 English: "the civil power must not be subservient to the advantage of any one individual or of some few persons", *Immortale Dei* 5); GS 73; *Compendium* 169 on the minority. (3) *The collective over persons:* defined by reference to the person, the order of things serving the order of persons (GS 26; CCC 1905, 1912); spiritual goods included (PT 57); socialism's root error is the person treated as a molecule of the social organism (CA 13).
- **What it does.** The political community exists for it, the source of its legitimacy (GS 74; PT 54; CCC 1902–1903; [political authority](#political-authority)); everyone owes it according to role, and MH 59 calls that commitment non-negotiable for a Christian (GS 75; CCC 1913); it widens to a [universal common good](#universal-common-good) (PT 132). It is the criterion for applying subsidiarity and every substitution (CA 48; [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)) and solidarity's object, the good of all and of each (SRS 38; [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md)). Its demands vary with the period: GS 74 calls the concept dynamic (*Compendium* 166).
- **Applied later.** The authority's right to regulate immigration is exercised for the common good in its charge (CCC 2241; [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)). Integral ecology is inseparable from it, and the climate is a common good, of all and for all (*Laudato Si'* 156, 23; [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)).
- **Level: rung 3, at its heaviest grade** (MM 1961, PT 1963, GS 1965, MH 2026: repetition over sixty-five years). Its hardest edge, that no innocent person may be directly killed for the community's sake: **rung 1** (*Evangelium Vitae* 57; [`moral-theology` 5.1](../moral-theology/lessons/05-01-the-inviolability-of-innocent-life.md)). Aquinas's whole-and-part account: a theologian's, at most **rung 4**. The *Compendium*'s "not a simple sum" had no level of its own until MH 60–61 taught it.

**Principle or application:** the definition and the three exclusions are principles. Every judgment that a budget, tax, curfew, school system or international body serves the common good is an application; so is PT 64's list of public services, the duty's 1963 shape. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): whether a measure delivers what it promises; GS 26's observation that interdependence grows daily.

- **Trap:** reading "sum" as a sum of goods.
- **Trap:** "the common good requires this budget, so Catholics must back it" (inflation) / "a slogan, or whatever wins the vote" (demotion).
- **Trap:** "Aquinas taught that the person exists for the State."

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)

### Critique of socialism

**The Church's judgment that socialism, as a doctrine and system of society, is irreconcilable with Christian teaching, because it absorbs the person into the collective.** [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md) owns the whole line.

| When | Act | What it says (paraphrased unless public domain) |
|---|---|---|
| 1846–1864 | Pius IX; Syllabus (1864) §IV | socialism and communism listed among errors condemned since 1846 (the Syllabus is never presented as defined) |
| 1878 | *Quod Apostolici Muneris* | "the Socialists wrongly assume the right of property to be of mere human invention" (Benziger 1903) |
| 1891 | *Rerum Novarum* 4–6, 15, 19 | community of goods hurts the worker first, who works in order to own; it is unjust, since ownership is a right "by nature"; it would breed disorder; it "must be utterly rejected" (§15); class is not naturally hostile to class (§19) |
| 1931 | *Quadragesimo Anno* 111–121 | communism seeks class war and the end of private ownership by any means (112); moderate socialism's just demands, even reserving dangerously powerful property to the State, are not peculiar to socialism (113–115); socialism that stays socialism, as doctrine, historical fact or movement, conceives society for material advantage alone and cannot be reconciled with Catholic teaching (117–119); no one can be at once a good Catholic and a true socialist (120); QA 121 invokes the pope's solemn authority |
| 1937 | *Divini Redemptoris* 58 | communism intrinsically wrong; and a ban on collaborating with it in any undertaking (a [practical directive](#practical-directive), later handed back to prudence by PT 160) |
| 1963, 1971 | PT 159–160; OA 26, 31–34 | the philosophy does not change; movements are distinguished from it ([error and the person who errs](#error-and-the-person-who-errs)); aspiration, movement and ideology distinguished |
| 1991 | *Centesimus Annus* 13, 24 | the fundamental error is anthropological: the person a molecule of the social organism, no longer the subject of moral decision; opposition to private property follows; the 1989 collapse read through the violation of rights and the void of atheism |

- CCC 2425 locates the rejection (with that of the market's absolute rule).
- **Level.** RN 6 and 15: **rung 3**. QA 117–120: **rung 3 at a heavy grade**, not a definition: QA 121's "solemn authority" is LG 25's manner of speaking within rung 3, and with no defining formula, default down (can. 749 §3). DR 58's "intrinsically wrong": **rung 3**. CA 13: **rung 3**. The 1949 Holy Office decree is a penalty: **law**, no rung ([`church-history` 9.1](../church-history/lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md)).

**Principle or application:** a principle as to socialism as a doctrine and system; an application as applied to any named party or movement (PT 159–160; OA 31). DR 58's collaboration ban was an application. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): RN 15's forecast that common ownership would dry up wealth; CA 24's account of why the system failed (CA 3: its analysis of events is not definitive).

- **Trap:** "QA defined that no socialist can be Catholic" (overstated) / "1931 politics", "a Cold War opinion" (understated; CA 13 restates the core).
- **Trap:** "QA condemned moderate socialism because it still attacks property" (wrong ground: the concept of society, QA 117–119).
- **Trap:** "PT lifted the condemnation" / "PT made cooperation a duty".
- **Trap:** "the Church condemns this welfare programme as socialism" (inflation).
- *Not done here:* the premise-by-premise reconstruction of RN 4–15, and any 1890s socialist's statement of his own remedy.

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Widening ownership

**The direction that law should multiply owners.** *Rerum Novarum* 46 (Benziger 1903): "The law, therefore, should favor ownership, and its policy should be to induce as many as possible of the humbler class to become owners" (the vatican.va revision reads "of the people"; quote the 1903 text). RN 47 names the gains, property more equitably divided, greater abundance, men attached to their country, provided taxation does not drain a man's means: the State would be "unjust and cruel" if "under the name of taxation" it took "more than is fitting" ([1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)). *Quadragesimo Anno* 59–63 repeats it: wages must let workers save and acquire modest property ([6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)); so do *Mater et Magistra* 113 and *Gaudium et Spes* 71.

- **Level: rung 3**, a direction for law stated as principle (2.1; 6.3), repetition adding weight within the rung. Whether distributism is its natural implementation: **rung 5** (6.3).

**Principle or application:** the direction is a principle. The means (land reform, tax policy, share schemes, cooperatives) are applications; so is RN 47's land as the model of a worker's property. RN 47's remark that no one would leave his country if it gave him a decent living is an empirical prediction, not a teaching on migration.

- **Trap:** "RN 46 makes distributism doctrine" (it teaches a direction and leaves the means; preferring small property to large is a lay proposal with no magisterial level, 6.3) / "multiplying owners is nostalgia" (demotes a principle QA, MM and GS restate).
- *Not done here:* which premises of Belloc's argument RN 46 or QA actually supports.

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)

### Unions and the strike

**Workers have a natural right to associate, and may strike, within limits, for what justice owes them.** [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md) owns the strike; [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md) the right.

- ***Rerum Novarum*** (Benziger 1903). Unions are the chief means, "of workmen alone, or of workmen and employers together" (§49). Private societies "cannot nevertheless be absolutely, and as such, prohibited by the State", for to enter one "is the natural right of man" (§51, the Source of 2.1); a State that forbids them "contradicts the very principle of its own existence". The law may stop associations whose purposes are "evidently bad, unlawful, or dangerous to the State", with safeguards (§52). The State should watch over them without intruding (§55). Where existing societies are run on principles hostile to Christianity and force men "either to join them or to starve", Catholics should found their own (§54, "Under these circumstances"; §57). Organization follows "circumstances of fact and of time" (§56); "a general and lasting law" governs the rest (§57).
- **Strikes in RN.** Strikes come "frequently" from long hours, hard work or low pay, and the law should remove the causes (§39, an empirical premise); a strike endangering public peace may justify the State's intervention (§36). RN states no right to strike.
- **Later.** *Quadragesimo Anno* 35 (1931): where purely Catholic unions are impossible, Catholics may join others that respect justice and leave conscience free, with the bishops' approval and religious associations alongside. *Gaudium et Spes* 68 (1965): the right to found unions; the strike a necessary but ultimate means. *Laborem Exercens* 20 (1981): unions indispensable, struggling *for* the just good, not *against* others: no class war, no group "egoism", no party's instrument; the strike legitimate in proper conditions and within just limits; no personal penalty for striking; an extreme means, never abused, above all for political ends; essential community services secured, by law if necessary. CCC 2435 locates it.
- **Level.** The right of association and the strike's legitimacy within limits: **rung 3** (RN 51; LE 20). QA 35's permission: an application.

**Principle or application:** principles: the natural right of association; the strike legitimate within limits. Applications: the Catholic, confessional form (RN 54–57); whether a given strike meets the conditions; which services are essential and what law secures them.

- **Trap:** "Leo told Catholics to form Catholic unions, so mixed unions are forbidden" (inflates §54's conditional advice) / "since that is application, so is the right to unionize" (demotes §51's natural right).
- **Trap:** a right without limits / limits without a right.
- **Trap:** "RN 19 condemns every strike" (CA 14; §39) / "RN grants a right to strike" (LE 20 does).

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md)

### Subsidiarity

**A higher body must not take over what lesser bodies can do; it exists to help them: support, coordinate, and supply what they cannot, only while they cannot.** [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md) owns it.

| Text | Paraphrased (RN quoted from Benziger 1903) |
|---|---|
| RN 35–36 (1891) | "the State must not absorb the individual or the family"; the law no further than the remedy requires. A forerunner, not the formulation. |
| QA 79 (1931) | granting that changed conditions now need large associations for many tasks small ones once did, it is an injustice and a grave harm to right order to assign to a greater and higher association what lesser ones can do, since social activity by its nature should help (Latin *subsidium afferre*) the members of the social body, never destroy and absorb them; a most weighty principle of social philosophy, fixed and unchangeable |
| QA 80 | the State, freed of lesser tasks, directs, watches, urges and restrains; the principle of "subsidiary function" |
| MM 53 (1961); PT 140–141 (1963) | the rule of State action in the economy; the rule binding a world authority to states |
| CA 48 (1991; quoted in CCC 1883) | a higher community should not interfere in a lower one's internal life and strip it of its functions, but support it in need and help coordinate its activity with the rest of society, toward the common good; the State's [substitute function](#substitute-function) as brief as possible; against the [Social Assistance State](#social-assistance-state) |
| CiV 57 (2009) | help to the person through the autonomy of [intermediate bodies](#intermediate-bodies), aimed at emancipation; the person always able to give. CCC 1884: God entrusts to each creature what it can do. CCC 1885: opposed to all collectivism |
| MH 68–72 (2026) | grounded in the person (68); does not justify the State's disengagement but guides its action, so that social actors are neither stifled, replaced nor reduced to mere facilitators (69); no paternalism; decisions as close to people as possible (70); in the digital sphere the highest level is major economic and technological actors (71) |

- **Pairing.** With solidarity, each correcting the other (CiV 58: [subsidiarity and solidarity](#subsidiarity-and-solidarity)); CA 15: the State's indirect mode beside its direct one. In aid ([aid and subsidiarity](#aid-and-subsidiarity), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)).
- **Not to be confused** with the EU's legal principle ([EU subsidiarity](#eu-subsidiarity)) or with Burke, Hegel or Tocqueville on intermediate bodies; it prescribes no constitution, federal or unitary, and no size of budget (GS 42, 76). The economist's version of the question is fiscal federalism (`public-economics` 8.1–8.2).
- **Level: rung 3**, [`fundamental-theology` 4.4](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-04-the-ladder-of-doctrinal-authority.md)'s own example of that rung ([4.3](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-03-religious-submission.md) runs the triad on it). QA's "unchangeable" raises the grade, not the rung. Nothing defines it. MH 31 credits Pius XI with formulating it systematically.

**Principle or application:** principles: both halves, their ground in the person, substitution only while it is needed. Applications: which level should run fire services, water or schools; QA's [vocational orders](#vocational-orders); MH 71's judgment about platforms (resting on an empirical premise, MH 95); any verdict that a bill fits subsidiarity (no rung). [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): QA 79's observation that some tasks now need large associations; CA 48's claim that needs are best met by those nearest them.

- **Trap:** reading it as a small-government presumption, or as a licence to withdraw help (MH 69).
