- **Trap:** "subsidiarity is one opinion about federalism" (demotion) / "subsidiarity forbids the national fire service" (inflation).
- **Trap:** the mirror error, help that absorbs: paternalism (MH 70).
- **Trap:** calling RN the source of the formulation (QA 79 is).

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)

### Quadragesimo Anno

**Pius XI's encyclical of 15 May 1931 on the reconstruction of the social order, for RN's fortieth anniversary: subsidiarity, social justice and ownership's social side, carried by a 1931 proposal.** Addressed to the bishops and all the faithful, in the depths of the Depression. QA 15 gives its aims: recall RN's benefits, defend and develop its teaching, and judge the economic regime and socialism. Paraphrase only (post-1930); cite by the vatican.va numbers (¶1 unnumbered; the numbering ends at 148).

| QA | Paraphrased | Entry |
|---|---|---|
| 35 | where purely Catholic unions are impossible, Catholics may join others that respect justice and leave conscience free | [unions and the strike](#unions-and-the-strike) |
| 39, 41 | RN the "Magna Charta" of Christian social activity; no competence in technique, a right and duty wherever the moral law is touched | [competence in the moral order](#competence-in-the-moral-order) |
| 45–49 | ownership's two sides; right and use differ; regulation without abolition | [private property](#private-property) |
| 57–58, 71, 74, 88, 110 | distribution by the norms of the common good, "that is, social justice" | [social justice](#social-justice) |
| 59–63 | the [redemption of the proletariat](#redemption-of-the-proletariat): wages that let workers acquire modest property | [widening ownership](#widening-ownership) |
| 64–75 | the wage contract not unjust; partnership advisable in present conditions; several measures | [just wage](#just-wage) |
| 78–80 | individualism wrecked the associations, leaving the individual and an overloaded State (78); the principle (79); "subsidiary function" (80) | [subsidiarity](#subsidiarity) |
| 81–87 | the Industries and Professions | [vocational orders](#vocational-orders) |
| 88 | free competition useful within limits but cannot direct economic life; social justice and [social charity](#social-charity) must | |
| 91–96 | the Italian system described, credited and feared | [interwar corporatism](#interwar-corporatism) |
| 105–109 | an "economic dictatorship" of those controlling credit replaced competition; abroad an "internationalism of finance" | [economic dictatorship](#economic-dictatorship) |
| 111–121 | socialism judged | [critique of socialism](#critique-of-socialism) |

- **The lesson's thesis** ([2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)): QA is the corpus's clearest case of a permanent principle carried by a contingent proposal; tell the cargo from the cart. Later documents kept the bodies, not the blueprint (MM 37; PP 39; CA 48–49), and MH 31 (2026) summarizes QA without the vocational order (the vatican.va English there misspells the title "Quadragesima Anno").
- **Ceded.** The politics of 1931 (*Non abbiamo bisogno*, 29 June; the 2 September settlement) are [`church-history` 9.1](../church-history/lessons/09-01-the-church-and-the-totalitarian-states.md)'s; credit itself is [`theology-of-debt` 6.1](../theology-of-debt/lessons/06-01-usury-and-finance-in-modern-teaching.md)'s.
- **Citation trap.** The CCC 1883 footnote's "Quadragesimo anno I, 184–186" gives AAS page numbers, not sections; QA 87's Latin has *doctrinae socialis christianae* ([social doctrine of the Church](#social-doctrine-of-the-church)).
- **Level.** An encyclical: **rung 3** by genre. The principles of 45–49, 64–75 and 88: **rung 3**; that the State cannot abolish what nature confers restates RN 6 and 47. Social justice's duties: **rung 3**; the word itself has no rung of its own. Subsidiarity: **rung 3**, its "unchangeable" raising the grade, not the rung. The socialism judgment: **rung 3 at a heavy grade**, not a definition. The proposal (81–87) and the assessment of the Italian system (91–95) carry no rung of their own. Pius XII's 1941 radio message: **rung 3 at its lightest**, at most.

**Principle or application:** principles: ownership's two sides; right versus use; regulation without abolition; distribution by the common good; the wage's several measures; subsidiarity; class war not society's order; competition unable to govern itself; socialism's incompatible idea of society. Applications: the Industries and Professions as a programme; partnership elements, which QA 65 ties to present conditions; the verdict on the Italian system (95); which property duties a State fixes. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): wages pushed beyond measure cause unemployment (74); power had passed to those controlling credit (105); competition had destroyed itself (109).

- **Trap:** "QA requires a corporative constitution" (mistakes the cart for the cargo; QA 82's "must" carries principles through a proposal whose form QA 86 leaves free) / "QA is a corporatist period piece" (dumps the cargo with the cart; subsidiarity, social justice and ownership's social side are restated in CA 48 and MH 31, 46).
- **Trap:** "QA endorsed Fascism" (drops QA 95's fears) / "QA condemned the system" (it credited advantages).
- **Trap:** "QA defined that no socialist can be Catholic" (rung 3 at a heavy grade, no defining formula).
- **Trap:** treating QA 105–109's diagnosis as timeless principle.
- *Not done here:* tracing the State's role from RN through QA, MM and GS, and wage arithmetic on the measures.

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)

### Commutative justice

**The justice of exchange between two parties: giving each exactly what is owed under contract or ownership.** Defined in [`moral-theology` 3.3](../moral-theology/lessons/03-03-the-cardinal-virtues-and-the-gifts.md) (ST II-II q.61 a.1; [the parts of justice](../moral-theology/reference.md#the-parts-of-justice)); this course applies it.

- **QA 47** (1931): commutative justice commands respect for the division of possessions; the owner's duty to use his goods well falls under other virtues, whose obligations cannot be enforced at law ([2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)). **QA 110:** relations of capital and labour must conform to commutative justice, with Christian charity, while public institutions answer to social justice.
- **The wage** ([4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)): the employer owes the RN 45 floor and his share of the measures in commutative justice. Its limit: a lone employer under competition may be unable to pay what justice asks (DR 53), and then [social justice](#social-justice) takes over; DR 51: besides commutative justice there is social justice.
- **Level.** The duties: **rung 3**. The category itself is theology, with no separate rung (4.2).

**Principle or application:** a principle.

- **Trap:** thinking the employer's duty ends with the agreed price (RN 43's rejected view; CA 8).
- **Trap:** loading onto one employer what social justice asks of the whole system (QA 71; DR 53).

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)

### Social justice

**The justice that orders a society's institutions and the sharing of its goods to the common good, so that no class shuts another out and the weakest can live with dignity.** *Quadragesimo Anno* 57–58 (1931): the distribution of goods must be brought back to the norms of the common good, "that is, social justice", by which no class may exclude the other from the benefits; the term goes to work in QA 71, 74, 88, 101, 110 and 126. QA is where it enters papal teaching as a working term; Pius XI had already paired "legal or social" justice in commending Aquinas (*Studiorum Ducem*, 29 June 1923, Latin *de iustitia legali aut de sociali*).

- **Defined.** *Divini Redemptoris* 51 (1937): it demands from each individual all that is necessary for the common good. DR 52: it is not satisfied while workers lack a wage that supports a family, a chance of modest property, and insurance for age, illness and unemployment. DR 53: under competition, employers who cannot pay justly alone must organize, and support, the institutions that make it possible ([4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)).
- **Directing economic life.** QA 88: with [social charity](#social-charity), it must direct economic life in place of free competition, and penetrate the juridical and social order ([4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)).
- **2026.** *Magnifica Humanitas* names it one of [the five principles](#the-five-principles) (46). MH 77–81: justice concerns structures, not only acts; social justice is an order's capacity to let everyone, the weakest above all, live a dignified life (77); it begins with the least, and the [preferential option for the poor](#preferential-option-for-the-poor) belongs here (78, citing SRS 42); [structures of sin](#structures-of-sin) and a restorative dimension (79); digital exclusion (80); the treatment of migrants a "litmus test" (81; [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)).
- **Its strongest secular denial** is Hayek's ([`philosophy-of-economics` 5.5](../philosophy-of-economics/lessons/05-05-hayek-knowledge-order-and-the-mirage-of-social-justice.md)).
- **Level.** The word is vocabulary with **no rung of its own**; the duties it names: **rung 3** (QA 57–58; DR 51–52; MH 77).

**Principle or application:** the duties are principles; which institutions realize them (wage councils, insurance schemes, allowances) are applications.

- **Trap:** treating the word as if it carried a rung, or as a slogan that carries none (its duties are rung 3).
- **Trap:** confusing it with [commutative justice](#commutative-justice): one governs this contract, the other the whole system's sharing.
- **Trap:** reading it as one class's claim on the other: QA 57 forbids either class to shut the other out.

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Social charity

**Pius XI's name for the charity that should be the soul of a just social order: the older vocabulary of what is now called solidarity.** *Quadragesimo Anno* 88 (1931): neither free competition nor economic dictatorship can direct economic life; social justice and social charity must, social justice shaping the juridical and social order and social charity being its soul, which public authority should protect. QA 126: society to be restored on the basis of social justice and social charity. QA 137: no charity can substitute for justice owed and wrongfully withheld; justice removes the causes of conflict but cannot unite hearts ([3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md)).

- **Lineage.** *Centesimus Annus* 10 (1991) identifies Leo XIII's "friendship" (RN 21, 25), Pius XI's "social charity" and Paul VI's "civilization of love" with the principle now called [solidarity](#solidarity). *Fratelli Tutti* 180 (2020) restates QA 88's image, citing it. CCC 1939 locates it.
- **Level: rung 3** (encyclicals); vocabulary, not a separate rung.

**Principle or application:** a principle.

- **Trap:** social charity is not almsgiving, and it presupposes justice; it never stands in for it (QA 137).

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Vocational orders

**QA's 1931 proposal: self-governing bodies joining employers and workers of one industry or profession by social function, to replace class war with cooperation by trade.** *Quadragesimo Anno* 81–87 (Latin *ordines*; the vatican.va English says "Industries and Professions", never "vocational orders", which is the course's name; MM 37's "vocational bodies" is the nearest magisterial English): the State and every citizen should work to end class conflict and promote their cooperation (81); the State's social policy must re-establish them (82); labour is no mere commodity, yet the labour market lines society up in two camps as if for battle (83); bodies organized by social function, for the common good (84–85); their form is as free as the form of government (86); free associations should prepare the way (87).

- **Afterlife.** In 1937 Pius XI wanted the form adapted to place and circumstance (*Divini Redemptoris* 54). *Mater et Magistra* 37 (1961) recalls the bodies as autonomous and independent of the State; *Populorum Progressio* 39 (1967) judges a variety of professional organizations and unions a help to freedom, the opposite of one body per branch; CA 48–49 keep subsidiarity and intermediate communities; MH 31 (2026) is silent on the design. Later documents kept the [intermediate bodies](#intermediate-bodies), not the blueprint.
- **Level.** The document is **rung 3**; the proposal is an application and **binds no one's assent**. It carries principles: overcoming class conflict, labour no commodity, subsidiarity.

**Principle or application:** an application carrying principles: the cart, not the cargo.

- **Trap:** "QA requires a corporative constitution" (QA 82's "must" against QA 86's free form).
- **Trap:** "the proposal's fate sinks the encyclical" (its principles are rung 3 and restated).
- **Trap:** quoting "vocational orders" as QA's own English.
- **Trap:** treating the earlier guilds of La Tour du Pin or the Fribourg Union ([social Catholicism](#social-catholicism)) as Church teaching.

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)

### Interwar corporatism

**The state-organized corporative systems of the 1920s and 1930s, whose state-made monopoly syndicates resembled QA's proposal and claimed it.** *Quadragesimo Anno* 91–95 describes, without naming Italy, one state-recognized syndicate per calling with a monopoly, compulsory dues and binding agreements (92); corporations of delegates as organs of the State (93); strikes and lockouts forbidden, public authority intervening (94). QA 95 grants advantages (peaceful class cooperation, socialist organizations repressed, a special magistracy) and then reports fears: that the State is substituting itself for free activity, and that the system serves particular political ends.

- **The claim on QA.** On 1 May 1934 Austria adopted a constitution that refounded it as a corporative state, and its makers claimed the encyclical as their warrant; Portugal's constitution of 1933 drew on the same text ([2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md); facts from secondary sources).
- **The hard edge, flatly.** The proposal resembled interwar corporatism and was used by it, and QA 95 counts the repression of socialist organizations among the Italian system's advantages. Catholic theologians answer in three moves: read QA against itself (free, self-governing associations, 83 and 87, against state-made monopolies, 92; QA 95's fears are subsidiarity's own objections); file the design as an application, as the later documents treat it; and concede that judging a dictatorship's labour regime is prudential, where *Donum Veritatis* 24 allows that magisterial interventions may not be free of every deficiency.
- **Level.** The regimes and constitutions: history, **no magisterial level**. QA's assessment (95): an application with no rung of its own.

**Principle or application:** QA 95 is a prudential assessment; the principles it applies (free association, subsidiarity) stand.

- **Trap:** "QA endorsed Fascism" (drops QA 95's fears) / "QA condemned the system" (it credited advantages).

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md)

### Maritain

**Jacques Maritain (1882–1973), the Thomist layman whose political philosophy is Pacem in Terris's philosophical neighbour and the Catholic liberal reading's first source.** *Man and the State* (University of Chicago Press, 1951): the person is the end of political life; the state is an instrument of the body politic, not its sovereign; natural law grounds rights. From UNESCO's 1947 inquiry into the grounds of human rights, whose symposium he introduced, he took the lesson that opposed philosophies can agree on a practical list of rights without agreeing why ([2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)). *Integral Humanism* (1936) proposed a "new Christendom", pluralist and secular rather than sacral; postwar Christian democratic movements drew on him ([6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md); [catholic liberal reading](#catholic-liberal-reading)).

- **Neighbour, not source.** PT addresses the same mixed audience but names its grounds, God and the natural law, and its notes do not cite him.
- **Economics.** He was a sharp critic of bourgeois capitalism, so the Catholic liberal reading's economic half is Novak's, not his (6.4).
- **Ceded.** The man and his Thomism are [`thomistic-synthesis` 6.3](../thomistic-synthesis/lessons/06-03-strict-observance-and-existential-thomism.md)'s; this course owns his political thought.
- **Level:** a philosopher's: **no magisterial level**.

**Principle or application:** philosophy, neither.

- **Trap:** "PT drew on Maritain" (no citation; a neighbour) / "practical agreement means the grounds do not matter" (PT names them).

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md)

### Socialization

**Mater et Magistra's name for the multiplication of social ties, associations and state programmes in modern life: ambivalent, made by free people, and so to be steered.** MM 59–67 (1961). The vatican.va English never says "socialization": it says the increase in social relationships; the Italian text has *socializzazione* and the Latin *socialium rationum incrementa*; the *Compendium* (94) calls it a key word of MM. Ties and associations multiply and State intervention grows with them (59–60); this widens access to health care, education, housing and work (61), but narrows freedom and initiative (62); free people made it, so it can and must be steered (63–64), by authorities with a sound view of the common good and by really autonomous [intermediate bodies](#intermediate-bodies) (65–66). MM 53 makes [subsidiarity](#subsidiarity) the rule of State action.

- **Level.** That socialization must be steered by the common good and autonomous intermediate bodies: **rung 3**. MM 59's description of the age: an [empirical premise](#empirical-premise).

**Principle or application:** the duty to steer is a principle; the description of the age is an empirical premise; how a given programme should be shaped is an application.

- **Trap:** confusing socialization with socialism. LE 14's "socialization" of means of production is a different usage ([4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)).
- **Trap:** reading MM as blessing or condemning organized society as such: the test is whether persons stay its authors.

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)

### Solidarity

**A virtue, not a feeling: a firm and persevering determination to commit oneself to the common good, the good of all and of each, because all are really responsible for all.** [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md) owns it.

- **The thing is older than the definition.** Leo XIII has the Church try "to bind class to class in friendliness and good feeling" (RN 21, Benziger 1903); Pius XI's [social charity](#social-charity) (QA 88); *Mater et Magistra* 155–158: the solidarity of the human race forbids wealthy nations indifference to hunger elsewhere ([2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)). CA 10 identifies these with solidarity.
- **The definition.** *Sollicitudo Rei Socialis* 38 (John Paul II, dated 30 December 1987): interdependence is to be accepted as a moral category; the fitting response, a moral and social attitude that is a virtue, is solidarity: not vague compassion or shallow distress, but a "firm and persevering determination" to commit oneself to the [common good](#common-good). SRS 39: the stronger take responsibility for the weaker and share with them; the weaker claim their rights and contribute, neither passive nor destructive; intermediate groups do not selfishly press particular interests; valid only where members recognize one another as persons, and by analogy between nations. SRS 36–38: only solidarity, with grace, overcomes [structures of sin](#structures-of-sin). SRS 40: a Christian virtue close to charity, with the communion of the divine Persons as its supreme model (a horizon, not a blueprint).
- **Pairing and successors.** CiV 58: [subsidiarity and solidarity](#subsidiarity-and-solidarity) stay linked ("social privatism" without the one, "paternalist social assistance" without the other); CA 15: the State's direct mode. *Fratelli Tutti*: fraternity and [social friendship](#social-friendship). MH 73–76 (2026): both a principle (the objective order in which each person's good depends on others') and a virtue (SRS 38), with attention to those most in need; global, intergenerational and digital; one of [the five principles](#the-five-principles). Aid that builds the recipient's own agency ([6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)).
- **Level: rung 3** (SRS 38–39; CA 10, 15; CiV 58; FT; MH 73–76). Repetition across 39 years raises the grade within rung 3, never to rung 2 (can. 749 §3). It rests on the command to love one's neighbour (Mt 22:39), proposed as revealed (**rung 1**), but SRS's social definition does not inherit that rung. Wojtyła's philosophy of the attitude: **no magisterial level**.

**Principle or application:** principles: interdependence as a moral category; solidarity a virtue owed by all, to all; SRS 39's three duties; structures of sin to be overcome; the two principles correcting each other. Applications: SRS's 1987 judgments on the blocs; CA 15's list of State measures; CiV 58's counsel on aid; every aid target, tariff or wage rule offered "in solidarity". [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): that interdependence is growing (SRS 38; MH 74); that aid can breed dependence (CiV 58).

- **Trap:** "solidarity is a feeling, or one party's slogan" (demotion) / "solidarity requires this trade deal, so its opponents dissent" (inflation).
- **Trap:** equivocating with Durkheim's solidarity ([`social-theory` 3.1](../social-theory/lessons/03-01-the-division-of-labor-and-social-solidarity.md)) or with solidarity in Adam and in Christ.
- **Trap:** philanthropy standing in for justice (QA 137).

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)

### Rights and duties

**Pacem in Terris's teaching that every right flowing from human nature comes bound up with duties, in the same person and in others.** PT 9 (1963): every human being is a person, with intelligence and free will, and so has universal, inviolable and inalienable rights and duties flowing from his nature.

| PT | The catalogue (paraphrased) |
|---|---|
| 11 | life and the means of a decent life, including support in unemployment that is enforced, not chosen |
| 12–17 | good name, truth and information; culture and education; worship by the right dictates of conscience; a state in life; the family |
| 18–22 | work and initiative; working conditions; a just wage; private property, productive goods included, with its social obligation |
| 23–27 | meeting and association; movement, and emigration when there are just reasons (taught in [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)); a part in public life; legal protection |
| 28–34 | the duties: same-person pairs (the right to life and the duty to preserve it; a decent standard and living becomingly; seeking truth and searching deeper, 29); everyone else's duty to respect a right (30); collaboration to supply the means (31–33) |

- PT 20, quoting Pius XII, calls work a duty nature imposes. Authority's chief task is to secure rights, and a government that violates them lacks binding force (60–61; [political authority](#political-authority)). PT draws repeatedly on Pius XII's 1942 Christmas radio message, and restates RN's rights of property and association (21, 23). The Universal Declaration (1948) is a step forward, with just reservations (143–144). The grounding of rights in dignity is [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)'s ([rights grounded on dignity](#rights-grounded-on-dignity)).
- **Level.** Rights paired with duties: **rung 3** (PT 28–30). Dignity's own level is fixed in [`creation-grace-last-things` 2.1](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md).

**Principle or application:** principles; the scheme that secures a right (a benefit's size and length, job-search rules) is an application (2.3, Example 1).

- **Trap:** "PT's rights are borrowed secular liberalism" / "the UDHR baptized" (grounded in nature and God, paired with duties, with reservations about the Declaration).
- **Trap:** claiming a right while refusing its paired duty: building with one hand and tearing down with the other (PT 30).

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)

### The four orders of Pacem in Terris

**PT's architecture: one moral order written in human nature, applied at four scales.** The order the Creator wrote into human nature and conscience reveals (PT 1–7) is applied:

| Order | PT | Key content |
|---|---|---|
| Among persons | Part I, 8–45 | [rights paired with duties](#rights-and-duties) |
| Between persons and public authority | Part II, 46–79 | [political authority](#political-authority): from God, a moral force, binding in conscience only within the moral order |
| Among states | Part III, 80–129 | states as subjects of rights and duties under the same natural law (80) |
| In the world community | Part IV, 130–145 | the [universal common good](#universal-common-good) and an [authority](#international-authority) adequate to it |

- Part V (146–172) gives pastoral exhortations, including [the hinge](#error-and-the-person-who-errs) (157–160).
- **Level.** The claims within each order carry their own levels, mostly **rung 3**; the four-part arrangement is the document's structure, not a separate teaching.

**Principle or application:** structure, neither.

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)

### Political authority

**Authority in the political community is natural and necessary, comes from God as its source, is designated by the people in a form they choose, exists for the common good, and binds in conscience only within the moral order.** [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md) owns the full account; [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md) and [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md) supply pieces.

- **Origin.** No society holds together without someone directing all to the common good, so authority, like society, comes from nature and has God for its author (*Immortale Dei* 3, 1885; *Diuturnum* 11, 1881). GS 74 (1965): political community and public authority belong to the order God designed. PT 46–48 (1963): from God (citing Rom 13:1–6), bounded by the moral order, a moral force.
- **Designation and form.** Rulers may in some cases be chosen by the people; the choice designates the ruler without conferring the right to rule (*Diuturnum* 6). Rule by one or many may be approved if just and for the common advantage (*Diuturnum* 7; *Immortale Dei* 4). Leo rejects the view that all power is delegated by the people and revocable at their will (*Diuturnum* 5; cf. PT 78). GS 74 leaves the regime and the rulers to the citizens; PT 52: compatible with any genuinely democratic form ([forms of government](#forms-of-government); [democracy and truth](#democracy-and-truth)).
- **End.** The common good is authority's sole reason (PT 54) and the source of its legitimacy (GS 74); it acts as a moral force, not despotically, and is legitimate only by morally licit means (CCC 1902–1903). No reason of state licenses an evil means, against Machiavelli ([`history-of-political-thought` 3.1](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/03-01-machiavelli-the-prince.md); [`moral-theology` 2.3](../moral-theology/lessons/02-03-intention-and-circumstances.md)).
- **Limit.** Laws against the moral order have no binding force in conscience (PT 51, citing Acts 5:29); a government that violates rights lacks binding force (PT 60–61). When laws are manifestly at variance with the divine law, "to resist becomes a positive duty, to obey, a crime" (*Sapientiae Christianae* 10, 1890, Benziger 1903). Two brakes: [the limits of obedience](#limits-of-obedience) and the five conditions of [armed resistance](#armed-resistance) (CCC 2243).
- **Level: rung 3.** That authority comes from God restates Rom 13:1; do not raise it to rung 1 from the verse. That no form of government is prescribed is itself **rung 3** (GS 74; *Immortale Dei* 4), so "forms are applications" is the corpus's own answer.

**Principle or application:** principles as above. Applications: that this regime meets CA 46's conditions, that this law is unjust, that this rising meets CCC 2243.

- **Trap:** "Romans 13 makes every command binding" (PT 51; *Sapientiae Christianae* 10).
- **Trap:** "authority from God is a medieval relic" (demotion; GS 74 restates it) / "the Church requires democracy, or this party" (inflation).

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md)

### Universal common good

**The common good of the whole human family, which no single state can secure.** *Pacem in Terris* 132 (1963): the human family is one, so there is a universal common good, an imperative of human nature itself. PT 133–135: diplomacy and treaties once sufficed, but states acting alone are now unequal to it. PT 139: it cannot be determined without reference to the human person. GS 26 (1965): interdependence gives the common good an increasingly universal complexion, with rights and duties toward the whole human race (CCC 1911 locates it). MH 64 (2026): any plan to eliminate or subjugate a nation is gravely immoral; more effective international institutions should safeguard the global common good without compromising peoples' legitimate diversity.

- **Its authority.** Hence some public authority with world-wide reach, by consent and under subsidiarity ([international authority](#international-authority); [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)).
- **In migration** ([5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)): the good for which an authority regulates immigration is the community's genuine good (PT 106), and that good includes its share of the universal common good.
- **Level: rung 3** (PT 132; GS 26; [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md)). It rests on the unity of the human family, not on trade statistics.

**Principle or application:** the principle, and MH 64's judgment on subjugating a nation, are principles; MH 64's call for more effective institutions is an application. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): PT 134–135's claim that states acting alone are unequal to it; GS 26's "interdependence grows daily".

- **Trap:** "a 1963 mood" (demotion) / "the universal common good requires this body" (inflation).
- **Trap:** confusing the principle with the empirical observation of interdependence.

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)

### International authority

**The teaching that the universal common good requires some public authority with world-wide competence, set up by consent and bound by subsidiarity.** *Pacem in Terris* 136–141 (1963): authority must be effective for its common good (136); the moral order itself demands some general public authority of world-wide reach (137); set up by the consent of all nations, never imposed by force, and impartial (138); aimed at personal rights (139); bound by subsidiarity, creating conditions for states rather than limiting their sphere or taking over their functions (140–141). PT 142–145: the United Nations, the Universal Declaration, and the hope that the UN adapt its structures (145).

- **Later** ([6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) T8, which owns the question): PP 78 hopes for a world authority able to act in law and politics; CA 58, for effective international agencies to match a globalizing economy; CiV 67 (2009) asks for reform of the UN and of economic and financial institutions, and for a world political authority regulated by law, observing subsidiarity and solidarity, universally recognized, with power to secure compliance; LS 175 wants institutions able to impose sanctions; FT 172–173 add that it need not be a personal authority and must be legally limited against capture by a few; MH 64 and 226 call the UN essential and in need of profound reform.
- **Level.** The need for a world-competent authority on PT 138–141's conditions: **rung 3** (6.2). Any institutional design, CiV 67's included, PT 145's hope and every UN reform: applications.

**Principle or application:** the need and the conditions are principles; the body, its powers and the treaty are applications. PT names no parliament, police or tax.

- **Trap:** "PT calls for a world superstate overriding the states" (138, 140–141) / "PT's world authority was a 1963 mood" (demotion).
- **Trap:** "opposing a world parliament is dissent" (inflation).

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md).

### Error and the person who errs

**John XXIII's two distinctions: between an error and the person who errs, and between a false philosophy and the historical movements it inspired, with cooperation left to prudence.** *Pacem in Terris* 157–160 (1963), paraphrased:

- **157:** the principles rise from natural rights, so applying them means cooperating with non-Catholics of natural integrity, while compromising neither religion nor morality (quoting MM 239).
- **158:** it is always legitimate to distinguish error from the person who errs, who never forfeits his dignity and may yet reach the truth.
- **159:** a false philosophy of man is distinct from the economic, social, cultural and political undertakings it inspired: the formula, once set down, does not change; the undertakings change with conditions and may contain good elements that right reason approves.
- **160:** whether the moment for cooperation has come, and its manner and degree, are for prudence, chiefly of those who lead in the field, acting by natural law, the social teaching and the directives of Church authority, which may intervene to judge practical applications ([practical directive](#practical-directive)).
- OA 30 quotes PT 159. [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md) uses it as the hinge between QA and DR on one side and OA 31 on the other. PT names no movement.
- **Level.** The two distinctions: **rung 3** (PT 158–159). Each decision to cooperate: an application.

**Principle or application:** the distinctions are principles; every decision about when, with whom and how far is prudence.

- **Trap:** "PT lifted the condemnation of socialism or communism" (159: the formula is fixed; QA 120 and DR 58's "intrinsically wrong" stand; only the 1937 directive lapsed).
- **Trap:** "PT made cooperation a duty" (drops 160's question whether its time has come).
- **Trap:** "PT 160 leaves everything to lay prudence" (drops its second half and MM 239).

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Practical directive

**A hierarchy's decision about how principles are to be carried out in a given time and place: it binds in conduct, within its authority's competence, but does not become a timeless truth.** *Mater et Magistra* 239 (1961): where the Hierarchy has decided a point, Catholics must follow its directives, for the Church may judge how principles are carried out. PT 160 (1963) restates the Church's right to judge practical applications.

- **The example.** *Divini Redemptoris* 58 (19 March 1937): no one who would save Christian civilization may collaborate with communism in any undertaking whatsoever (paraphrased, with its conditioned subject). That was a 1937 directive, which PT 160 handed back to prudence; DR 58's judgment that communism is intrinsically wrong (**rung 3**) stands.
- **How it binds.** It asks obedience in conduct within its competence and serious consideration, not religious submission as to a permanent truth, and it can lapse when conditions change ([2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)). [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md) puts it as discipline: it binds conduct, not assent to its truth (law binds practice: [`thomistic-synthesis` 6.5](../thomistic-synthesis/lessons/06-05-what-the-church-has-said-about-thomism.md)). GS 43 forbids anyone to pass off his own application as such a decision.
- **Level:** discipline in force, **no doctrinal rung**; the right to issue it (MM 239) is taught at **rung 3**.

**Principle or application:** an application with binding force in conduct.

- **Trap:** treating a directive as a timeless principle / treating it as weightless.
- **Trap:** "MM shows every application is free" (§239 follows the section that allows disagreement).

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md)

### Gaudium et Spes

**Vatican II's pastoral constitution on the Church in the modern world (7 December 1965), whose note on its own title tells you to sort its sentences.** Note 1: the constitution is "pastoral" because, resting on doctrinal principles, it sets out the Church's relation to the world and to people today. Part One gives her teaching on the person, the world and human relations; Part Two treats urgent questions in that light, so its matter mixes elements of permanent and of only transitory value. The whole is read by the general norms of interpretation, with changeable circumstances in mind, especially in Part Two (paraphrased; [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)).

| GS | Paraphrased | Entry |
|---|---|---|
| 4, 44 | the duty to read the signs of the times by the Gospel | [signs of the times](#signs-of-the-times) |
| 25 | every social institution has the person as its starting point, its subject and its goal | [person as end of institutions](#person-as-end-of-institutions) |
| 26, 74 | the common good defined | [common good](#common-good) |
| 36 | rightful autonomy; autonomy from the Creator false | [rightful autonomy of earthly affairs](#rightful-autonomy-of-earthly-affairs) |
| 42 | no proper political, economic or social mission; a religious purpose that yields a task, light and strength; bound to no system | [the doctrines disclaimers](#the-doctrines-disclaimers) |
| 43 | the split between faith and daily life among the age's graver errors; temporal tasks the laity's proper field; honest disagreement on concrete solutions; no one claims the Church's authority for his own | [the lay mission in temporal affairs](#the-lay-mission-in-temporal-affairs) |
| 67–71 | the wage; the enterprise joins persons, so participation in its running is to be promoted; unions and the strike as an ultimate means; the universal destination and extreme necessity; property and land reform | [just wage](#just-wage), [unions and the strike](#unions-and-the-strike), [universal destination of goods](#universal-destination-of-goods) |
| 73–76 | the political community; competence in the moral order; bound to no political system | [political authority](#political-authority), [competence in the moral order](#competence-in-the-moral-order) |
| 80 | acts of war aimed indiscriminately at whole cities condemned, the Council's only formal condemnation | [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md) |

- **The note sorts elements, not parts.** Part Two also holds permanent elements: GS 68's principle that an enterprise joins persons together, and GS 80's condemnation. Its worked example (2.4, Example 1): GS 25 is a principle with nothing tied to 1965; GS 68 states a permanent principle and leaves the means to be worked out; GS 70's principle (monetary decisions owe care to weak economies) is permanent, its particular worry belongs to the 1960s world of fixed parities.
- **Genre.** "Pastoral" means resting on doctrine, not containing none ([`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)). The Council as event is [`church-history` 9.2](../church-history/lessons/09-02-the-second-vatican-council.md)'s. MH 34 (2026) calls GS a turning point.
- **Level.** No new dogma: its principles (25, 36, 42–43) are **rung 3**, at the conciliar grade; its time-bound elements are applications.

**Principle or application:** sorted sentence by sentence, as its own note directs.

- **Trap:** "pastoral, so not binding" (demotion; the note says it rests on doctrine).
- **Trap:** "Part One binds and Part Two doesn't" (misreads the note; GS 80 is in Part Two).
- **Trap:** "Vatican II called justice constitutive of the Gospel" (the word is the 1971 Synod's: [constitutive dimension](#constitutive-dimension)).
- **Trap:** "the Church has no political mission", from GS 42 with its next sentence dropped.
- **Trap:** a GS 39 progress-versus-Kingdom reading is [`creation-grace-last-things` 7.2](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/07-02-the-parousia-the-last-judgment-and-the-new-creation.md)'s, not this course's.
- *Not done here:* GS's place in a trace of the State's role from RN to the Council.

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)

### Populorum Progressio

**Paul VI's encyclical on the development of peoples (26 March 1967): the social question carried to the whole world as development.** Paraphrased:

| PP | Content |
|---|---|
| 13 | the Church does not wish to be involved in the politics of any nation; she offers a global perspective on man (the "no technical solutions" wording is SRS 41's and CiV 9's, not PP 13's) |
| 14, 42 | [integral development](#integral-human-development): of each person and of the whole person, not growth alone (PP 14's "eminent specialist" is L.-J. Lebret, per its note) |
| 23–24 | quoting Ambrose, property not an absolute and unconditional right; great estates may sometimes have to be expropriated (taught in [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md); [expropriation](#expropriation)) |
| 31 | revolutionary uprising breeds new injustices, except where there is manifest, long-standing tyranny gravely damaging fundamental personal rights and the common good |
| 39 | a variety of professional organizations and unions helps freedom ([vocational orders](#vocational-orders)) |
| 76–87 | under the heading "Development, the New Name for Peace": gross disparity breeds discord (76); development means peace (87) |

- The international programme is [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)'s. MH 35 (2026) cites PP 76 for "the new name for peace" and recalls the founding of the Pontifical Commission Justice and Peace. PP 31's exception is restated in *Libertatis Conscientia* 79 (1986); CCC 2243 lists five conditions for armed resistance (a locator; [armed resistance](#armed-resistance)).
- **Level.** An encyclical: PP 14 and development as peace **rung 3**, repeated since, frequency raising the grade (LG 25); PP 31's exception and its conditions **rung 3**.

**Principle or application:** principles: integral development; development and peace; property not absolute; PP 31's exception. Applications: the international programme; whether a given regime is the tyranny PP 31 excepts; PP 24's judgment on particular estates. [Empirical premises](#empirical-premise): PP 76's claim that disparity endangers peace; PP 31's remark that, as everyone knows, uprisings breed new injustice.

- **Trap:** reading PP 31 as opening a door to revolution: the exception sits inside a warning and covers only manifest, long-standing tyranny.
- **Trap:** citing "development is the new name for peace" as one numbered sentence: it is the heading over 76–87, and §87 says it outright.

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md).

### Integral human development

**Development of each person and of the whole person, in every dimension, not economic growth alone.** *Populorum Progressio* 14 (1967): authentic development must promote each person and the whole person; again PP 42 and 20–21. *Caritas in Veritate* 18 (2009) restates it. *Magnifica Humanitas* 82–83 (2026): development is a duty and a right of individuals and of peoples, truly human only when it puts people, not accumulated wealth, at the centre. The heading over PP 76–87 calls development the new name for peace ([Populorum Progressio](#populorum-progressio)).

- **Its edges** ([6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md), which owns the international order built on it): [superdevelopment](#superdevelopment), an excess of goods for some that enslaves them to possession, is as inadmissible as underdevelopment (SRS 28); aid must serve the recipients' own agency. MH 159 asks for measures of progress beside GDP ([1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md)).
- **Level: rung 3**, repeated from PP 14 to MH 82, which raises its grade within the rung (LG 25). Development as peace: **rung 3**.

**Principle or application:** a principle. PP 51's world fund from arms budgets, SRS 43's reforms, CiV 60's aid share and MH 159's metrics are applications; every development policy is one (OA 50; GS 43).

- **Trap:** "integral development is 1960s economics" (demotion) / "it requires this treaty or aid target" (inflation).
- **Trap:** reducing development to growth in output.

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)

### Justice in the World

**The document of the 1971 Synod of Bishops (De iustitia in mundo), published by papal rescript: weighty counsel, not papal teaching, and the source of the "constitutive dimension" sentence.** Issuer: the Synod of Bishops, second ordinary general assembly (autumn 1971; the justice debate ran 19–30 October and the text was voted on 6 November, per Charles Murphy, *Theological Studies* 44, 1983). Published by a *Rescriptum ex audientia* of 30 November 1971 (AAS 63 (1971) 897): the pope ordered the Synod's two texts of recommendations (*vota*) published, approved and confirmed only the conclusions that agree with the norms in force, and reserved to himself which proposals should become directives or norms of action.

- **The text.** AAS 63 (1971) 923–942; the Latin is unnumbered (introduction, then four parts), and English editions number it 1–77. Its introduction disclaims a deep analysis of the world's condition (English §3), so its picture of the world is a perception, revisable. The sentence that made it famous: [constitutive dimension](#constitutive-dimension) (English §6).
- **Level: no magisterial level of its own.** A synod counsels ([`ecclesiology-and-mariology` 4.5](../ecclesiology-and-mariology/lessons/04-05-vatican-ii-primacy-collegiality-synodality.md)). The rescript is a generic, conditional approval: at most **rung 3, lightest grade**, for what JW restates. What "constitutive" means: **rung 5**.

**Principle or application:** its proposals are counsel published by papal decision; its core is taught elsewhere at rung 3 (EN 29, 31, 34; CA 5: [social doctrine and evangelization](#social-doctrine-and-evangelization)).

- **Trap:** calling it a papal document, or a Vatican II text.
- **Trap:** quoting "constitutive" without its hedge, "appear to us".
- **Trap:** "JW is not magisterium, so ignore what it says about justice" (loses the rung-3 floor taught in EN and CA).

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)

### Constitutive dimension

**The 1971 Synod's phrase: action for justice and participation in transforming the world fully "appear to us" as a constitutive dimension of preaching the Gospel. What it means is disputed.** *Justice in the World*, introduction (AAS 63 (1971) 924; English §6): the Latin is *ratio constitutiva* (Murphy writes "dimensio"; the AAS text and the International Theological Commission read "ratio"), glossed as the Church's mission to redeem the human race and free it from every oppressive situation (paraphrased).

- **History.** The word passed without debate in 1971 (Murphy 1983). At the 1974 Synod, Bishop Ramón Torrella, who had chaired the 1971 drafting group, said it meant "integral part", not "essential". Juan Alfaro SJ, the 1971 synod's theologian, called that equation an error: justice is essential, though not the only dimension. The ITC, *Human Development and Christian Salvation* (1976), read it as an integral part, not an essential one. The "integral" reading is Torrella's intervention, not a text of the 1974 Synod, which produced no full document (EN 2).
- **What Paul VI settled** ([Evangelii Nuntiandi](#evangelii-nuntiandi)): the order, not the word. EN never uses "constitutive". Murphy reports that Paul VI later praised JW but never cited this passage.
- **The floor.** That evangelization includes the social message is **rung 3** papal teaching (EN 29, 31; CA 5), and would bind without JW.
- **Level.** What "constitutive" means: **rung 5**. Torrella, Alfaro, Murphy and the advisory Commission: **no magisterial level**. EN 27's ordering rests on the revealed truth that salvation is offered in Christ (**rung 1**).

**Principle or application:** the floor is a principle; how much time, money and which cause a parish or chaplaincy gives to justice work is an application (GS 43; OA 50).

- **Trap:** "Vatican II defined that political action is the Gospel's essence" (inflation; the word is a synod's, hedged, under a conditional approval).
- **Trap:** "'constitutive' is only a synod's word, so justice work is optional" (demotes the rung-3 floor).
- **Trap:** "EN defined 'integral'", or "the ITC settled it" (EN never uses the word; the ITC is advisory).

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)

### Evangelii Nuntiandi

**Paul VI's apostolic exhortation on evangelization (8 December 1975): the social message is inside the Gospel, but neither its centre nor its substitute.** Written after the 1974 Synod (the Third General Assembly) handed its work to the pope (EN 2). Paraphrased:

| EN | Content |
|---|---|
| 27 | the clear proclamation of salvation in Jesus Christ is the foundation, centre and summit of evangelization |
| 28 | the sacraments complete evangelization rather than distorting it |
| 29 | evangelization would not be complete without an explicit message about rights and duties, family, social and international life, peace, justice, development and liberation |
| 31 | links of the anthropological, theological and evangelical (charity) orders join evangelization to human advancement |
| 32 | the temptation to reduce the mission to a temporal project |
| 34 | the Church will not confine her mission to the religious field, yet affirms the primacy of her spiritual vocation and will not let human liberation stand in for proclaiming the Kingdom |
| 35 | liberation and salvation linked, never identified |
| 36–38 | structures and conversion; no violence; a social teaching no true Christian can ignore |

- EN never uses "constitutive" ([constitutive dimension](#constitutive-dimension)). The English of *Libertatis Nuntius* V.5 miscalls EN an "Apostolic Constitution"; it is an exhortation. CiV 15 draws on EN 29 and 31.
- **Level.** An apostolic exhortation: EN 27–35 **rung 3**; the ordering in 27 rests on the revealed truth that salvation is offered in Christ (**rung 1**).

**Principle or application:** EN's ordering is principle; a community's justice project is an application.

- **Trap:** reading EN 34 as confining the Church to the religious field (it says the opposite) / reading EN 29 as making liberation the Gospel's centre (EN 27).
- **Trap:** "EN defined the meaning of 'constitutive'."

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)

### Human dignity

**The worth every human being has by what he or she is, made in God's image and willed by God, not by what one does, owns, achieves or is granted: the foundation of every social principle.** [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md) owns it.

- **Ground.** Every human being is made in God's image (Gen 1:26–27; GS 12); the person, body and soul, is one (GS 14).
- **Received, not conferred.** Intrinsic, prior to any recognition, not earned and never lost, whatever one's condition, capacity or conduct ([*Dignitas Infinita*](#dignitas-infinita) 1, 7, 15, 22, 2024; MH 50, 52–53). DI 25: it rests on human nature, not on preference or social recognition, and licenses no arbitrary multiplication of rights.
- **Three social jobs.** It fixes the end of institutions: every social institution starts from the person, has the person as its subject and exists for the person (GS 25; [person as end of institutions](#person-as-end-of-institutions); [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)). It grounds rights prior to the State (PT 9, 60–61; MH 54–55: rights express dignity itself, not something added from outside; [rights grounded on dignity](#rights-grounded-on-dignity)). And it judges conditions without judging persons: calling a condition undignified judges the condition, not the person in it (DI 8; [four senses of dignity](#four-senses-of-dignity)).
- **Uses later.** Work for man, not man for work, and the disabled worker a full subject (LE 6, 9, 22; [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md)); the one picture of the person by which socialism and liberal capitalism are both judged (CA 13; [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)); the ground of religious liberty, known by revelation and by reason (DH 2; [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)). MH 46–58 sets dignity and rights out as the foundations before the five principles.
- **Level.** The image of God: **rung 1** ([`creation-grace-last-things` 2.1](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md)). Indelible ontological dignity: **at least rung 3** (DI, approved in ordinary form; MH 52–53 raises the grade, not the rung). GS 25 and PT 9: **rung 3**. The four senses: an **analytic tool**, the assent going to what they serve. DI's catalogue (33–62): each item keeps the weight of what it restates (direct killing of the innocent: **rung 1**). The personalistic norm: philosophy, **no magisterial level**.

**Principle or application:** principles; every policy that claims to honour dignity is an application. DI 37's reading of new forms of poverty joins an [empirical premise](#empirical-premise) to a [prudential judgment](#prudential-judgment).

- **Trap:** "dignity requires this housing programme" (inflation: the principle does not choose the instrument).
- **Trap:** "dignity is a slogan each side fills as it likes" (demotion; DI 25).
- **Trap:** "an undignified condition makes the person undignified" (DI 8).

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md)

**Module 3: The principles**

### Four senses of dignity

**One word, four senses: only the first is a verdict on what a person is worth, and it is never lost.** *Dignitas Infinita* 7–8 (DDF, 2024) sorts them:

| Sense | What it names | Can it change? |
|---|---|---|
| Ontological | what the person is: willed, created and loved by God | never lost (DI 7, 15) |
| Moral | how she uses her freedom | can in fact be lost (DI 7) |
| Social | the quality of her living conditions | rises and falls with the conditions (DI 8) |
| Existential | how she experiences her own life and worth | rises and falls (DI 8) |

- *Magnifica Humanitas* 52 (2026) restates the four, says the moral, social and existential senses can be enhanced or diminished, and widens social dignity to the concrete respect society gives a person. "Lost" is DI 7's word, not MH's.
- **The social use.** Calling a condition undignified judges the condition, not the person in it (DI 8): condemn the stable, never the cathedral. Wrongdoing touches moral dignity only, which is why DI 7 calls the wrongdoer to conversion and leaves his rights untouched.
- **Level.** The taxonomy is an **analytic tool** with no assent of its own; the assent goes to what it serves, indelible ontological dignity, **at least rung 3** (see [Dignitas Infinita](#dignitas-infinita)). The image of God beneath it is **rung 1**. The taxonomy itself is taught in [`creation-grace-last-things` 2.1](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md); 3.1 uses it.

**Principle or application:** a tool serving a principle (ontological dignity cannot be lost); any claim that conditions must change in a particular way is an application.

- **Trap:** reading "undignified conditions" as a verdict on the people who endure them (DI 8).
- **Trap:** reading lost moral dignity as lost rights (DI 7: ontological dignity is never lost).
- **Trap:** letting social dignity fix a figure. It sets a floor ("subhuman living conditions", GS 27); "four men per room" is an application.

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)

### Dignitas Infinita

**The 2024 declaration on human dignity of the Dicastery for the Doctrine of the Faith (DDF).** Dated 2 April 2024 and approved by Francis in ordinary form, so it stays the Dicastery's act. The sections the course uses:

| DI | What it says (paraphrased) | Used in |
|---|---|---|
| 1, 7, 15, 22 | every person's dignity is intrinsic, prior to any recognition, unearned, never lost, whatever their condition, capacity or conduct | [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md) T2 |
| 2 | praises the Universal Declaration of Human Rights (1948) without taking it as the ground of rights | 3.1 P3 |
| 7–8 | the [four senses of dignity](#four-senses-of-dignity) | 3.1 T5 |
| 25 | dignity rests on human nature, not on preference or social recognition, and licenses no arbitrary multiplication of rights | 3.1 |
| 33–62 | a catalogue of grave violations: extreme poverty, war, trafficking, abortion, surrogacy, euthanasia, digital violence and more; §34 adds GS 27's list and the death penalty | 3.1 T6 |
| 37 | new forms of poverty that old measures miss | 3.1 |
| 38 | legitimate defence reaffirmed | [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| 65 | states must secure the conditions for dignity | 3.1 Ex 2 |

- **Level of the act: rung 3** (a dicastery's declaration approved in ordinary form). What it teaches on equal, indelible dignity: **at least rung 3**. *Magnifica Humanitas* 52–53 (encyclical, Leo XIV, signed 15 May 2026) restates the four senses and quotes DI 1. That raises the **grade** within rung 3 (a heavier genre; repetition is one of LG 25's signs), **not the rung**: nothing was made definitive.
- **The catalogue keeps its items' own weights.** It is one rung-3 act, but each item weighs what it restates: that direct killing of the innocent is always gravely wrong is **rung 1** (*Evangelium Vitae* 57; [`moral-theology` 5.1](../moral-theology/lessons/05-01-the-inviolability-of-innocent-life.md)), while its readings of present trends are applications. DI 37 fuses an [empirical premise](#empirical-premise) with a [prudential judgment](#prudential-judgment).
- DI as theology of the image belongs to [`creation-grace-last-things` 2.1](../creation-grace-last-things/lessons/02-01-the-image-of-god.md); the death penalty item to [`moral-theology` 5.4](../moral-theology/lessons/05-04-the-death-penalty-and-ccc-2267.md).

**Principle or application:** indelible dignity (DI 1, 15, 25) is a principle owed religious submission; the declaration's readings of present conditions (DI 37) are applications and empirical premises, owed serious consideration and evidence.

- **Trap:** "a dicastery's declaration is only an opinion": understates; an approved declaration is a rung-3 act.
- **Trap:** "*Magnifica Humanitas* made indelible dignity definitive": overstates; grade, not rung.
- **Trap:** "every judgment in §§33–62 binds at one level": overstates some items and understates others.

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)

### Person as end of institutions

**Institutions exist for persons, never the reverse: they are judged by what they do to persons.** *Gaudium et Spes* 25 (paraphrased): every social institution starts from the person, has the person as its subject and exists for the person. GS 26: the social order and its development must serve the good of persons; the order of things is subordinate to the order of persons, as the sabbath was made for man. CCC 1881 locates it.

- **Level: rung 3.** Conciliar teaching in the doctrinal part of a pastoral constitution (Vatican II, 7 December 1965); a pastoral constitution carries its principles at their own weight ([`fundamental-theology` 4.5](../fundamental-theology/lessons/04-05-reading-a-documents-weight.md)). The Catechism locates; it adds no weight.
- **In use** (3.1 Ex 1): a rule rationing rehabilitation places by expected years of paid work makes patients serve the economy, the ideology *Magnifica Humanitas* 51 names (a person must earn his worth, the efficient valued more). Rationing by clinical criteria such as likely benefit and urgency ranks needs, not persons, and passes.

**Principle or application:** a principle, owed religious submission; how a given institution should be reshaped is an application.

- **Trap:** "only pastoral": understates the doctrinal part of GS.
- **Trap:** quoting GS 25 as "origin, subject and end": not the vatican.va English. Paraphrase it and cite GS 25.

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)

### Rights grounded on dignity

**Rights come from what a person is, before any state: law recognizes them or violates them; it does not issue them.** *Pacem in Terris* 9 (John XXIII, 11 April 1963; its note cites Pius XII's Christmas radio message of 1942): every human being is a person, with intelligence and free will, and so has rights and duties flowing directly from his nature, universal, inviolable and inalienable.

| Text | What it adds (paraphrased) |
|---|---|
| PT 11 | life, bodily integrity, and the means of a life worthy of a person: food, clothing, shelter, medical care, rest, social services; support when deprived of a livelihood through no fault of one's own |
| PT 15 | the right to found a family |
| PT 25 | citizenship of one state does not remove membership of the human family |
| PT 60–61 | authority's chief task is to recognize and protect rights; a government that refuses fails its duty, and its decrees lack binding force |
| GS 26 | everything necessary for leading a truly human life |
| MH 51, 54–55 | rights belong to everyone by being human; they express dignity itself, not something added from outside; the right to life comes first |
| CCC 1930 | locator: rights prior to society, the basis of an authority's legitimacy |

- *Rerum Novarum* 40 already holds the floor: "no man has in this matter power over himself" (Benziger 1903), so a worker cannot sign away treatment his dignity forbids. 3.1 close-reads that passage.
- **Level: rung 3** (an encyclical of 1963, restated in GS 26 and MH 54–55), a principle owed religious submission.

**Principle or application:** the grounding and the rights PT names are principles; which benefits, how much, by when and at what level of government are applications. "Dignity requires an income of X" is a split item: the right to the means of a worthy life (PT 11; GS 26) is rung 3; the instrument and the figure have no magisterial level.

- **Trap:** "rights are what a political community grants its members": denies the principle (PT 9, 25; CCC 1930).
- **Trap:** "rights language is a secular import the Church has tolerated since 1948": understates. The Church praises the Universal Declaration (DI 2; MH 54) but grounds rights in nature, not in it. The honest history: Leo XIII sorted the "so-called modern liberties" into good and evil elements (*Libertas*, 1888, recalling *Immortale Dei*), yet *Rerum Novarum* already spoke of natural rights to property and association.
- **Trap:** confusing this with the statist–cosmopolitan dispute over distributive justice ([`political-philosophy` 6.1](../political-philosophy/lessons/06-01-global-justice-cosmopolitans-and-statists.md)), whose statists still hold that human rights are owed to everyone.
- **Citation:** MH's note 68 cites this passage as "PT 5" (another numbering); cite PT 9. CCC 1930's note says "PT 65"; the matching vatican.va passage is PT 60–61.

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)

### Legal justice

**Aquinas's name for justice as the virtue that directs every other virtue's acts to the common good; rulers have it first, subjects too.** ST II-II q.58 (English Dominican Province):

- **a.5:** justice is called "general" because it directs the acts of all the virtues to the common good.
- **a.6:** it is a special virtue in essence, with the common good as its proper object, and it is "in the sovereign principally and by way of a mastercraft, while it is secondarily and administratively in his subjects".
- **a.7 ad 2:** the common good and the individual good differ "not only in respect of the *many* and the *few*, but also under a formal aspect", as whole differs from part. This is the classical root of "the common good is not a sum" ([common good](#common-good)).
- **The limit** (I-II q.21 a.4 ad 3): "Man is not ordained to the body politic, according to all that he is and has".
- Particular justice, commutative and distributive, is [`moral-theology` 3.3](../moral-theology/lessons/03-03-the-cardinal-virtues-and-the-gifts.md)'s; law defined by the common good (I-II q.90) is [`history-of-political-thought` 2.3](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-03-aquinas-on-law.md)'s.
- **Level.** Aquinas's account, with its whole-and-part metaphysics, is a theologian's: **at most rung 4** (common teaching of the Thomist schools). Its magisterial counterpart is **rung 3**: everyone must contribute to the common good according to role (GS 75; CCC 1913), a commitment *Magnifica Humanitas* 59 calls non-negotiable for a Christian.

**Principle or application:** the duty to contribute (GS 75) is a principle; Aquinas's analysis is theology beneath it, not a further principle.

- **Trap:** "legal justice is the council's business, not mine": a.6 puts it in subjects too, secondarily.
- **Trap:** "Aquinas taught that the person exists for the state": I-II q.21 a.4 ad 3 says otherwise, and his frame is a theologian's, not doctrine.

*Lessons:* [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md)

### Intermediate bodies

**Everything between the individual and the state: families, associations, unions, parishes, firms, towns and regions.** These are the lesser bodies [subsidiarity](#subsidiarity) protects and helps. The documents' names for them: civil society (*Compendium* 185, the web of relations between individuals and intermediate groupings); "intermediary bodies" (CCC 1894); intermediary organizations and the third sector (*Magnifica Humanitas* 68–70).

- *Caritas in Veritate* 57 (2009): subsidiarity helps the person through the autonomy of intermediate bodies, aiming at their emancipation.
- MH 69 (2026): such bodies must be neither stifled nor replaced nor reduced to "mere facilitators".
- Burke's little platoons ([`history-of-political-thought` 5.3](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/05-03-burke-prescription-against-abstract-rights.md)), Hegel's corporations ([6.1](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/06-01-hegel-freedom-realized-in-the-state.md)) and Tocqueville's associations ([6.3](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/06-03-tocqueville-tyranny-of-the-majority-and-soft-despotism.md); [`social-theory` 6.1](../social-theory/lessons/06-01-tocqueville-on-associations.md)) describe and prize such bodies. Subsidiarity adds a normative rule about what the higher body owes them, grounded in the person.
- **Level.** No level of its own: the term names what subsidiarity, a **rung-3** principle, protects.

**Principle or application:** that these bodies have their own work, which the higher body may neither absorb nor abandon, is principle; which body should run fire services, water or schools is an application.

- **Trap:** taking the Burke, Hegel or Tocqueville resemblance for the doctrine itself.
- **Trap:** help with strings that remake what a body is: conditions securing safety belong to the State's guardian role, conditions that change what the body is turn help into absorption (3.3 Ex 2). Where a given condition falls, the principle does not settle.

*Lessons:* [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)

### Substitute function

**The State may stand in for social sectors too weak to act, but only briefly, and only while they cannot.** *Centesimus Annus* 48 (John Paul II, 1 May 1991): in exceptional circumstances, where social sectors or business systems are too weak or just getting under way, the State may exercise a "substitute function"; such interventions must be as brief as possible, so as not to take permanently from society the functions that are its own. *Compendium* 188: substitution must not last longer than absolutely necessary, its justification lies only in the exceptional situation, and the common good is the criterion.

- **The order of questions** (3.3): can the lesser body do it; if not, can help make it able; only then substitution, and only while needed. The principle fixes the order; it does not answer the questions.
- **Level: rung 3**, as part of the principle: "both halves with substitution kept temporary" (3.3). The *Compendium* has [no magisterial level of its own](#compendium-of-the-social-doctrine).

**Principle or application:** that substitution is exceptional and temporary is principle; whether a case is exceptional, and how long "as brief as possible" is, are prudential judgments.

- **Trap:** substitution as first resort. Help comes first: closing the Stavenby schools that a refit grant could save skips the help half (3.3 Ex 2).
- **Trap:** substitution as permanent (CA 48; *Compendium* 188; MH 69, in 3.5's Flashback).

*Lessons:* [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)

### Social Assistance State

**The name, reported in CA 48 from the welfare state's critics, for a welfare state whose excesses replace society's own responsibility.** *Centesimus Annus* 48 (1991): the welfare state arose to relieve poverty and deprivation unworthy of the person; its excesses and abuses drew harsh criticism, and critics dubbed it the "Social Assistance State". Its malfunctions come from misunderstanding the State's tasks. Subsidiarity corrects them: the higher community supports the lower one in need instead of absorbing its functions. The same section gives the State real duties, and *Magnifica Humanitas* 69 adds that subsidiarity never licenses the State to disengage. 4.4 owns the critique; 3.3 points to it.

- **Level.** CA 48 is an encyclical (**rung 3**); the principle at work is subsidiarity (**rung 3**). CA 48's claim that needs are best met by those closest to them is offered as how things appear ("it would appear"): an **empirical premise** (3.3; 4.4).
- **Asked economically**, the same question is crowding out ([`public-economics` 1.2](../public-economics/lessons/01-02-voluntary-provision-and-crowding-out.md)) and the design of social insurance ([`public-economics` 7.1](../public-economics/lessons/07-01-adverse-selection-as-a-policy-problem.md)–[7.3](../public-economics/lessons/07-03-tagging-ordeals-and-in-kind-benefits.md)).

**Principle or application:** subsidiarity is the principle; any judgment that a given welfare state has become a Social Assistance State is an application (4.4).

- **Trap:** "CA 48 condemns the welfare state": it criticizes excesses and abuses, grants that the welfare state answered poverty beneath human dignity, blames the malfunctions on a misunderstanding of the State's tasks, and assigns the State duties in the same section.
- **Trap:** "CA 48 coined the name": it reports the label critics gave the welfare state.
- **Trap:** Tocqueville's "tutelary" power is a family resemblance, not this doctrine ([`political-philosophy` 6.4](../political-philosophy/lessons/06-04-welfare-property-and-subsidiarity.md)).

*Lessons:* [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md)

### EU subsidiarity

**The European Union's treaty rule on when the Union, not the member states, acts: a secular borrowing of the word, not the Catholic principle.** Treaty on European Union art. 5(3) (paraphrased): outside its exclusive competences, the Union acts only where member states cannot sufficiently achieve an aim and the Union, by reason of scale or effects, can do better.

- **What it lacks** (3.3): it has two tiers only, no duty of help, and no ground in the person. The Catholic principle governs every higher and lower body, binds inside a unitary state and prescribes no constitution.
- **Level: no magisterial level.** It is secular law.

**Principle or application:** neither; it is law, not Church teaching, and settles nothing about the Catholic principle.

- **Trap:** treating the two as one idea ("the same idea as the EU's treaty rule"): it misstates the source (3.3 P2).
- **Trap:** inferring from the EU rule that subsidiarity is about federal design. The economic version of the question is Oates's decentralization theorem ([`public-economics` 8.2](../public-economics/lessons/08-02-assignment-spillovers-and-grants.md)), which measures welfare, not the agency of the bodies.

*Lessons:* [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)

### Interdependence

**The fact that persons and peoples depend on one another in every sphere of life; once recognized, it makes a claim, and the answer it calls for is solidarity.** *Sollicitudo Rei Socialis* 38 (John Paul II, dated 30 December 1987): interdependence, sensed as a system shaping every sphere of life, is to be accepted as a "moral category"; the fitting response, a moral and social attitude that is a virtue, is [solidarity](#solidarity).

- **The picture** (3.4): on a rope team the rope is the interdependence; solidarity is each climber's settled decision to climb as a team. A bare fact obliges nobody: you can depend on people and still exploit them.
- **Durkheim's "organic solidarity"** ([`social-theory` 3.1](../social-theory/lessons/03-01-the-division-of-labor-and-social-solidarity.md)) describes the same fact sociologically, with no moral claim.
- **Level.** That recognized interdependence is a moral category calling for solidarity is **rung 3** (SRS 38, an encyclical). That interdependence is *growing* is an **empirical premise** (SRS 38; *Magnifica Humanitas* 74), and so is GS 26's observation that it grows daily ([3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md)).

**Principle or application:** the moral category is a principle; the growth claim is an empirical premise, revisable on evidence.

- **Trap:** reading Durkheim's descriptive term and SRS's moral category as one claim.
- **Trap:** treating the growth claim as doctrine, or the doctrine as hostage to it: the duty does not wait on the trend.

*Lessons:* [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md)

### Structures of sin

**Personal sins hardened into customs, incentives and institutions that make the next sin easier: sin only by analogy, and every link in them is a person.** The category is [`moral-theology` 3.5](../moral-theology/lessons/03-05-social-sin-and-cooperation-in-the-sins-of-others.md)'s, copied here and not re-levelled. This course applies it:

- **SRS 36** (1987): structures of sin are rooted in personal sin and linked to the concrete acts of those who introduce, consolidate and entrench them; they grow, spread and become sources of further sins.
- **SRS 37:** two attitudes typically build them, the "all-consuming desire for profit" and the "thirst for power", each pursued "at any price".
- **SRS 38:** only the opposite attitude, solidarity, overcomes them, with the help of grace. *Compendium* 193 (a locator) calls the remedies "structures of solidarity".
- **3.4 Ex 1, the Ebbsford call-on:** a casual-hiring system with an unwritten injury blacklist, renewed by managers who know of it and signed off by an inspector who never boards. No single villain built it, but every link is a person.
- **3.6:** Puebla's "social sin" and *Magnifica Humanitas* 79 apply the category to the gap between rich and poor; *Libertatis Nuntius* XI.8 asks radical reform of structures that are themselves forms of violence.
- **6.2:** a world of blocs and imperialisms is subject to structures of sin, rooted in persons' acts (SRS 20–22, 36). **6.4:** the progressive reading builds its structural critique on SRS 36.
- **Level: rung 3**, as `moral-theology` 3.5 fixed it.

**Principle or application:** that structures of sin exist, are rooted in personal acts and must be overcome is a principle; SRS's 1987 verdict on the blocs, and any particular remedy (say, a guaranteed-shift labour pool), are applications.

- **Trap:** blaming "the system" without naming persons, the usage *Reconciliatio et Paenitentia* 16 rejects (`moral-theology` 3.5).
