## Arguments

The skeletons the lessons build, one premise a line, in course order. A premise is tagged *(principle)*, *(empirical premise)* or *(application)* where the tag does work, and *Levels* gives the lesson's own marks; the assent each kind asks is [the three assents](#the-three-assents). Where a Boss part asks you to produce a reconstruction, the entry is an **outline**: it names the parts the lesson teaches and stops, and the numbered premises are yours. Post-1930 documents are paraphrased.

**Module 1: What the Church's social doctrine is**

### Argument: Competence without a blueprint

Why the Church judges social arrangements, and why she will not design them.

1. **P1.** The Church is charged to interpret the whole moral law, natural law included (*Humanae Vitae* 4; argued in [`moral-theology` 1.3](../moral-theology/lessons/01-03-natural-law-in-light-of-revelation.md)). *(principle)*
2. **P2.** Economic, social and political arrangements touch persons' fundamental rights and the salvation of souls.
3. **C1.** So she may teach a social doctrine and pass moral judgment on public matters when rights or souls require it (GS 76; CCC 2420). Leo XIII had claimed it as a right, the Church necessary to a solution, never sufficient (RN 16). See [competence in the moral order](#competence-in-the-moral-order).
4. **P3.** Technical solutions, models and systems are not moral law: workable models arise in history from those who face the problems (CA 43); the Church has no technical solutions and is no "third way" (SRS 41; CiV 9).
5. **C2.** She disclaims the blueprint, not the judgment ([the disclaimers](#the-doctrines-disclaimers)). Pastors teach the principles; the concrete ordering of society is properly the laity's, who may not claim the Church's authority for their own solution (GS 43; DCE 29).

*Levels:* as 1.1's Mark the levels gives them; the competence over the natural law is **at least rung 3** (`moral-theology` 1.3). Boss 1(a) asks you to level the disclaimers yourself.

*Lessons:* [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md)

### Argument: What an application inherits

The working rule of the whole course: a principle passes its direction down to an application, not its weight.

1. **P1.** A permanent principle holds whatever the facts. Test: would the sentence still hold if the facts it mentions changed? ([the standing question](#the-standing-question); *Compendium* 80's rule that weight follows independence from contingent elements)
2. **P2.** An empirical premise says how the world is; it carries no doctrinal rung and stands or falls with the evidence (CA 3; LS 188).
3. **P3.** An application joins a principle to empirical premises in a [prudential judgment](#prudential-judgment) about here and now; it is only as sound as its principles and its information (EJA 134–135).
4. **C1.** The rung belongs to the document. Religious submission attaches to its principles; its applications ask serious, respectful consideration, with loyalty the rule (DV 24); its premises answer to evidence.
5. **C2.** After weighing, a Catholic may judge an application otherwise without dissenting (GS 43). He may not deny the principle, treat the application as weightless, or claim the Church's authority for his own.
6. **C3.** Two errors follow from breaking the rule ([the two symmetrical errors](#the-two-symmetrical-errors)): an application inflated into a principle ("the Church teaches this figure"), a principle demoted to a preference ("the living wage is one opinion").

*Levels:* CA 3 and GS note 1 **rung 3**; DV 24 **rung 3 at a light grade**; GS 43 and EG 182 **rung 3**; conference letters **particular teaching**; how MH 27 and 46 weigh in a hard case **rung 5**. Nothing here is defined.

*Lessons:* [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md)

### Argument: The instability objection and the reply

Does an inductive method make the teaching follow the data? The objection and the documents' answer; the verdict is left open.

1. **O1.** See-judge-act starts from facts (SEE), and facts shift.
2. **OC.** So a teaching built this way shifts with them ([the instability objection](#the-instability-objection)).
3. **R1.** Facts enter at SEE and are judged at JUDGE by principles that do not come from the data (GS 4: the signs of the times read by the Gospel; MH 22).
4. **R2.** New evidence can revise an application at ACT, never the principle beside it (CA 3: the analysis of events claims no definitive force; the principles engage the teaching authority).
5. **R3.** The teaching is constant in its basic principles and criteria, ever new in adapting them (SRS 3); there are not two social doctrines, before and after the Council (CiV 12).
6. **RC.** Stable principles, revisable judgments. Chenu pressed the opposite worry (1979): "social doctrine" as a deductive system had worked as an ideology, and he welcomed the inductive turn of OA 4.

*Levels:* the method itself is **a method commended, with no doctrinal rung** (MM 236); reading the signs of the times **rung 3**; Chenu's thesis **no magisterial level**; whether the reply settles the objection **rung 5** (Boss 1(d) asks for your verdict).

*Lessons:* [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md)

### Argument: Owning and using (ST II-II q.66 a.2)

Aquinas's two relations to external goods, the map the modern corpus starts from.

1. **P1.** Two things belong to man regarding exterior things: the power to procure and dispense them, and their use ([procuring, dispensing and use](#procuring-dispensing-and-use)).
2. **P2.** As to procuring and dispensing, private possession is lawful and "necessary to human life", for three reasons: each takes more care of what is his own, affairs run in better order, and peace is surer when each is content with his own. *(empirical premises)*
3. **P3.** As to use, man ought to possess external things "not as his own, but as common", ready to share them with others in their need (warranted by 1 Tim 6:18).
4. **P4.** The division of possessions is "not contrary to the natural law, but an addition thereto devised by human reason" (ad 1).
5. **C.** Ownership is lawful and use is common. Giving from surplus, and to one in extreme need, is a precept (q.32 a.5); only in urgent need with no other remedy is taking not theft ([extreme necessity](#extreme-necessity), q.66 a.7, its conditions taught in [`history-of-political-thought` 2.4](../history-of-political-thought/lessons/02-04-aquinas-on-kingship-tyranny-and-property.md)).

*The seam:* P4 against *Rerum Novarum* 6's "by nature" is the natural-right tension. Reconciling them, usually through *ius gentium* (q.57 a.3), is the theologians' work (**rung 4**), done in 3.5. *Gaudium et Spes* 69's reception of a.7 is Boss 1(b)'s close reading and is not set out here.

*Levels:* Aquinas's articles **no magisterial level** as his, common teaching (**rung 4**), **rung 3** where RN 22 and GS 69 restate them.

*Lessons:* [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)

**Module 2: The tradition takes shape, 1891–1971**

### Argument: Rerum Novarum against the socialist remedy

**Outline only (Boss 2(b)).** The parts 2.1 names, in the encyclical's order (Benziger 1903; vatican.va numbering): the harm to the worker (§§4–5); the injustice against a natural right (§6); the right's grounds in reason and foresight (§§6–7), in labour (§§9–10) and in the family (§§12–13); the rejection (§15). §15's forecast about the sources of wealth is an *empirical premise*. The numbered reconstruction, the 1890s democratic socialist's statement (Erfurt Programme or *Fabian Essays*) and the reply are Boss 2(b)'s.

*Levels:* under [private property](#private-property) and [critique of socialism](#critique-of-socialism); nothing in RN is defined. Never write that RN's labour argument is Locke's, or that RN repeats Aquinas.

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Argument: Rerum Novarum on family property, the wage and alms

**Outline only (Boss 2(a)).** The three passages 2.1 and 4.2 read: §13, the head of a family's right of property; §45, the wage floor beneath free agreement ([just wage](#just-wage)); §22, what almsgiving from surplus is a duty of. Which premise carries the appeal to nature and which the appeal to the family, and which words put the sufficient living under justice rather than charity, are the Boss's to find.

*Levels:* under [just wage](#just-wage) and [private property](#private-property). The boards of §45 and the Catholic societies of §54 are *applications* by Leo's own markers ("advisable"; "Under these circumstances"; §57's "a general and lasting law" against §56's "circumstances of fact and of time").

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)

### Argument: The duty of the State and its limit (RN 32-37)

**Outline only (Boss 3(c)).** The parts 2.1 teaches: "the State" means the State "as rightly apprehended", no form of government (§32); it serves the common good with distributive justice to every class (§33); it must not absorb individual or family (§35); it steps in when a class suffers harm that can be met no other way, and the law must go no further than the remedy (§36); the poor have a special claim on it (§37). Whether a given scheme is the intervention §37 contemplates, and which words decide it, is Boss 3(c)'s.

*Levels:* the protective duty and its limit **rung 3**; how far a given law goes is the legislator's [prudential judgment](#prudential-judgment).

*Lessons:* [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)

### Argument: Subsidiarity from the purpose of social activity (QA 79-80)

Why taking over what a smaller body can do is an injustice, not just inefficiency.

1. **P1.** Changed conditions now need large associations for many tasks that small ones once did (QA 79's concession). *(empirical premise)*
2. **P2.** It is gravely wrong to take from individuals what they can do by their own initiative and hand it to the community.
3. **P3.** Every social activity by its nature ought to help the members of the social body (*subsidium afferre*), never destroy and absorb them. *(principle; its ground is the person able to act and give, CiV 57; CCC 1884)*
4. **C1.** So it is an injustice, and a grave harm to right order, to assign to a higher association what lesser ones can do; leaving them their work frees the State for what it alone can do (QA 79–80, "subsidiary function").
5. **P4.** The higher body must also support the lower in need and coordinate it toward the common good; where social sectors are too weak it may stand in, as briefly as possible (CA 48; the [substitute function](#substitute-function)).
6. **C2.** [Subsidiarity](#subsidiarity) has two halves, restraint and help, owed by the same body; it governs every higher and lower body and prescribes no constitution.

*Where it stops:* whether this service belongs to this level is an *application*; the crux between subsidiarity and [solidarity](#solidarity) in a hard case is Boss 3(b)'s.

*Levels:* the principle, both halves with substitution kept temporary, **rung 3** (QA's "fixed and unchangeable" raises the grade, not the rung); MH 71's platforms as the highest level an *application*.

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md)

### Argument: Why socialism cannot be reconciled (QA 117-120; CA 13)

The objection is to socialism's picture of society and of man, which no softened program cures.

1. **P1.** Communism seeks class war and the end of private ownership by any means (QA 112).
2. **P2.** Moderate socialism's just demands, even reserving dangerously powerful kinds of property to the State, are not peculiar to socialism (QA 113–115).
3. **P3.** Socialism as doctrine, historical fact or movement, while it remains [truly socialism](#critique-of-socialism), holds that society exists for material advantage alone (QA 117–119).
4. **C1.** That conception cannot be reconciled with Catholic teaching: no one can be at once a good Catholic and a true socialist (QA 120).
5. **P4.** CA 13 names the root: an anthropological error that makes the person a "molecule" of the social organism, cancels the [subjectivity of society](#subjectivity-of-society), and has atheism as its first cause.
6. **C2.** The judgment is on socialism as doctrine and system. Whether a given party or movement "remains truly socialism" is an *application* (PT 159–160; OA 31).

*Levels:* QA 117–120 **rung 3 at a heavy grade**, not a definition (QA 121's solemn tone is LG 25's manner of speaking); CA 13 **rung 3**; DR 58's "intrinsically wrong" **rung 3**, its 1937 collaboration ban a *directive* PT 160 returned to prudence.

*Lessons:* [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Argument: From the moral order to a world authority (PT 132-141)

A world problem needs an authority equal to it, built by consent and serving the nations.

1. **P1.** Public authority exists for the common good and must be able to secure it (PT 136).
2. **P2.** The human family is one, so it has a [universal common good](#universal-common-good) (PT 132).
3. **P3.** States acting by treaty are now unequal to it (PT 134–135). *(empirical premise)*
4. **C1.** So the moral order itself demands some public authority with world-wide reach (PT 137; [international authority](#international-authority)).
5. **P4.** It must be set up by the consent of all nations, never by force (138), aim at personal rights (139), and be bound by subsidiarity, creating conditions for states rather than absorbing their functions (140–141).
6. **C2.** Which body, what powers and what treaty are *applications* (PT 145's hope for the UN; CiV 67's design; FT 172–173's legal limits against capture).

*Levels:* the need for a world-competent authority on PT 138–141's conditions **rung 3**; every design an application.

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)

### Argument: The error, the person and the movement (PT 158-160)

How the corpus keeps a condemnation and still works with people.

1. **P1.** Error must be distinguished from the person who errs, who keeps his dignity (PT 158).
2. **P2.** A false philosophy's formula is fixed; the historical undertakings it inspired change and may contain good elements (PT 159).
3. **C.** The doctrine stays condemned; whether the moment for cooperation has come, with whom and how far, is for prudence, chiefly of the lay leaders, under natural law, the social teaching and Church authority's directives (PT 160; MM 239's [practical directive](#practical-directive)).

*Example:* DR 58's 1937 ban on all collaboration with communism was such a directive; PT 160 hands the question back to prudence, while DR 58's judgment that communism is intrinsically wrong stands. OA 31 applies the same distinction to socialist currents (aspiration, movement, ideology).

*Levels:* PT 158–159 **rung 3**; each decision to cooperate an *application*.

*Lessons:* [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Argument: The social message inside the Gospel (EN 27-35)

Against both noticeboard posters of 2.4: justice is inside evangelization, neither its centre nor its substitute.

1. **P1.** The proclamation of salvation in Jesus Christ is the foundation, centre and summit of evangelization (EN 27).
2. **P2.** Evangelization would not be complete without an explicit message about rights and duties, social life, justice, development and liberation (EN 29).
3. **P3.** Links of the anthropological, theological and evangelical orders join it to human advancement (EN 31).
4. **P4.** The Church affirms the primacy of her spiritual vocation and will not let programs of liberation stand in for proclaiming the kingdom (EN 34); she links liberation and salvation, never identifies them (EN 35).
5. **C.** Working for justice belongs to preaching the Gospel without becoming its essence. *Justice in the World*'s hedged "constitutive dimension" (a synod's sentence under conditional approval) is read inside this floor; whether it means "integral" or "essential" is open ([constitutive dimension](#constitutive-dimension)).

*Levels:* EN (apostolic exhortation) **rung 3**; JW **no magisterial level of its own**, at most rung 3 lightest for what it restates; "integral or essential" **rung 5**. EN never uses the word "constitutive".

*Lessons:* [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md)

**Module 3: The principles**

### Argument: Rights from dignity, before the state

The state recognizes rights; it does not issue them.

1. **P1.** Every human being is made in God's image (Gen 1:26–27; GS 12).
2. **P2.** So each has a dignity that is intrinsic, prior to any recognition, not earned and never lost (DI 1, 7, 15; MH 52–53; [human dignity](#human-dignity)).
3. **P3.** No one can sign it away: "no man has in this matter power over himself" (RN 40, Benziger 1903), so a freely accepted contract does not cure treatment that defeats a person's end.
4. **P4.** Each human being is a person, with intelligence and free will, and so has rights and duties flowing directly from his nature, universal, inviolable and inalienable (PT 9).
5. **C1.** Authority's chief task is to recognize and protect those rights; a government that refuses fails in its duty, and its decrees lack binding force (PT 60–61; [rights grounded on dignity](#rights-grounded-on-dignity)).
6. **C2.** Every institution starts from, is carried by and exists for the person (GS 25; [person as end of institutions](#person-as-end-of-institutions)).

*Where it stops:* dignity fixes the end and rules some means out on any evidence; it does not pick the housing programme. The [four senses](#four-senses-of-dignity) are an analytic tool: only ontological dignity grounds rights.

*Levels:* P1 **rung 1**; P2 **at least rung 3** (a DDF declaration approved in ordinary form; MH restates it, raising the grade within rung 3); P3–C2 **rung 3**.

*Lessons:* [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md)

### Argument: The common good is not a sum

Why neither adding up welfare nor counting votes reaches the common good.

1. **P1.** The common good of the realm and the particular good of the individual differ "not only in respect of the *many* and the *few*, but also under a formal aspect", as whole differs from part (ST II-II q.58 a.7 ad 2).
2. **P2.** So no adding of private goods reaches it: it is indivisible and reached only together (*Compendium* 164), a "plus" (MH 61). The definition sums *conditions* of social life, not goods (GS 26).
3. **P3.** Every citizen has a right to share in it, and authority must promote it for all, favouring no individual or category (PT 56).
4. **P4.** It is defined by reference to the person: the order of things serves the order of persons (GS 26; CCC 1912); man is not ordained to the body politic in all that he is (I-II q.21 a.4 ad 3).
5. **C1.** The [common good](#common-good) is not the sum of private goods (P1–P2), not the majority's good (P3), not the collective's good over persons (P4).
6. **C2.** The political community exists for it and authority is legitimated by serving it, as a moral force (GS 74; [political authority](#political-authority)); because the human family is one, it widens to a universal common good (PT 132).

*Levels:* T1–T5 **rung 3 at its heaviest grade**; that no innocent may be directly killed for the community's sake **rung 1** (EV 57); Aquinas's whole-and-part metaphysics **at most rung 4**; Maritain and Mounier **no magisterial level**.

*Lessons:* [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md)

### Argument: Solidarity from interdependence (SRS 38-40)

How a fact becomes a duty, and why the duty is a virtue rather than a feeling.

1. **P1.** Interdependence, felt as a system shaping every sphere of life, is a fact. *(empirical premise: that it grows, SRS 38; MH 74)*
2. **P2.** Accepted as a moral category, it calls for a fitting response (SRS 38).
3. **P3.** That response is not vague compassion but a "firm and persevering determination" to commit oneself to the common good, of all and of each, because all are really responsible for all (SRS 38, paraphrased).
4. **C1.** [Solidarity](#solidarity) is a virtue owed by all to all. SRS 39 assigns the duties: the stronger take responsibility for the weaker; the weaker claim their rights and contribute; intermediate groups do not press their interests against others'.
5. **P4.** [Structures of sin](#structures-of-sin), built by the desire for profit and the thirst for power "at any price", are overcome only by the opposite attitude, with grace (SRS 36–38).
6. **C2.** Solidarity and subsidiarity correct each other: without solidarity, social privatism; without subsidiarity, paternalist assistance (CiV 58; [the pairing](#subsidiarity-and-solidarity)).

*Where it stops:* SRS 40's Trinity is a horizon that inspires, not a constitution. Every aid target or tariff offered "in solidarity" is an *application*.

*Levels:* SRS 38–39, CA 10 and 15, CiV 58, MH 73–76 **rung 3**, repetition raising the grade; the love of neighbour beneath it is proposed as revealed.

*Lessons:* [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md)

### Argument: The ordering of property

The end governs the means and its limits; each error cuts one of the two.

1. **P1.** God destined the earth and all it contains for the use of every person and people ([universal destination of goods](#universal-destination-of-goods); GS 69; LE 19 ranks it the first principle of the social order).
2. **P2.** Private ownership is legitimate, a right every man has "by nature" (RN 6; CA 30), securing the autonomy of person and family (GS 71). *(Aquinas's three reasons beneath it are empirical premises.)*
3. **P3.** Property is subordinate to the destination, never the reverse (LE 14; CCC 2403; FT 120's [secondary natural right](#secondary-natural-right); MH 66).
4. **P4.** As to managing, goods are rightly private; as to use, the owner holds them as common (q.66 a.2, quoted in RN 22): the [twofold character](#twofold-character-of-ownership) (QA 45) and the [social mortgage](#social-mortgage) (SRS 42).
5. **C1.** The limits: extreme necessity (GS 69); surplus owed to the poor (RN 22; QA 50); regulation of use by public authority (QA 49; GS 71); [expropriation](#expropriation) of idle or poorly used estates where the common good requires it, with equitable compensation (GS 71; PP 24); and misuse or non-use does not by itself destroy the right (QA 47).
6. **C2.** Two errors are forbidden: abolishing private ownership (collectivism, LE 14) and an absolute right of use against the needy (individualism, PP 23): QA 46's "twin rocks of shipwreck".

*The natural-right tension:* RN's "by nature" against Aquinas's "addition devised by human reason" is reconciled by the theologians, not by an act (**rung 4**); whether FT 120's "secondary" changes RN is **rung 5**. The floor for every reading: property is a natural right **and** subordinate.

*Levels:* P1–C2 **rung 3**; MH 66 calls the social function Church doctrine (LG 25's manner of speaking); every land tax or compensation formula an *application*.

*Lessons:* [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md)

### Argument: What the 1984 and 1986 instructions answer

**Outline only (Boss 3(d)).** The parts 3.6 teaches. What *Libertatis Nuntius* affirmed: the Gospel liberates, first from sin (Introduction); the yearning for liberation is a sign of the times (I.1); the rich–poor gap is a scandal (I.6); effective action needs a scientific grasp of poverty's causes (VII.3); Scripture has a political dimension (X.5); structures that are violence need radical reform (XI.8). What it criticized in "certain forms": Marxist concepts borrowed uncritically (VI.10, VII.6–9); class struggle as history's law (VIII.5–8, IX.2, IX.7); salvation reduced to earthly liberation (VI.4, IX.4–5); a class "Church of the people" (IX.11–13); a political rereading of the Exodus and of the poor (IX.10, X.5, X.14). What *Libertatis Conscientia* added: love of preference (68), base communities (69), salvific liberation first (71), unions without class struggle (77), reform over revolution (78), armed struggle only against obvious, prolonged tyranny (79). The two horns of the objection (Gutiérrez, 1971; Novak, 1986) and the reply are Boss 3(d)'s.

*Levels:* LN and LC **rung 3 at a light grade** (CDF instructions approved in ordinary form); the [preferential option](#preferential-option-for-the-poor) itself **rung 3** through SRS 42 and CA 57. See [liberation theology](#liberation-theology).

*Lessons:* [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md)

**Module 4: Work, the economy and the two critiques**

### Argument: The priority of labour over capital

**Outline only (Boss 4(a)).** The sections 4.1 teaches, in order: the objective and subjective dimensions of work (LE 5–6); [economism](#economism), labour valued as merchandise (LE 7, 13); labour and capital in the production process (LE 12); ownership of the means of production and its title (LE 14); the "postulate" and the personalist argument (LE 15). Which premise carries the subjective dimension, and which blocks the move from priority to a claim about who must own, are Boss 4(a)'s.

*Levels:* the principle **rung 3**; LE 14's co-ownership and shareholding proposals *applications* whose feasibility LE leaves open ([priority of labour over capital](#priority-of-labour-over-capital); [worker participation](#worker-participation)).

*Lessons:* [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md)

### Argument: The just wage, floor first

The wage is judged by justice, not by the bargain alone, and above the floor by three measures.

1. **P1.** For most people the wage is the practical access to goods destined for all, so a just wage is the key test of a whole system's justice (LE 19; MH 37).
2. **P2.** Free agreement is the rule but does not settle justice: beneath it stands a floor, a frugal and well-behaved worker's living (RN 45; how §45 argues it is Boss 2(a)'s). Pay is left neither to the market nor to the stronger (MM 71).
3. **P3.** Above the floor no single rule decides (QA 66–68). The [three measures](#three-measures-of-the-wage): the worker's and his family's support (QA 71); the firm's condition, where inertia is no excuse (QA 72); the common good, including jobs for others (QA 74); GS 67 adds role and productivity.
4. **P4.** The family measure has two routes: a [family wage](#family-wage) or [family allowances](#family-allowance) matched to dependants (LE 19; CA 15).
5. **P5.** [Commutative justice](#commutative-justice) binds this employer to this worker; [social justice](#social-justice) binds the system, and a lone employer under competition must support the institutions that make fair pay possible (DR 51–53); the [indirect employer](#indirect-employer) shapes the terms without absolving the direct one (LE 17).
6. **C.** The employer owes his share, the system the rest. The route that closes a family's gap, partnership forms (QA 65; LE 14) and every figure are *applications*; whether a floor costs jobs is an *empirical premise*.

*Levels:* T1–T5 and the wage contract's legitimacy **rung 3, heavy grade** by frequency; withholding wages owed **at least rung 3** (Deut 24:14–15; Jas 5:4); every figure **no rung**. Boss 4(b)'s case arithmetic is yours.

*Lessons:* [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md)

### Argument: The two critiques meet in Centesimus Annus 42

Each critique hits the other system only where it is made absolute; what each leaves standing meets in CA 42.

1. **P1.** Socialism's fundamental error is anthropological: the person reduced to a molecule of the social organism (CA 13; [critique of socialism](#critique-of-socialism)).
2. **P2.** Liberal capitalism's error is to let free competition direct economic life by itself (QA 88), an "unbridled liberalism" of profit, competition and absolute ownership (PP 26), and an [idolatry of the market](#idolatry-of-the-market) that forgets the needs and goods no market serves (CA 34, 40; [critique of liberal capitalism](#critique-of-liberal-capitalism)).
3. **P3.** What the first critique leaves standing: social provision, solidarity, some public ownership (QA 114). What the second leaves standing: markets, property, enterprise, profit (CA 34–35).
4. **C1.** [CA 42](#centesimus-annus-42) answers its own question, whether capitalism is the victorious system, the goal for the countries rebuilding, and the model for the Third World, in two senses. If "capitalism" means an economy recognizing the positive role of business, the market, private property and responsibility for the means of production, and free human creativity in the economy: yes, though "business economy", "market economy" or "free economy" are better names. If it means economic freedom not circumscribed within a strong juridical framework that places it at the service of human freedom in its totality, a freedom whose core is ethical and religious: no. It warns against a "radical capitalistic ideology" (paraphrased).
5. **P4.** The Church has no model to present and is no "third way"; she proposes and prefers no system (CA 43; SRS 41).
6. **C2.** Yes to markets inside a legal and moral frame; no to economic freedom as an absolute; no system blessed. MH 39 (2026) reads CA 42 the same way.

*Where it stops:* the diagnosis of a "blessed the free market" editorial is Boss 4(c)'s; whether the doctrine rules out one live system is Boss 4(d)'s.

*Levels:* RN 6 and 15, QA 88, PP 26, CA 13, 40, 42 (read whole), 43, SRS 41 **rung 3**; EG 54's trickle-down verdict an *empirical premise*, no rung.

*Lessons:* [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md)

### Argument: Gift inside the economy (CiV 34-39)

Gift is not a weekend hobby: it runs through contract and politics as well as civil society.

1. **P1.** The person is made for gift; gift neither replaces justice nor sits beside it as an add-on (CiV 34).
2. **P2.** Friendship, solidarity and reciprocity belong within economic activity itself, not only outside it or after the money is made (CiV 36).
3. **P3.** Economic life needs three logics: contractual exchange, just laws with redistribution, and works of gift (CiV 37; [the three logics](#economy-of-gift)).
4. **P4.** If market and State each keep a monopoly over their own area, society is corroded, and no law can create gratuitousness (CiV 39).
5. **C.** Gift belongs inside firms and markets as well as in civil society; firms with different institutional ends should compete on equal terms, so hybrid forms can emerge (CiV 38, 46; [civil economy](#civil-economy); [stakeholders](#stakeholders), CiV 40).

*Where it stops:* CiV's praise of the economy of communion binds no Catholic owner to adopt it (*application*); dismissing the gift chapter as decoration demotes a rung-3 principle.

*Levels:* T1–T5 and the duty to stakeholders **rung 3**; CA 35's efficiency claim an *empirical premise* whatever its setting.

*Lessons:* [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md)

### Argument: Someone must answer (MH 103-107)

A machine can sort people; only a person can answer for the sorting.

1. **P1.** Technology is neither hostile nor evil in itself, but in practice never neutral: it takes on the aims of those who design, fund and use it (MH 4, 9).
2. **P2.** A system builds choices into what it counts, leaves out and optimizes for; if it treats some lives as less worthy, a criterion contrary to dignity is built in (MH 104).
3. **P3.** Only persons bear moral responsibility; when no one bears an algorithm's judgment, exclusion hides behind a look of objectivity (MH 103).
4. **C1.** Discernment must judge design, not only use ([AI is not morally neutral](#ai-is-not-morally-neutral)), and responsibility must be assigned at every stage from design to use (MH 105; [responsibility at every stage](#responsibility-at-every-stage)).
5. **P4.** "Alignment" is not enough when a few choose the values aligned to (MH 107).
6. **C2.** Law, oversight and contestable decisions are the *applications* (MH 106, 164); the claims about present AI are *empirical premises* MH 98 itself dates ([the MH 98 caveat](#the-mh-98-caveat)).

*Levels:* the principles of T2, T3, T5, T6 **rung 3** (an encyclical); *Antiqua et Nova* a dicastery note approved in ordinary form, never "papal teaching".

*Lessons:* [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md)

**Module 5: The political community, peace and the stranger**

### Argument: Authority, its end and its limit

Authority's end fixes its limit; the Church stands beside the line, not on the throne.

1. **P1.** No society holds together without someone directing all to the common good, so authority comes from nature and has God for its author (*Immortale Dei* 3; *Diuturnum* 11; GS 74).
2. **P2.** The people may designate the ruler and choose the form; designation does not confer the right to rule, and no form is prescribed (*Diuturnum* 6–7; *Immortale Dei* 4; GS 74; [forms of government](#forms-of-government)).
3. **P3.** Authority binds in conscience when it acts within the moral order for the common good, as a moral force (GS 74; PT 47–48), and only by morally licit means (CCC 1903): no reason of state licenses an evil means.
4. **C1.** A law against the moral order does not bind (PT 51); against divine law, in Leo XIII's words, "to resist becomes a positive duty, to obey, a crime" (*Sapientiae Christianae* 10, Benziger 1903); CCC 2242 obliges refusal ([limits of obedience](#limits-of-obedience)).
5. **P4.** Two brakes: citizens must still give what the common good objectively requires (GS 74; CCC 2242); [armed resistance](#armed-resistance) needs all five conditions of CCC 2243.
6. **C2.** Democracy is valued "inasmuch as" it secures participation and the peaceful replacement of rulers, on a correct conception of the person (CA 46; [democracy and truth](#democracy-and-truth)); it is not the only legitimate regime.

*Levels:* T1–T7's principles **rung 3**; that no form is prescribed is itself rung 3; whether CCC 2243's conditions hold in a case is an *application* resting partly on forecasts.

*Lessons:* [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md)

### Argument: The ground of religious liberty (DH 2-3, 10)

The duty to seek the truth is the reason for the freedom, not its rival.

1. **P1.** Persons are bound to seek the truth, above all about God, and to adhere to it when known (DH 2–3).
2. **P2.** They can meet that duty in a way befitting persons only when free from external coercion (DH 2).
3. **P3.** The act of faith is free by its nature, so no one may be forced to embrace the faith (DH 10).
4. **C1.** So every person has a right to immunity from coercion in religious matters, privately and publicly, within due limits, and it is to become a civil right (DH 2; [religious liberty](#religious-liberty)). The right lies in nature, not in dispositions, and persists in those who do not seek the truth.
5. **P4.** Government exceeds its competence if it commands or forbids religious acts (DH 3); it may favour religious life and may give one community special recognition, if all keep effective freedom (DH 3, 6).
6. **C2.** The limit is [just public order](#just-public-order) (DH 7); the object is a civil immunity, which leaves untouched the traditional doctrine on the moral duty of persons and societies toward the true religion (DH 1; [duty of societies](#duty-of-societies-toward-the-true-religion)); and there is no right to error (CCC 2108).

*Where it stops:* the two readings of what DH declares, Murray's and Pink's, are named one line each under Thinkers and texts; stating them at full strength, their crux and the verdict are Boss 5(c)'s. *Immortale Dei* 36's close reading is Boss 5(a)'s.

*Levels:* DH's core **rung 3** (that it is definitive is not manifest); no coercion into faith **at least rung 3**; DH 1's duty of societies **at least rung 3**; *Quanta Cura* a disputed candidate *ex cathedra* act, **not manifestly defined**.

*Lessons:* [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md)

### Argument: The ad bellum judgment and its assignment (CCC 2308-2314)

The conditions feed one judgment, assigned to rulers; the absolute limits feed none.

1. **P1.** While the danger of war lasts and no competent international authority exists, governments cannot be denied [legitimate defence](#legitimate-defence) once peaceful means are exhausted; for those responsible for others it is a grave duty (GS 79 §4; CCC 2265, 2308).
2. **P2.** Defence by military force must meet, at once: damage by the aggressor lasting, grave and certain; every other means shown impractical or ineffective; serious prospects of success; no evils graver than the evil removed, where modern weapons weigh very heavily (CCC 2309; [just-war criteria](#just-war-criteria)).
3. **P3.** Evaluating these conditions belongs to the prudential judgment of those responsible for the common good (CCC 2309), who must provide fairly for [conscientious objection](#conscientious-objection) (GS 79 §3).
4. **C1.** The doctrine states the conditions and assigns the verdict; the verdict on a given war has **no rung**, and no one may claim the Church's authority for his own (GS 43).
5. **P4.** In war the natural law still binds: non-combatants, the wounded and prisoners must be respected ([non-combatant immunity](#non-combatant-immunity)); any act of war aimed indiscriminately at the destruction of whole cities or wide areas with their people is a crime against God and man (GS 80; [the condemnation of total war](#the-condemnation-of-total-war)).
6. **C2.** The absolute limits are not weighed against the cause; foreseen harm is weighed by double effect ([`moral-theology` 4.2](../moral-theology/lessons/04-02-double-effect-and-cooperation-as-catholic-method.md)).

*Where it stops:* applying this to a strike on a city-sited facility, and saying at what level FT 258 and MH 192 are taught, are Boss 5(d)'s. PT 127's object is redress (*ad violata iura sarcienda*), not defence.

*Levels:* defence as right and duty **at least rung 3**; CCC 2309's conditions and assignment **rung 3**; GS 80 **at least rung 3, heaviest conciliar grade**; directly killing the innocent **rung 1**; Aquinas q.40 a.1 **rung 4**, common teaching.

*Lessons:* [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md)

### Argument: The 1982 condition and what came after

Deterrence was tolerated on a condition that made the toleration a wasting one.

1. **P1.** The Council named deterrence without blessing it: whatever the facts about deterrence, the arms race is no safe road to peace (GS 81); war on cities is condemned (GS 80).
2. **P2.** In current conditions, deterrence based on balance, not as an end in itself but as a step toward progressive disarmament, may still be judged morally acceptable (John Paul II, 1982, §8; [the 1982 condition](#the-1982-condition)).
3. **P3.** No one may intend to kill the innocent as part of a deterrence strategy, nor use the weapons to destroy population centres (CoP §§147, 178, restating GS 80).
4. **C1.** The US bishops' [strictly conditioned acceptance](#strictly-conditioned-acceptance) (1983, §186), their own prudential judgment: deterrence only against others' use, sufficiency not superiority, each new weapon judged by whether it brings disarmament nearer (§188); in 1993, justified only temporarily.
5. **P4.** Francis (addresses of 2017 and 2019) condemned the very [possession](#possession-of-nuclear-weapons) of the weapons; FT 262 calls deterrence through fear no solution and elimination a moral and humanitarian imperative, without calling possession immoral; MH 194 calls the belief that deterrence is indispensable to security erroneous, without speaking of possession.
6. **C2.** The floor holds: never intend or do the destruction of cities; deterrence is never an end and is ordered to disarmament; elimination is a moral goal; [legitimate defence](#legitimate-defence) stands.

*Where it stops:* whether the possession judgment is a principle or a reading of conditions is **rung 5**. MH 192's two readings are 5.4's; the level at which it and FT 258 teach is Boss 5(d)'s.

*Levels:* the floor **rung 1** (no direct killing of the innocent) and **at least rung 3** (GS 80; defence); 1982 and the peace messages **rung 3 at its lightest**, the 2017 and 2019 addresses the same at most; CoP and *Harvest* **particular teaching**; FT 262 and MH 194 **rung 3** as acts.

*Lessons:* [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md)

### Argument: The paired duties of migration

Two rights, each answered by duties; every number is handed to prudence.

1. **P1.** The earth is made for the use of all (*Exsul Familia*, quoting Pius XII's 1941 message; the [universal destination of goods](#universal-destination-of-goods)).
2. **P2.** When there are just reasons a person must be permitted to emigrate, and he has a right to enter where he hopes to provide better for his family (PT 25, 106; [right to migrate](#right-to-migrate)).
3. **P3.** Better-off nations must, as far as they are able, receive the foreigner who seeks safety and a living, and protect the guest (CCC 2241, restating PT 106's limit of the community's genuine good). "Able" is not "willing".
4. **P4.** For the common good in their charge, political authorities may set legal conditions on immigration; the newcomer owes respect for his new country's heritage, obedience to its laws and a share of civic burdens (CCC 2241; EMCC 29).
5. **P5.** There is also a [right to remain](#right-to-remain), not to have to emigrate (FT 38; MH 81); until conditions at home improve it does not cancel the welcome (FT 129).
6. **C.** The refugee's claim presses hardest (PT 105; FT 130; [refugees](#refugees)); numbers, procedures and programmes are *applications* for political authorities to judge.

*Where it stops:* marking a bill's clauses, and diagnosing a statement that calls a cap figure contrary to Catholic teaching, are Boss 5(b)'s.

*Levels:* under [right to migrate](#right-to-migrate); CCC 2241 footnotes nothing, so find the acts behind it (5.5). The [four verbs](#the-four-verbs) are a pastoral programme, **no rung** beyond the principles beneath them.

*Lessons:* [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md)

**Module 6: Creation, development, and the arguments among Catholics**

### Argument: One crisis, one cry (LS 137-162)

Every branch runs back to one crisis and on to the common good: that is what "integral" means.

1. **P1.** Creation is a gift in which every creature has its own value; dominion is read through "till and keep" as responsible [stewardship](#stewardship) (LS 67, 76, 116).
2. **P2.** Environmental, economic, social and cultural ecology and the ecology of daily life are aspects of one reality (LS 138–155; [integral ecology](#integral-ecology)).
3. **P3.** The human roots of the crisis are a [technocratic paradigm](#technocratic-paradigm) and a [throwaway culture](#throwaway-culture) (LS 101–109, 22).
4. **C1.** There is one complex crisis, social and environmental, whose remedies must fight poverty and protect nature together; hence [the cry of the earth and the cry of the poor](#the-cry-of-the-earth-and-the-cry-of-the-poor) are heard together (LS 139, 49).
5. **C2.** Integral ecology is inseparable from the [common good](#common-good), becomes an option for the poorest, and includes those who come after ([intergenerational justice](#intergenerational-justice); LS 156–159).

*Where it stops:* LS marks its own science as borrowed and its policies as proposals (LS 61, 188); the sort of particular LS claims is Boss 6(a)'s. The principles hang on no temperature.

*Lessons:* [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md)

### Argument: Who owes what in development

Principles fixed, every share and design revisable.

1. **P1.** Development is [integral](#integral-human-development): of each person and the whole person, not growth alone (PP 14; CiV 18; MH 82–83).
2. **P2.** The duty falls first on the wealthier nations and is threefold: aid (solidarity), fairer trade (social justice), a world where no one's progress is bought at another's expense (universal charity) (PP 44; [the duty of rich nations](#the-duty-of-rich-nations)); it binds nations, not only individuals (SRS 32).
3. **P3.** Poor nations are the architects of their own development, though not in isolation (PP 77; SRS 44; [agents of their own development](#agents-of-their-own-development)).
4. **P4.** Aid must build the recipient's own capacity, keeping subsidiarity and solidarity joined (CiV 58; [aid and subsidiarity](#aid-and-subsidiarity)).
5. **P5.** Rerum Novarum's rule holds between nations: the consent of very unequal parties does not by itself make a contract fair (PP 59; [trade between unequal partners](#trade-between-unequal-partners)).
6. **C.** Aid shares (CiV 60), trade terms (CA 33's judgment, offered as how things seem), institutional designs (CiV 67) and metrics beside GDP (MH 159) are *applications*; terms of trade and aid dependence are *empirical premises*.

*Levels:* T1–T6's principles and the need for a world-competent authority **rung 3**; debt and Jubilee are [`theology-of-debt` 6.2](../theology-of-debt/lessons/06-02-international-debt-and-the-jubilee-call.md)'s.

*Lessons:* [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md)

### Argument: Belloc and the servile state

**Outline only (Boss 6(b)).** The parts 6.3 teaches from *The Servile State* (Foulis, 1912): capitalism, ownership by a few joined to freedom for all, is unstable (Sections I, V); a reformer who keeps freedom must restore property to many (the Distributive State) or to none (the Collectivist State) (Section VI); collectivism will yield instead a society where many are constrained by positive law to labour for others (Section I). Beside it the lesson sets RN 46 (the law should induce as many as possible of the humbler class to become owners) and QA 59–65 (the redemption of the proletariat; the partnership contract). The reconstruction in numbered premises, and which premises RN 46 or QA support and which are Belloc's own, are Boss 6(b)'s.

*Levels:* Belloc, Chesterton and the Distributist League **no magisterial level**; RN 46 and QA 59–63 **rung 3**; whether distributism is the natural implementation **rung 5**. See [the servile state](#the-servile-state) and [distributism](#distributism).

*Lessons:* [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md)

### Argument: The floor every reading keeps

A reading may stress; it may not delete.

1. **P1.** Every reading must keep what is taught at rung 3 or higher: religious freedom as a civil immunity within due limits (DH 2); the moral duty of persons and societies toward the true religion, left untouched (DH 1; **at least rung 3**); the choice of regime left to citizens, no form condemned in itself (GS 74; *Immortale Dei* 36); the condemnation of socialism as doctrine (QA 117–120; CA 13) and of economic freedom outside a strong juridical frame (CA 42, read whole); the option for the poor, never exclusive (SRS 42; CA 57); the disclaimers (CA 43; SRS 41; LC 74; GS 76).
2. **P2.** Every reading has **no magisterial level**, and each one's distinctive premise is **rung 5**.
3. **P3.** No system: each reading's programme (democratic capitalism, the confessional polity, a structural agenda, the distributive state) is therefore an *application*, owed serious consideration and open to sincere disagreement (GS 43).
4. **C.** Readings may disagree where the texts leave room and must agree everywhere else; presenting any reading as "simply what the Church teaches" is the inflation error.

*Where it stops:* stating the [Catholic liberal](#catholic-liberal-reading), [integralist](#integralism) and [progressive](#progressive-reading) readings at full strength, and naming the premise on which each pair divides, are Boss 6(c)'s; arguing for one is Boss 6(d)'s.

*Lessons:* [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md)

## Documents of the corpus

Chronological. The corpus proper runs from 1891; its nineteenth-century background comes first, and regional, conference and curial texts are marked as such. This table says what each document added, as the lessons say it. Each document's level, as the lessons mark it, is under [Levels of authority used in this course](#levels-of-authority-used-in-this-course). The Lessons column gives where a document is chiefly read. Post-1930 documents are paraphrased.

| *Quanta Cura* | 8 Dec 1864 | encyclical, Pius IX | condemned the opinion that liberty of conscience and worship is each person's right, to be proclaimed in law and restrained by no authority (§3) | [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) |

| *Libertatis Nuntius* | 6 Aug 1984 | CDF doctrinal instruction, approved in ordinary form | takes the injustice as given and criticizes "certain forms" of liberation theology that borrow Marxist concepts uncritically; names no theologian | [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md) |
| *Libertatis Conscientia* | 22 Mar 1986 | CDF doctrinal instruction, approved in ordinary form | LN's promised companion: love of preference (§68), base communities (§69), reform over the myth of revolution (§78), armed struggle only against obvious, prolonged tyranny (§79) | [2.4](lessons/02-04-gaudium-et-spes-to-justice-in-the-world.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) |
| *Economic Justice for All* | Nov 1986 | pastoral letter, US bishops | a prudential judgment is only as sound as its principles and its information (§§134-135) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md) |
| *Sollicitudo Rei Socialis* | 30 Dec 1987 | encyclical, John Paul II | social doctrine is moral theology, no "third way" (§41); superdevelopment (§28); structures of sin (§36); solidarity defined (§38); the option as a special form of primacy (§42) | [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) and others |
| *Donum Veritatis* | 24 May 1990 | CDF doctrinal instruction, approved in ordinary form | where contingent elements mix with principles, time sorts them and a loyal willingness to submit remains the rule (§24) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md) and others |
| *Centesimus Annus* | 1 May 1991 | encyclical, John Paul II | principles against analysis of events (§3); socialism's anthropological error (§13); the conditional answer on capitalism (§42); no models (§43); democracy on conditions (§46); the Social Assistance State (§48) | [3.5](lessons/03-05-the-universal-destination-of-goods-and-private-property.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) and others |
| *Catechism of the Catholic Church* | 1992; 2nd ed. 1997 | catechism | a locator: each paragraph keeps its source's weight (CCC 2241 footnotes nothing, so its acts must be found) | [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) and others |
| *The Harvest of Justice Is Sown in Peace* | 17 Nov 1993 | statement approved by the US bishops | deterrence justified only temporarily, active commitment to disarmament its only moral basis | [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| *Apostolos Suos* | 21 May 1998 | apostolic letter *motu proprio*, John Paul II | a conference's doctrinal declaration is authentic in its territory only if unanimous, or by two-thirds with *recognitio*; never universal (§22) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| *Doctrinal Note* on Catholics in political life | 24 Nov 2002 | CDF note, approved in ordinary form | pluralism covers no policy undermining fundamental ethical requirements (§§3, 5); autonomy from the Church, not from morality (§6) | [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md) |
| *Compendium of the Social Doctrine of the Church* | Presentation 2 Apr 2004 | compilation, Pontifical Council for Justice and Peace | a map of the corpus with no magisterial level of its own; weight follows independence from contingent elements (§80) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md) and others |
| *Erga Migrantes Caritas Christi* | approved 1 May 2004 | instruction, Pontifical Council for the Pastoral Care of Migrants and Itinerant People, approved by John Paul II | sets the right to emigrate beside each country's right to pursue an immigration policy for the common good, and affirms the right not to emigrate (§29) | [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) |
| Address to the Roman Curia | 22 Dec 2005 | papal address, Benedict XVI | a hermeneutic of reform, against discontinuity and rupture: continuity of principle, discontinuity in contingent applications | [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) |
| *Deus Caritas Est* | 25 Dec 2005 | encyclical, Benedict XVI | the Church may not take up the political battle or replace the State, nor stay on the sidelines (§28); ordering society justly is directly the laity's (§29) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md) |
| Address at Aparecida | 13 May 2007 | papal address, Benedict XVI | the option for the poor is implicit in faith in a God who became poor for us | [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md) |
| Address to the UN General Assembly | 18 Apr 2008 | papal address, Benedict XVI | when a state cannot protect its people, the international community must act through the Charter's means | [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md) |
| *Caritas in Veritate* | 29 June 2009 | encyclical, Benedict XVI | no two social doctrines (§12); gift inside the economy (§§34-39); subsidiarity and solidarity joined (§§57-58); purchasing a moral act (§66); a world political authority under law (§67) | [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [4.4](lessons/04-04-enterprise-consumption-and-the-economy-of-gift.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) and others |
| *Evangelii Gaudium* | 24 Nov 2013 | apostolic exhortation, Francis | teachings on contingent situations open to discussion, yet concrete (§182); an economy of exclusion and an idolatry of money (§§53-55); the option a theological category (§198) | [1.2](lessons/01-02-permanent-principles-and-contingent-applications.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) |
| *Laudato Si'* | 24 May 2015 | encyclical, Francis | integral ecology: one complex crisis, the cry of the earth and the cry of the poor; the technocratic paradigm; does not presume to settle science or replace politics (§188) | [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md) and others |
| "An Appeal to the Catholic Church" (Rome conference) | 11-13 Apr 2016 | statement of a conference sponsored by the Pontifical Council for Justice and Peace and Pax Christi International; no magisterial level | asks the Church to stop teaching just-war theory and shift to a "Just Peace" approach | [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| World Day of Peace message 2017 | for 2017 (signed 8 Dec 2016) | papal message, Francis | active nonviolence the "natural and necessary complement" to limiting force by moral norms | [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| Addresses on nuclear weapons (Vatican symposium; Hiroshima) | 10 Nov 2017; 24 Nov 2019 | papal addresses, Francis | the threat of use and the very possession of the weapons to be firmly condemned; possession called immoral | [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| World Day of Migrants and Refugees message 2018 | signed 15 Aug 2017 | papal message, Francis | welcome, protect, promote, integrate | [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) |
| *Fratelli Tutti* | 3 Oct 2020 | encyclical, Francis | social friendship (§99); property a secondary natural right (§120); the right to remain and the four verbs (§§38, 129); the old just-war criteria hard to invoke (§258); elimination of nuclear weapons an imperative (§262) | [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) and others |
| *Laudate Deum* | 4 Oct 2023 | apostolic exhortation, Francis | answers those who deride the climate evidence, holds the human origin of warming beyond doubt (§11), hopes for a binding transition (§59); not an encyclical | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md) |
| *Dignitas Infinita* | dated 2 Apr 2024 (approved 25 Mar) | declaration of the Dicastery for the Doctrine of the Faith, approved in ordinary form | dignity indelible; four senses of dignity (§§7-8); a catalogue of grave violations (§§33-62) | [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| *Antiqua et Nova* | dated 28 Jan 2025 (approved 14 Jan) | note of two dicasteries, approved in ordinary form | AI and human intelligence; autonomous weapons undermine last resort (§99); not papal teaching | [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md) |
| Letter to the bishops of the United States | 10 Feb 2025 | papal letter, Francis | dignity measures laws (§3); legal status is not criminality, and a nation may act against serious criminals and regulate migration (§§4-5); the true *ordo amoris* (§6) | [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) |
| Addresses to the cardinals and to the Centesimus Annus foundation | 10 and 17 May 2025 | papal addresses, Leo XIV | the name chosen with *Rerum Novarum* in mind; "doctrine" means a disciplined body of knowledge, not an opinion, with no monopoly on truth | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md) |
| Decree on the Mass for the care of creation | 8 June 2025; published 3 July | decree, Dicastery for Divine Worship | a Mass for the care of creation enters the Roman Missal: liturgical law, no doctrinal rung | [6.1](lessons/06-01-integral-ecology-laudato-si.md) |
| *Dilexi Te* | signed 4 Oct 2025, published 9 Oct | apostolic exhortation, Leo XIV (begun by Francis) | reads Medellín, Puebla's "social sin" and Aparecida into a papal text (§§87-101); quotes the 1984 instruction approvingly (§98) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [3.6](lessons/03-06-the-preferential-option-for-the-poor.md) |
| Address to the World Meeting of Popular Movements | 23 Oct 2025 | papal address, Leo XIV | states have the right and duty to protect borders, balanced by the moral obligation to give refuge | [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md), [6.4](lessons/06-04-three-readings-catholic-liberal-integralist-progressive.md) |
| World Day of Peace message 2026 | signed 8 Dec 2025 | papal message, Leo XIV | deterrence rests on fear and force, not law and trust; legitimate defence still named as a principle | [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md) |
| *Magnifica Humanitas* | signed 15 May 2026, published 25 May | encyclical, Leo XIV | safeguarding the person in the time of AI; five principles (§46); property's social function a doctrine of the Church (§66); someone must answer (§105); the just-war sentence (§192) and deterrence's necessity called erroneous (§194) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [1.3](lessons/01-03-see-judge-act.md), [3.1](lessons/03-01-human-dignity.md), [3.2](lessons/03-02-the-common-good.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [4.5](lessons/04-05-the-new-things-of-our-time-magnifica-humanitas.md), [5.3](lessons/05-03-war-and-peace.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) and others |

## Thinkers and texts

Everyone the lessons name, grouped by role. Theses are given as the lessons give them. A theologian's or lay author's work has **no magisterial level**, whichever reading it serves. A pope's documents carry the levels under [Levels of authority used in this course](#levels-of-authority-used-in-this-course); his private writing as a philosopher does not. Modern authors are paraphrased.

| Who | When | Key work | Thesis, as the lessons give it | Lessons |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Popes of the corpus** | | | | |
| Leo XIII (Gioacchino Pecci) | pope 1878-1903 | *Quod Apostolici Muneris* (1878); *Diuturnum* (1881); *Immortale Dei* (1885); *Libertas* (1888); *Rerum Novarum* (1891) | answers the socialist and the liberal remedy at once: property by nature, the wage owed in justice, the State protecting the weak within limits; authority from God, no form prescribed | [1.4](lessons/01-04-before-1891-the-roots-and-the-social-question.md), [2.1](lessons/02-01-rerum-novarum.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md), [5.2](lessons/05-02-religious-liberty-dignitatis-humanae.md), [6.3](lessons/06-03-distributism-and-the-alternative-proposals.md) and others |
| Pius XI (Achille Ratti) | pope 1922-1939 | *Quadragesimo Anno* (1931); *Divini Redemptoris* (1937) | subsidiarity and social justice as working terms; a vocational order proposed; socialism and communism judged in their picture of man and society | [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [3.3](lessons/03-03-subsidiarity.md), [4.2](lessons/04-02-the-just-wage.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md) |

MH 29 credits him with the title "Social Doctrine of the Church" (1950) | [1.1](lessons/01-01-the-social-doctrine-sources-genre-and-claim.md), [2.2](lessons/02-02-quadragesimo-anno-and-the-interwar-proposals.md), [2.3](lessons/02-03-mater-et-magistra-and-pacem-in-terris.md), [5.1](lessons/05-01-the-political-community-and-the-limits-of-the-state.md), [5.5](lessons/05-05-migration.md) |

Wojtyła's solidarity as an attitude of participation is philosophy, taught in `phenomenology-and-personalism` 5.5 | [3.4](lessons/03-04-solidarity.md), [4.1](lessons/04-01-work-and-the-worker-laborem-exercens.md), [4.3](lessons/04-03-socialism-and-liberal-capitalism-each-judged.md), [5.4](lessons/05-04-deterrence-and-the-challenge-to-just-war.md), [6.2](lessons/06-02-integral-human-development-and-the-international-order.md) |
